# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

Please choose versions by [Semantic Versioning](http://semver.org/).

* MAJOR version when you make incompatible API changes,
* MINOR version when you add functionality in a backwards-compatible manner, and
* PATCH version when you make backwards-compatible bug fixes.

## Unreleased

- refactor: **the manager sweep no longer depends on a particular person's Obsidian vault.** Four agents and eleven command files declared the vault runbook `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` -- a personal-vault path -- as their canonical procedure and as the winner whenever the two disagreed. On any install without that vault the pointer dead-ended, and the agent that names the runbook as the winner had no winner to read; the repo had already made this exact move once, for the agent's *location* (v0.21.0), and `docs/fleet-surface.md` already states the rule for the spawn shape and the fleet table. The sweep's output frame -- the marker line, the column/width table, the icon legend, the Session and `Met` cells, the optional-scope split, and the full `box-table.py` stdin contract -- now lives in **`docs/sweep-output.md`**, which is canonical. ⚠️ **Winner clauses were rewritten to name the plugin copy, never re-pathed**, because a re-pathed vault path is still a vault path; the remaining citations use the wikilink-by-title form `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Referencing vault notes mandates, and folder literals carry the *(operator's vault; not shipped with this plugin)* marker. Personal-vault nouns and file counts were genericised. ⚠️ **One file outside the named set was repointed too** -- `commands/steps/manager-sweep.md` carried the same frame reference in prose, so it would have passed a grep for the path while still pointing at the vault. ⚠️ **Four baselined bodies grow, and their entries in `scripts/budget-baseline.json` are raised to the sizes this PR leaves** (`agents/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`): the winner-scoping, canonical-pointer and marker text is added content rather than a restatement of existing text, which is the case that ratchet's own `_note` records entries post-change for. The largest raise is ~0.7 KB, and all four remain over the 30 KB budget either way.


- fix: **the verdicts cache's key derivation now has one home in code, because as prose it was re-implemented per leg and the legs disagreed.** A drive leg's own `Audit` block reported it against itself on **2026-10-12T15:49:30Z**: *"CACHE-KEY DRIFT (report, do not fix): the 8 stored entries reproduce under a `mode:`-only exclusion, NOT the clause's four-field derivation."* Writer and reader therefore keyed the same row differently, so the row read as cold on every tick and the audits kept dispatching — the cost this cache exists to remove. ⚠️ **`scripts/check-content-key-formula.py` predicted exactly this in its own docstring** (*"it cannot prove the leg computes the key this way"*): a prose-shape check cannot hold a computation, and all six of its assertions were green throughout. The derivation now lives as `content_key` in `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, reachable from the leg through a new `--content-keys` mode (`<key>\t<path>` per input path, in order, with an unreadable path reported and exit 2 rather than silently skipped); `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) now names the call and states the derivation is never the reader's to write, keeping its property list as the check a correct call is read against rather than a spec to implement. ⚠️ **The guard gained the half it said it could not reach** — it now imports the script and asserts `CONTENT_KEY_EXCLUDED` / `CONTENT_KEY_HEX` against the clause's own list, so a drift between the two halves of the contract fails there rather than in production. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `scripts/check-content-key-formula.py`, `scripts/tests/test_content_key.py` (new), `scripts/tests/test_check_content_key_formula.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **a one-shot A2A control reply is now unwrapped from its `SendMessageResponse`, so `create`, `list` and `stop` work against a real controller.** The JSON-RPC `result` is a oneof — `{ "task": {…} }` or `{ "message": {…} }` — and the client read it as the task itself. Measured 2026-10-13 against `a2a-worker-controller` v0.4.4 (a2a-go v2.6.0) in the first live `spawn_agent(target: 'docker')`: the controller started the worker container, the supervisor found no status and no artifact, returned an error, and orphaned the worker. The same path backs the v0.139.0 liveness mirror's `list`, which therefore never stamped anything. A bare task is still accepted. The streaming path already unwrapped `{ task }` and is unchanged.

- test: a wrapped `{ result: { task } }` reply must parse to the same `ControlResponse` as the streaming framing.

- fix: **the manager's attention arm remembers what it has already surfaced, so a re-arm no longer re-emits every still-open gate.** `poll()` opened with `previous = None`, so the first poll of *every* arm treated every held gate as newly entered -- a change-detector within an arm and none across one. A `Monitor` is bounded at 30 min and re-armed, so a gate that stayed open cost one full manager turn per arm for as long as it stayed open: measured 2026-10-12 on the Fleet Manager, panes 1065, 1151, 1175, 1094, 1193, 1191, 1196 re-reported ~8 times, each a turn over a ~600k-token context, for no state change. New `--feed-state-dir` (default `~/.claude/state/gate-feed/`) holds `<self-session-id>.json` mapping each kept pane to a fingerprint of its gate; `--reemit-after SECONDS` (default 3600) bounds the backstop below. ⚠️ **The fingerprint is `sha256(pane + normalise(gate text))`, seeded on the state that actually changes.** A clock- or counter-derived fingerprint would re-emit everything on every poll, or on every restart -- the defect this removes, reintroduced one layer down. The gate's text is read from the newest event-log record for the pane (`detail`, falling back to `questions`/`kind`), NOT from the feed line: the feed renders an AGE (`0m`, `5h49m`) beside every row, so a line-derived fingerprint would differ on every poll. `normalise()` is the same whitespace collapse `notify-gate.py` applies to its own key, so a re-rendered gate keeps one identity. ⚠️ **A held gate is re-emitted once its entry expires, and that TTL is the whole reason the change is safe.** Persisting the emit is exactly the shape that loses a message: the arm prints a pane, the `Monitor`'s notification is lost, and a store recording it as delivered never retries -- dedup plus event-only delivery, a permanent silent stall. The re-arm WAS that backstop (`65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md`, both copies: *"the re-arm is the documented backstop for a wake that was lost"*), and `manager-attention-watch.py`'s `AGED` rung is not a full replacement: it fires on the TRACKED set, while this filter's kept set is wider (unowned panes, a dead spawner's panes, peer non-managers), so a gate in that residual class would have no re-drive at all. The TTL restores the backstop at a bounded cost -- one turn per gate per hour instead of one per <=30-minute arm -- and `--reemit-after 0` disables it for an operator who finds the hourly re-emit noisier than the lost wakes it insures against. ⚠️ **An absent or corrupt store reads as FIRST RUN, never as "nothing changed".** The two are indistinguishable from the file alone, and choosing the quiet one would mean a store that was never written, or was lost, silently suppressing every gate it should have carried with no way back; the fail-open direction here is a wake rather than a silence. That is deliberately the OPPOSITE of `drive-ledger.py`, which REFUSES a corrupt ledger -- there an empty read re-emits everything, so refusing is the safe side, and here it is the same read with the same effect. ⚠️ **Entries for panes not in the kept set are dropped, a deliberate divergence from "prune by AGE and LIVENESS, never by sweep-absence".** That rule protects a SHARED ledger, where "absent from my sweep" is a claim about one reader's partial view and pruning would let one sweep delete another's marks; this store is per-session and single-writer, and the failure direction of a wrong prune is a re-emit, a wake, never a silence. The write is atomic (`makedirs` -> **pid-suffixed** tmp -> `os.replace`), and the pid suffix is load-bearing rather than tidy: every manager shares one state dir, so a bare `<path>.tmp` would let one arm's half-written file be renamed over another's good one. ⚠️ **`--interval` now requires `--self`, and that is what keeps the fix from being a silent no-op** -- the store is keyed on it, so an arm without one has nowhere to persist and would re-emit the whole kept set on every re-arm while looking exactly like a fixed one. The documented arm passes it. An unwritable store fails open to the PRE-fix behaviour (every kept pane re-emitted) and says so **once** on stderr (`STATE  unwritable: ...`), because the silent version of that is the worst shape available: the arm looks healthy and the `LIVENESS` line keeps advancing while the change does nothing at all. `CLAUDE.md` § Layout gains the script's own entry naming the new state path. Tests: **2150 -> 2165**, the 15 new cases driving SEPARATE arms -- a new process and a new producer counter per call, so the state file is the only thing that crosses between them, since a test reusing one process would re-prove the in-process detector that already worked. ⚠️ **Four pre-existing poll tests failed on the first cut, and the cause is a trap worth naming:** they shared the REAL `~/.claude/state/gate-feed/<ME>.json`, because the harness passed no `--feed-state-dir`, so whichever test ran first left entries that suppressed the next one's news. The harness now points every invocation at its own tmpdir.

- fix: **`CHANGELOG.md` is rotated into a rolling archive, because it had crossed the release watcher's 1 MiB read cap and the watcher had therefore dropped the whole repo from every poll cycle.** Measured 2026-10-13: **1 056 367 bytes** against a 1 048 576-byte cap, and `watcher.go:290] repo dropped from cycle: owner=bborbe repo=claude-supervisor err=CHANGELOG.md bborbe/claude-supervisor too large: 1056367 bytes (max 1 MiB)` on every cycle since `4d863ffc` (21:20:53Z) — the refusal is emitted by the *watcher* (`github-release-watcher` `pkg/githubclient.go:330`, on the API-reported `Size` before decoding), so no release task was ever emitted, `## Unreleased` accumulated with no tag cut, and the releaser pod never ran. ⚠️ **The visible symptom is a missing tag, and a missing tag is indistinguishable from a slow one** — that is why it survived a day of merges. ⚠️ **A one-time trim is not a fix here:** the file gains ~35–50 KB/day (306 653 B at `v0.61.0` on 2026-09-27 → 533 860 at `v0.96.2` → 916 432 at `v0.122.8` on 2026-10-09) and is never pruned, so cutting it once re-breaks in about three weeks. `scripts/changelog-archive.py` therefore keeps `## Unreleased` plus the newest released sections up to a **384 KiB** target — never fewer than 20 — and moves the rest into a new `CHANGELOG-archive.md`; this run takes `CHANGELOG.md` to **399 115 bytes** (38 % of the cap) and archives 316 sections. ⚠️ **Sections are sliced, never re-serialised:** a re-joining implementation silently rewrote the blank lines around 70 released headings while every count still matched, and the changelog fold guard byte-compares each released section against its own tag, so the retained prefix and the archived suffix are copied verbatim — `scripts/tests/test_changelog_archive.py` asserts both byte-for-byte and asserts the two section counts sum to the input's. ⚠️ **`make check-changelog-size` is the half that makes it durable:** it fails above 768 KiB (75 % of the cap) naming `make changelog-archive`, and runs in `make precommit`, so CI goes red while there is still runway rather than the watcher silently dropping the repo; `make changelog-archive` is idempotent. ⚠️ **`CHANGELOG-archive.md` is declared in a new `.reviewignore`**, because ~1900 added lines of frozen moved prose exceeds the PR reviewer's 1200-line size gate on its own and parks every archive PR at `human_review` before a pod spawns. The exclusion removes it from the reviewer's context as well as from the gate; the file is machine-produced and never hand-edited, and the move is pinned by the byte-for-byte tests and the section-count probe rather than by reading it. `.reviewignore` cannot exclude itself, so this policy line is itself reviewed. New: `scripts/changelog-archive.py`, `scripts/tests/test_changelog_archive.py`, `CHANGELOG-archive.md`, `.reviewignore`. Changed: `CHANGELOG.md`, `Makefile`.

## v0.139.0

- feat: **an A2A worker is now stamped live while its controller still lists it.** Since v0.137.0 nothing stamped a `docker` / `k8s` worker, so every sweep read a running one as not-live and rehydrate pruned it. A new 30 s mirror beside the cluster poll lists the controller's workers and stamps each open `a2a` ledger record whose `worker_id` the controller still reports in a non-stopped state; it is silent when no controller is configured. The match is by `worker_id`, never by task. The ledger record gains `worker_id` and `target` (never the worker token), so a reconnected server can map a controller worker back to its session, and rehydrate re-adopts an A2A row under its target's name. The `list` call is bounded at 10 s, because the poll skips a tick while one is in flight and an unanswered list would otherwise wedge the mirror for the process's life. ⚠️ An unreachable controller makes these stamps read dead rather than UNKNOWN — acceptable only because A2A refuses `resume`.

- test: new cases for `a2aLiveSessions` (stopped-state skip, worker-id match, open-record filter), `pollA2A` with injected list/stamp, a never-answering list being aborted at its bound, the ledger's `worker_id` / `target` fields, and rehydrate's `a2a` status mapping. `make precommit` **PASS** — 668/668 node tests, 2161 python tests `OK`.

## v0.138.2

- fix: **the worker counter no longer counts a manager, the gate-owner filter no longer crashes on a corrupt record, and the `shipping` comment now matches the code — three pre-existing defects from the PR #178 review, plus the two further readers the same crash class had.** ⚠️ **The count was right about a hand-started manager only by accident, and wrong about a spawned one.** A hand-started manager writes no ledger record, so it fell out of the count incidentally; `spawn_agent(role="manager")` writes a record exactly like a worker's, so `server/worker-sessions.mjs` and `scripts/worker-sessions.py` counted it against the fleet-wide `spawn.maxConcurrent` cap and pushed the fleet toward refusing ordinary spawns. Both now skip a record whose `role` is exactly `manager`, and ⚠️ **ONLY an explicit `manager` excludes** — never `role !== 'agent'`: a record with no `role` was written before the field existed, and those are pre-field workers and managers alike, so defaulting the other way would drop every pre-field worker out of the population the cap exists to bound. The four sites asserting a ledger record implies a counted worker (`server/worker-sessions.mjs`, `scripts/worker-sessions.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 5, `README.md`) are corrected — README's *"the managers are excluded"* was true of hand-started managers only. ⚠️ **The crash class was enumerated, not patched at the reported surface.** `gate-owner-filter.py`'s `load_ledger` called `rec.get("session_id")` outside its `try`, so a ledger file holding valid-but-non-object JSON (`null`, a list, a scalar) raised `AttributeError` out of `main()` — against the module's own fail-open contract and its docstring's *"the caller must treat `{}` as unknown and fail open"*. The same `json.load`-then-`.get` shape had **two more instances in the same file** (`live_ids` over the session registry, `log_items` over the event log), and all three now skip a non-dict record. The pre-fix shape was reproduced raising `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'get'` on exactly the reported input, so the new test discriminates rather than merely passing. ⚠️ **The `shipping` claim was corrected to the code rather than the code to the claim.** `server/supervisor.mjs` carried a comment saying the flag was recorded *"so a resume can see the worker was shipping"*, while `agentView` in the same file and `registry-rehydrate.mjs` both already called it unrecoverable — rehydrate hardcodes `shipping: false` precisely because `buildRecord` has no such parameter. The outlier comment now agrees with the other two, and a test pins both halves (no `shipping` key on the record, no comment claiming one); making the flag genuinely recoverable is a separate change. ⚠️ **One item is deliberately NOT fixed, and the measurement is why.** `is_manager` reads a role-less manager record as a worker, so such a manager's workers' gates emit to every peer. The obvious arm — *"a label that names a manager"* — is unsafe at every strength against the live ledger (1633 records): label *contains* `anager` → **159** hits, overwhelmingly worker task titles; label *ends with* ` Manager` → **9**, of which **5 are worker task titles** and only **2 are managers**; and the structural alternative is no better (**19** role-less sessions are named as a `parent_session`, ~17 of them workers, because workers spawn sub-workers). Any of them would flip real workers to manager and **DROP** their gates — the fleet-wide silence `is_manager`'s own docstring refuses, and the very outcome the fix's control existed to prevent. The residue keeps its home in that docstring's stated known gap, whose resolution is a re-spawn; the ledger now carries **4** `role:"manager"` records against 1455 pre-field ones, so it shrinks on its own. Change set: `server/worker-sessions.mjs`, `server/worker-sessions.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/ledger.test.mjs`, `scripts/worker-sessions.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_sessions.py`, `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.138.1

- fix: **`session-liveness.py --check` no longer licenses a decisive `ABSENT` from a readable endpoint alone -- the verdict path now pays the coverage precondition the `--list` path already paid.** The file's own header has always stated the licence and its limits -- *"ABSENT is licensed only while the endpoint's coverage of live sessions is COMPLETE, and that is a precondition this file cannot establish for itself"*, and *"do not trust ABSENT until the coverage has been MEASURED"* -- but `_check()` reached its ABSENT return after confirming only that the list route was readable and that the per-id route 404s. It called neither `coverage()` nor `unstamped()`, which are reached only from the `--coverage` and `--list` paths, so a live session that is not yet stamping the store read as a confident negative. Measured 2026-10-10 at ~17:27: `--check bf00c554-…` returned `ABSENT -- no session id matches bf00c554-…` (exit 1) for a session three independent signals read live -- the registry listed it `busy`, its transcript had been written a minute earlier, and its pane rendered with a live status bar. ⚠️ **ABSENT is the one verdict that authorises a resume, so a false one is not a missed detection but a second writer on one transcript** -- the exact hazard this file's header exists to prevent, and the gate `commands/manager-loop.md`'s orphan rule and the drive leg's auto-resume gate both key on. The licence is now established **per id**, by the new `_absent_licensed()`, on the one path that would otherwise license ABSENT: an id the identity registry holds as non-dead, for which the endpoint holds **no row at all**, yields `UNKNOWN` (exit 2) instead of `ABSENT` (exit 1), because "the endpoint has never heard of it" is a fact about the endpoint's COVERAGE and not about the session. ⚠️ **The predicate is `alive is not False` -- deliberately the same one `coverage()` and `unstamped()` already use**, so the measurement and the verdict cannot disagree: `coverage()` reports `missing >= 1` in exactly the state `--check` now refuses. `session-identity.py`'s `alive` is a three-state and only `False` is death; `None` ("pid occupied, identity unprovable") is "cannot tell", and reading it as death is the direction that resumes onto a live conversation. ⚠️ **Two things the guard must NOT swallow, and both are pinned by a control test.** An id the registry does not hold keeps its `ABSENT` -- a guard that answered `UNKNOWN` for every unmatched id would erase the death verdict the whole instrument exists to give -- and so does an id the endpoint POSITIVELY reports `live: false`, which is a dead session the store can see and is answering about rather than a rollout gap. An **unreadable** registry is `UNKNOWN`, never `ABSENT`: `read_registry` returns `None` for a missing directory, distinct from `{}`, and "I could not measure the precondition" is not "the precondition holds" -- the same rule `coverage()` carries at exit 2. A **prefix** is resolved against the registry as it already is against the endpoint, because `read_registry` is keyed by the full id while a prefix is what the fleet passes. ⚠️ **The registry is read only on the licence path** -- the LIVE path still touches it never, so a caller asking about a live session pays no identity probe, which is the cost property the module docstring states. The message for a genuine ABSENT is unchanged, byte for byte, so no caller's parsing moves. `--check` also gains the `--dir` threading the other three subcommands already had. ⚠️ **Two baselined bodies grow, and their entries in `scripts/budget-baseline.json` are raised to the sizes this PR leaves** (`agents/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`): the corroborating-reads clause the four manager files are required to carry is added text rather than a restatement of existing text, which is the case that ratchet's own `_note` records entries post-change for. Both remain far over the 30 KB budget either way; the raise is ~1 KB across the two, not a new overage.

- test: **the incomplete-coverage case fails on the pre-fix code and passes after, with both directions of the licence controlled.** Six cases in `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`: the 2026-10-10 shape (registry non-dead, no endpoint row) must be `UNKNOWN`; a **populated** registry with no match for the argument must still be `ABSENT`; an endpoint row reporting `live: false` must still be `ABSENT` even with a registry record present; an unreadable registry must be `UNKNOWN`; a **prefix** of a registry-live id must be `UNKNOWN`; and `--coverage` must report `missing=1` on the same fixture where `--check` withholds ABSENT. ⚠️ **Measured: 4 of the 6 fail on the pre-fix code and the 2 controls pass on both**, which is what makes them a falsifier rather than a restatement -- a fix that answered `UNKNOWN` for every unmatched id would pass the first and fail a control. The `check()` helper now passes `--dir`, so a case cannot read the machine's real registry: without it the fixture would be decided by whatever this host happens to be running, the same isolation rule `setUp` already states for the start cache, the heartbeat store and the spawn ledger.

## v0.138.0

- feat: **an A2A worker's first turn is now sent, and its result collected afterwards.** The `docker` and `k8s` spawns created a worker and never prompted it; they now send the first turn over the streaming A2A route and file the outcome against the agent record when it arrives. ⚠️ **The send and the collection are two calls, not one, and the split IS SC5.** SC5 measures the send's latency against the turn's own duration — *"the send returns in **< 2 s** while the turn's duration is **> 60 s**"* — and names its falsifier outright: *"a send whose latency tracks the turn's duration (blocking)"*. A single call that returned the reply would BE that falsifier by construction, because its latency is the turn's duration and no amount of care in the caller could change it. `openWorkerTurn` issues the request and waits at most **1500 ms** for acknowledgement **without ever aborting** — an abort would kill the turn it had just started — and hands back either the response or the still-pending request, so a worker that has not answered yet degrades the tool result to *"sent, not yet acknowledged"* instead of blocking the spawn. `collectWorkerTurn` consumes the SSE body and derives the state, `contextId` and reply. A **failed send is filed against the record rather than returned as an error**, because the worker exists by then and something has to reap it — the same distinction the card failure draws, arriving in the opposite order.

- test: **14 new cases**, and the SC5 shape is asserted directly rather than described. One case holds the worker's response open and requires the send to return anyway, then releases it and requires the **same pending request** to produce the turn's outcome — which is what proves it was never aborted. Others pin the three-way send outcome (acknowledged / not yet acknowledged / never left), the last-`statusUpdate` rule, a `contextId` mismatch, an error frame inside HTTP 200, and a FAILED turn being filed as an error **while keeping its outcome**. The patch's key set is asserted exactly, so a spread of the agent record — the way a `workerToken` would reach the ledger — fails loudly. `make precommit` **PASS** — 654/654 node tests (up from 640), 2161 python tests `OK`. ⚠️ **Nothing here has yet run against a real controller** — the path is written and unit-tested, not proven end to end.

## v0.137.0

- feat: **the `docker` and `k8s` spawn targets now create a worker instead of refusing.** New `server/a2a-spawn.mjs` holds the path's rules — every argument that names a LOCAL process, window or policy is refused rather than dropped (the controller creates this worker and this server never runs it), the controller is located from `SUPERVISOR_A2A_URL` / `SUPERVISOR_A2A_TOKEN` or an `a2a` config section with **both** sources validated rather than only the winner, and the `create` control call is a ONE-SHOT `SendMessage` carrying a data part. `IMPLEMENTED_SPAWN_TARGETS` gains both names in the same change as the dispatch branch that serves them, which is the rule that list exists to enforce.

- feat: **the spawn returns before any turn is sent, and the record says so.** `spawnClusterWorker` holds its request open until the pod answers and writes a finished `result`; SC5 requires this path's send to return in **under 2 s** while the turn runs **over 60 s**, so the A2A spawn stops once the worker exists and is reachable and the record carries `result: null` — a placeholder would read to `agent_status` and `list_agents` as a finished turn that produced nothing. The worker's own Agent Card is read before anything is recorded, which is what proves the URL `create` returned actually answers and is where the turn's endpoint comes from.

- feat: **`a2a` is a fourth ledger `mode`, and the roster status is the target name.** An A2A worker is neither a tab, an in-process query, nor the shared cluster pod, so folding it into `headless` would file it under a mode whose whole meaning is "a local process that parks its gates on this server". ⚠️ `buildRecord` **throws** on a mode outside `MODES`, so a status the derivation did not know would lose the ledger record entirely rather than mis-file it — the mapping now has one home in the spawn module and is unit-tested.

- test: **32 new cases**, and the refusals are asserted as hard as the successes. The two wire calls take an injectable `fetch`, so a `FAILED` create, a reply with no `a2a_url`, a reply with no token, a non-2xx, a transport failure, a card that 404s and a card carrying only a top-level `url` are all exercised against a stub rather than a live controller. `make precommit` **PASS** — 640/640 node tests (up from 608), 2161 python tests `OK`. ⚠️ **Nothing here has run against a real controller, and no turn has been sent to a worker** — the `target: "docker"` / `"k8s"` path is written and unit-tested, not proven end to end.

## v0.136.1

- fix: **the A2A client no longer claims a `FAILED` control call means the operation did not happen — a claim measured false on 2026-10-13, when the controller's own `v0.4.0` answered `FAILED` to every call including `list`.** The controller's failure can land in its **task store**, *after* the executor has already run: that build's store copies a task through gob and died with `gob: type not registered for interface: pkg.ControlResponse`, so a `create` could have started a container and still reported `FAILED`. ⚠️ **The old wording asserted the opposite in a merged, released module** — *"it means the operation did not happen"* — and a caller trusting it would retry a side effect that had already occurred. `a2aControlResponseFor` now reports the outcome as **UNKNOWN** and names the reconciliation (`list`), and the `A2A_STATE_FAILED` constant's comment carries the measurement rather than the inference it replaced.

- fix: **`list` results are normalised, because gob does not transmit zero values and the controller's empty worker list therefore arrives as `null`.** The controller's store collapses an empty slice into `{"workers": null}` — measured 2026-10-13 against its `v0.4.1` and confirmed a property of gob rather than a defect in that build — so **the EMPTY case is the one that is not an array**, and it is the most common case at the start of a run. A caller iterating `workers` without a guard throws on exactly the state it meets first. New `a2aWorkerList(response)` normalises `null` and a missing key to `[]`, passes a populated array through unchanged, and **refuses** a non-array or a response whose `operation` is not `list` — so a `create` reply read as a list cannot be mistaken for "no workers".

- test: **4 new cases, each pinning a trap that was measured rather than reasoned about.** The `FAILED` case asserts the outcome reads as UNKNOWN and that the retracted phrase is *absent* (`doesNotMatch`), so the old claim cannot creep back. The list cases cover `null`, `[]`, a missing key, a populated array, and three refusals. `make precommit` **PASS** — 608/608 node tests (up from 604), 2161 python tests `OK`.

## v0.136.0

- feat: **the A2A client gains its CONTROLLER-facing half (`server/a2a-client.mjs`) — the `create` / `list` / `stop` control call the `docker` and `k8s` spawn targets make before any worker exists.** The controller (`bborbe/a2a-worker-controller`) is a different service from a worker, and it differs on exactly two wire points, both read at source (`pkg/types.go`, `pkg/a2a.go`, `pkg/a2a-executor.go`, and the `a2a-go` v2 SDK's `a2a/core.go`) rather than inferred from the worker's shape — because the worker's shape is exactly what would have produced a request that looks right and is not. ⚠️ **The control call carries a DATA part, not a text part.** The controller reads its `ControlRequest` from the message's FIRST data part and refuses a message carrying none, so a text part is not a near miss: it answers `ErrInvalidParams` and no operation runs at all. ⚠️ **The control call is one-shot — the bare method `SendMessage`, never `SendStreamingMessage`.** A control call is a single request and response with no live turn to interrupt, which the controller's own `Cancel` states outright, so a client that opened a stream would wait on frames that are never sent. The call sends no `accept: text/event-stream`, and `contextId` is carried for the same reason the worker half carries it.

- feat: **the flattened-part rule is now pinned at its source, and the SDK enforces it harder than the client's own comment claimed.** `Part.UnmarshalJSON` requires **exactly one** of `text` / `raw` / `data` / `url` to be set: zero yields `unknown part content type` — the literal text of the `-32700` the worker half was built around, so that defect's cause is now pinned rather than suspected — and more than one yields `expected exactly one of text, raw, data, or url`. ⚠️ **A `null` data payload is refused here rather than sent**, because the SDK hydrates `data` into a `*any`: a JSON `null` leaves that pointer nil and counts as no discriminator at all, so it fails on the wire exactly as a missing part does — quietly enough to be worth refusing before it leaves.

- feat: **the control request refuses a malformed operation instead of guessing at it, and the reply is read status-first.** `a2aControlRequestFor` builds exactly one operation-specific field, which is the controller's own rule: it refuses a request that sets none or more than one rather than picking one, so a builder that silently dropped a stray field would ask for a *different* operation than the caller named. ⚠️ On the reply, the terminal status is read **before** the payload, and that order is load-bearing: a backend failure yields `TASK_STATE_FAILED` carrying **no** artifact, and the controller deliberately withholds the backend's error text, so a client that hunted for the data part first would find none and have to invent a reason while the status states the real one. The returned operation is **asserted equal** to the one sent — the same defence as the worker half's `contextId` assertion, and for the same reason: a reply naming a different operation is the only client-side observable that the answer does not belong to the call that asked for it. Both reply framings are accepted, since one SDK handler serves both and the executor is identical behind either.

- test: **12 new cases, and the refusals are asserted as hard as the successes.** A data part must be `{ data }` and must never carry `content` or `text`; a `null`, array, string or number payload must refuse; the call must use the bare `SendMessage`, must not equal the streaming spelling, and must send no SSE `accept`; a `FAILED` status must surface as the error rather than as a missing payload; a completed call with no data part must refuse and name the text-part trap; an operation mismatch and a JSON-RPC error must each refuse. `make precommit` **PASS** — 604/604 node tests (up from 592).

## v0.135.0

- feat: **the supervisor gains its first A2A client, as a pure module (`server/a2a-client.mjs`).** The worker-facing half of the `docker` and `k8s` spawn targets: read the A2A endpoint off a worker's Agent Card, shape one streaming turn, and read the frames back. ⚠️ **Every rule in it was measured on the real wire, and each one produced a request that looked correct and was not** — which is why the module is written as refusals rather than defaults. The card's endpoint is `supportedInterfaces[0].url` and **not** a top-level `url` (the v2 SDK's `AgentCard` carries no such field, so the obvious read yields `undefined`); a message part is **flattened** (`{ text }`, never `{ content: … }`) — a `content` part was refused with `-32700 parse error: unknown part content type` *inside* a well-formed `HTTP 200 text/event-stream` envelope, so every transport check went green while nothing executed, and an error frame is therefore **fatal** here rather than data; `role` is the enum string `ROLE_USER`, not `"user"`; and the method name is **bare** (`SendStreamingMessage`), since the spec's `message/stream` spelling survives only in a compatibility bridge a v2 service does not mount. `contextId` is required rather than optional: a turn sent without one runs under the server's default session and the follow-up opens a *different* conversation, and because the server returns an id either way there is nothing on the client side to compare against — so the id is sent explicitly and the returned one is **asserted equal**, which is the only client-side observable of a dropped `contextId`. The mechanism is **streaming**, chosen because it is the only one of the two candidate contracts that exists: the streaming contract is completed and shipped, while the polling contract is still `next`.

- test: **the client's refusals are pinned as hard as its successes, and one of them caught a real defect before it shipped.** Each measured trap has a case that fails against the broken behaviour: a card shaped the obvious way (top-level `url`, no `supportedInterfaces`) must refuse rather than fall back; a `content` part and a slash-form method must not appear; a first turn with no way to mint a `contextId` must refuse rather than send contextless; an error frame must be fatal; and a `contextId` mismatch must surface at the call. ⚠️ The outcome case is the one that matters — it initially **failed**, because `a2aOutcome` read the task frame's own status and reported `TASK_STATE_SUBMITTED` on a turn that had completed. The frame order is `task(SUBMITTED)` → `artifactUpdate` → `statusUpdate(COMPLETED)`, so the task frame is the *first* one and carries a status too; the **last** `statusUpdate` is what wins.

## v0.134.0

- feat: **`spawn_agent` now accepts `target: "docker"` and `target: "k8s"` — and REFUSES both rather than opening a local worker.** The two A2A worker-controller targets (`docker` = one container per task on this Mac, `k8s` = one pod per task in nuke dev, each then reached DIRECTLY over A2A) are listed in `SPAWN_TARGETS` and published by the tool schema's `enum`, but their client has not shipped, so the spawn refuses. ⚠️ **The refusal is the point of the change, not a placeholder for it.** The dispatch resolves the target, handles `cluster`, and then falls through to the LOCAL path for everything else — so a target added to the enum with no branch of its own would open an ordinary local worker under a call that asked for a container or a pod, and report success. That is the failure `resolveSpawnTarget` already refuses an UNKNOWN target to avoid, arriving through a KNOWN one, and it is the worst shape available here because a `docker` call answered by a local tab is indistinguishable from a working call. `IMPLEMENTED_SPAWN_TARGETS` (`['local', 'cluster']`) is the second list the guard consults. ⚠️ **The two lists are deliberately not the same, and collapsing them reintroduces a defect either way.** `SPAWN_TARGETS` answers *"is this a target at all"* and is what the schema publishes; `IMPLEMENTED_SPAWN_TARGETS` answers *"does anything implement it"*. Merged into one, a caller asking for `docker` before the client lands is told *"not a spawn target"* — which names the wrong problem, since the target is perfectly well-formed and merely unimplemented. A backend therefore ships as two edits in ONE change: its dispatch branch in `supervisor.mjs` and its entry in the implemented list. Removing an entry is never a way to retire a backend; it would silently reroute that target to `local` instead of refusing it. Neither target is a `SPAWN_MODES` value, for exactly the reason `cluster` is not — a target reachable from `SUPERVISOR_SPAWN_MODE` or `spawn.mode` would let one config edit default the whole fleet somewhere, and the target decision is per-call by design.

- test: **the fall-through guard is pinned, because the file that would otherwise carry it cannot be tested at all.** `supervisor.mjs` connects a stdio server at load, so no test can import it; the rule therefore lives in the pure `spawnTargetImplementationRefusal` and is exercised directly. Every target in `SPAWN_TARGETS` is asserted allowed when implemented and refused when not; every implemented target is asserted to be a known one; and the difference between the two lists is asserted literally as `['docker', 'k8s']`, so a new target is an edit someone makes on purpose rather than a silent widening. ⚠️ **The refusal message is asserted to name the local fall-through it prevents** — a message reading only *"not implemented"* would leave the next reader free to conclude that opening a local worker was the safe answer, which is the hazard inverted. Two further cases pin the per-call-only rule on BOTH sources rather than one: `docker` and `k8s` are each refused as a spawn mode from the environment and from the config file, matching the existing `cluster` case beside them.

## v0.133.7

- fix: **`session-liveness.py --check` no longer answers a malformed argument with `ABSENT`.** A value that could not name a session — a newline-separated id blob, a string with no session-id shape, a whitespace-only value — returned `ABSENT` at exit 1, the verdict every manager surface maps to *"this session is dead"* and the one verdict that authorises a resume. Measured 2026-10-12 in manager session `1e03b4d0` over a 175-row tracked set: a caller's `for id in $ids` loop over a newline-separated id string is **not** word-split under zsh, so every multi-id row was probed with the whole blob as a single argument, the probe answered `ABSENT`, and a row was recorded dead whose session was live — re-probed seconds later as `LIVE (heartbeat 5 s)`. The blast radius is a spawn, not a report: a manager acting on it drafts a resume onto a session that is already running, which is the duplicate-writer hazard the fleet's own gates exist to prevent. A value that is not a single well-formed session id now exits **4** with a `MALFORMED —` message on stderr and never prints `ABSENT`. 4 rather than 2 because argparse already exits 2 for its own usage errors (`--check` with no value at all) and `_check` returns UNKNOWN (2) for a zero-length argument — three facts, three codes. ⚠️ **The shape rule is loose about length and strict about hyphens, and both halves are load-bearing.** `_check` matches by `startswith`, so *any* prefix of a real id is a legitimate argument — and on a collision it answers `AMBIGUOUS — … pass a longer id: <m[:12]>`, handing the caller a **12-char prefix** as the recovery input, so a rule demanding exactly 8 characters would refuse the instrument's own instruction and narrow `prefix matching` the verdict machinery leaves alone. Hyphens are admitted only at the UUID separator offsets (8/13/18/23), which is what separates a prefix from prose: `fa2dc5f3-8dd` passes, `not-an-id-at-all` does not. Case is folded because `_check` folds it, so an id that resolves today keeps resolving.

- fix: **`--check ""` no longer falls through to the listing.** `main()`'s guard was `if args.check:` — a truthiness test — so a zero-length argument skipped the `--check` branch entirely and ran the `--list` path, printing the full live roster and exiting **0**. An empty argument silently changed the command's *mode*, and a caller reading that output as a verdict saw a non-empty result. The guard is now `args.check is not None`: empty reaches the verdict path, where `_check` already answers `UNKNOWN — no session id given` at exit 2, and no roster is printed. ⚠️ **The whitespace-only case is deliberately not folded into it.** `_check` strips before its own emptiness test, so the shape guard runs on the *raw* argument and a whitespace-only value stays malformed — a guard that stripped first would answer UNKNOWN for both and silently merge two different facts.

- test: **the malformed class is pinned beside the existing ABSENT cases, including the direction over-tightening would break.** Eleven cases cover the blob, a no-whitespace non-id string, whitespace-only, internal whitespace, a 37-character value, the empty case with its no-roster assertion, the unchanged `ABSENT` control, the 12-char recovery prefix, a longer prefix, an uppercase id, and a `--list` that still works when it is asked for. ⚠️ **The two acceptance cases are the ones that carry the risk**: a 12-char prefix of a hyphenated id and an uppercase id must both reach the verdict path rather than exit 4, because a guard that narrowed prefix matching or demanded literal lowercase would pass every rejection case while breaking a live caller. The assertions read stdout and stderr **separately** — the refusal prints to stderr while the `--list` fall-through it replaced printed to stdout, so a merged capture could not tell a roster from a verdict, and a stdout-only `assertNotIn("ABSENT")` would pass on the broken code. Two existing cases were updated rather than weakened: the 404 case now probes a well-formed id (a non-id string can no longer stand in for "an id that matches nothing"), and the hostile-id case now asserts **both** layers — `main()` refuses the value at the argument boundary, and `_check` still percent-encodes it for any caller that reaches it directly, so the encoding guarantee is pinned where it lives instead of being inferred from the guard.
## v0.133.6

- fix: **the cluster spawn's transport bound is raised above the module's own deadline, so a turn longer than five minutes can return its answer.** `startClusterSession` armed a ten-minute `AbortController`, but the request went through Node's global `fetch` -- undici -- whose `headersTimeout` defaults to 300 000 ms. `POST /prompt` sends no response headers until the turn ends, so undici aborted a HEALTHY long turn at 300 s, and the client's disconnect then cancelled the turn in the pod (`prompt intake failed: turn cancelled: context canceled`) -- losing the answer AND the work. Measured 2026-10-12 against a stub holding its headers 310 s: **300 931 ms** on Node 26.11.1 / undici 8.11.2 and **300 966 ms** on Node 22.22.3 / undici 6.24.1, with the stub answering ~9 s *after* both clients had given up. `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` never fired. The spawn now sends through an `Agent` whose `headersTimeout` and `bodyTimeout` sit `TRANSPORT_TIMEOUT_SLACK_MS` (5 s) **above** `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS`, so this module's own `AbortController` stays the authority -- it is what produces the readable `no answer within <timeoutMs>ms` -- and undici's bound is a backstop. ⚠️ **`fetch` and `Agent` now come from the same `undici` package, and that pairing is load-bearing rather than tidy.** A dispatcher is honoured only by the fetch sharing its undici copy, and Node exposes no `node:undici` builtin, so a package `Agent` handed to the GLOBAL fetch is not a raised timeout but a broken request -- measured on Node 26 as `InvalidArgumentError: invalid onRequestStart method` (`UND_ERR_INVALID_ARG`). This fleet is split **24/9** between Node 26 (bundled undici 8.11.2) and Node 22 (bundled 6.24.1), selected by the launching session's PATH because the `start` script calls bare `node`, so a global-fetch fix would have mixed undici majors on one group whichever version was pinned. The `UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT` comment is corrected too: it is excluded from retry because it is a **post-delivery** code on a non-idempotent endpoint, **not** because a header-less server is wedged -- the reading that hid this defect for as long as it did. Adds `undici@^8.11.2`.

- test: **the transport bound is pinned against a real socket, because every existing timeout test structurally cannot see it.** All prior timeout tests inject `fetchImpl`, so they exercise the module's own `AbortController`, and a fake fetch has no `headersTimeout` to fire; a test asserting only that the options carry a dispatcher would pin the *shape* of the fix rather than the defect. The new test drives the module's real default fetch at a stub that holds its response headers, injecting only the dispatcher -- whose bound is what the assertion is about -- and it **fails on the pre-fix code by succeeding**: measured, the old module returns `{status:200, outcome:"answered"}` at 2527 ms with the injected 200 ms bound ignored, while the fixed module fails with `UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT` at ~1010 ms. ⚠️ **The stub holds 4000 ms rather than 400, and undici's granularity is why**: undici enforces `headersTimeout` on a coarse timer -- measured, a 200 ms bound let a 600 ms response through and fired at ~1008 ms -- so a hold shorter than that tick would pass on the fixed code too and read as a broken fix. The hold also has to clear that tick by a wide margin, because the tick is the *failing* direction's duration: were it to fire late under load the stub would answer first and the test would report a false failure. On the fixed code the request still fails at the tick, so the wider hold costs the passing path nothing. Two further guards pin the pairing (the import and the `fetchImpl` default must move together, or the 300 s ceiling returns silently) and the shared-instance property (an `Agent` owns a connection pool, so one per spawn would leak a socket per spawn).

## v0.133.5

- fix: **a store timeout is reported as a timeout, not as an unreachable store — and on `post` the message says the card may already exist.** `socket.timeout` is an alias of `TimeoutError`, which subclasses `OSError`, so `attention-ask.py`'s single `except (urllib.error.URLError, OSError)` swallowed both and printed `FAILED: attention store unreachable at <url> (timed out)` for a store that had accepted the connection and was merely slow. Measured 2026-10-12 against a listener that accepts and never answers, on `post` and on `poll` alike. Reachability was never decidable from that error anyway: an unroutable host raises `URLError(reason=TimeoutError)` — a connect that *timed out*, not one that was refused — so only the timeout is observable, and only the timeout is now named. A refused connection keeps the unreachable wording, which is the negative control the new tests pin: a blanket relabel would have passed the positive probe while destroying the message in the one case it was written for. On `post` the timeout also leaves the item's existence UNKNOWN, and the message now says so, because the store may commit the card while the client gives up waiting for the response — measured at a 0.15 s timeout against a ~0.13 s create path, the client printed `FAILED` and the store had committed the item. The retry is safe and non-duplicating (the store suppresses a re-push on the same `producer_id` + `dedup_key`), so the message names re-posting the same `--dedup-key` rather than leaving the caller to guess. ⚠️ That caveat is a fact about the **caller**, not about the failure, which is why it is a parameter and not a property of the error: `poll` writes nothing and must not claim it. The default `STORE_TIMEOUT` also rises 3 s → 10 s. The store's create path is O(items) — `Push` walks the whole items bucket on every push and queues behind the read path on one BoltDB write lock (`attention-controller` `pkg/attention-store-impl.go:1845`; 38,194 items when measured) — so its latency grows with the board: measured 0.10–0.19 s idle, 0.42–1.11 s under 6 concurrent readers, and a 2.26 s peak under 20, which is under 1.5x headroom against a 3 s default. ⚠️ The repo therefore reads `ATTENTION_STORE_TIMEOUT` with two defaults — this one now at 10 s, `attention-card-lookup.py` and `answered-watch.py` already there, and `attention-answer.py`, `pod-attention.py`, `manager-attention-watch.py`, `who-needs-me.py` and `notify-gate.py` still at 3 s — and the shipped comment states the divergence and its reason rather than leaving it silent. The server-side scan is filed separately; this is the client-side half.

## v0.133.4

- docs: **register `scripts/drive-ledger.py` in the README's script table and `llms.txt`'s helper list.** The script shipped in v0.133.2 and `agents/fleet-drive.md` was wired to it in v0.133.3, but neither index named it -- so the two places a reader looks to find out what a `scripts/*.py` is for both omitted the one that now owns the fleet-drive ledger's write path.

## v0.133.3

- fix: **`agents/fleet-drive.md` routes both of its ledger steps through the script.** Step 3 loads with `python3 $P/drive-ledger.py read` and step 7 persists with `merge`, so the agent supplies only this round's entries and never holds the merged set — a round can no longer shrink the ledger by omission. An **exit 2** from `read` means the file exists but cannot be parsed, which is **not** an empty ledger: every candidate is `unverifiable` that round and the refusal is reported in the header. A genuinely absent file prints `{"round": null, "entries": []}` and exits 0, and the two cases are deliberately different — reading a corrupt ledger as empty is how the 2026-09-23 run lost 29 entries. The file also gains the `P=${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT:-$HOME/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/claude-supervisor}/scripts` definition its siblings already carry and it never had. `commands/steps/drive.md` owns no ledger rules and is unchanged.

- fix: **the drive leg's ledger write path is a mechanical gate, not a keep-in-sync sentence.** `scripts/check-drive-ledger-writes.py` asserts four things — first that `scripts/drive-ledger.py` **exists on disk**, then, across `agents/` and `commands/`: `agents/fleet-drive.md` names `drive-ledger.py` at least twice (a read and a write — the only clause that fails on the tree as it stood, **0** before this change); the literal `ledger.json` appears **exactly once**, as the `LEDGER  ~/.claude/state/…` report line (**5** occurrences over **3** lines before); and no ledger-referencing line **writes** it through an inline interpreter. ⚠️ **The third clause is scoped twice, and both scopes were forced by a false positive rather than chosen.** Unscoped across the two directories the interpreter pattern has **12** matches at v0.133.0 (`commands/open.md` 6, `agents/manager-drive.md` 2, plus four more) in files this change never touches, so a tree-wide "0 matches" can never exit `0`; and unscoped to *writes* it flagged this change's own step 7, because that line **forbids** the practice and therefore names `jq` and `python3 -c` on a line that mentions the ledger — a rule that cannot tell a prohibition from an instruction forces you to stop naming the tool you are forbidding, which is worse prose. The threat is data *loss*, which needs a write; a `jq` that only reads is harmless. The redirect is spelled `\s>` and never a bare `>`: this doc is full of `<n>`/`<ts>`/`<SECTION>` placeholders and a bare `>` matches their closing bracket (measured 2026-10-12, flagging step 7 on the `>` in `"<ts>"`). ⚠️ **`\b` must never appear in a `git grep -E` pattern** — git's ERE does not implement it and it matches nothing, so a zero-hit sweep reads as a pass (reproduced on this repo: on `scripts/asked-ledger.py`, `argparse` → 3 hits, `\bargparse\b` → 0). `scripts/tests/test_check_drive_ledger_writes.py` (14 cases) stages a real tree per clause — the check derives its root from its own `__file__`, so the resolution path under test is the real one — and every absence assertion is shown firing.

- fix: **two findings from the PR review of the gate itself, both taken.** (1) The gate validated that `agents/fleet-drive.md` *names* `drive-ledger.py` but never that the file **exists** — so it passed green on a branch where the agent would have run a command that was not there, and the drive leg would have died at runtime with `No such file`. It now asserts the callee, checked **before** the mention count, because a name is exactly what a mention count cannot tell from a working call; `TestClauseDTheCalleeExists` is the test that was red on that branch. (2) `WRITE_SIGNAL` used `\b`, which works in Python `re` and matches **nothing** in git's ERE — the trap this file's own docstring warns against. A pattern correct here and silently broken one file over is a worse neighbour than a slightly longer one that is correct in both, so every boundary is now spelled `[^A-Za-z0-9_]`. `TestPatternHygiene` asserts no pattern contains `\b` and that the rewrite did not narrow the pattern, in both directions — and that test caught a hole the old `\b` shared: `shutil.copyfile` was never matched, because `copy` is followed by `f` and the trailing boundary rejected it.

## v0.133.2

- fix: **the fleet-drive ledger has one writer, and it is a script.** `agents/fleet-drive.md` step 7 merged `~/.claude/state/fleet-drive/ledger.json` with a hand-written jq expression; measured 2026-10-12, a precedence bug in one round collapsed the file from 376 entries to 1, and the re-nudge/re-reap suppression history — the whole reason the file exists — went with it. The prose already said *"Merge, never replace"* and had said it for months: prose has nothing to run against it. The merge now lives in `scripts/drive-ledger.py`, with `read` and `merge` subcommands, and **the agent supplies only this round's entries** — so a round cannot shrink the ledger by omission, because it never holds the merged set at all. ⚠️ **The file carries two shapes at once, and they are two schemas rather than two containers.** Measured 2026-10-12 14:13: 16 top-level flat rows keyed by an 8-char `sid` (3 of them also carrying `open_boxes`, never `suppression`) *and* 5 `entries`-list rows keyed by `session_id`, with **3 sids present in both** — 18 distinct sessions, and a reader that sees only `entries` sees 5. The flat shape is not even internally uniform, so normalisation assumes no fixed field set on either side. A canonical `entries` row wins over a legacy flat row for the same sid, and a legacy row's absent `open_boxes` normalises to `null`, never `0`: 0 means verified-finished and null means never counted, and step 3's suppression rule compares the value across rounds. ⚠️ **A corrupt ledger is REFUSED, not read as empty** — the one place this deliberately diverges from its sibling `scripts/asked-ledger.py`, which degrades to an empty ledger on an unreadable file. A false "empty" is exactly the regression step 3 records from 2026-09-23 (37 entries read as 8), so `read` exits `2` on a file it cannot parse and `merge` refuses to overwrite one, leaving the `.bak` as the forensic copy. Locking and the atomic write follow `asked-ledger.py` (`fcntl.flock` held across the read-modify-write) and `fleet-snapshot.py` (`makedirs` → tmp → `os.replace` → unlink-on-failure). The tmp name is **pid-suffixed** rather than the fixed `ledger.json.tmp` the prose named — two overlapping writers on one tmp path is part of what made the wipe silent. `.bak` is new (it has no prior art in this repo) and is written from the **pre-merge** content *before* the rename. Ships `scripts/drive-ledger.py`, `scripts/tests/test_drive_ledger.py` (23 cases) and `scripts/check-drive-ledger-writes.py`, wired into `precommit`.
- test: **the drive ledger's merge is pinned by mutation, not by a single happy path.** Five of the 23 cases in `scripts/tests/test_drive_ledger.py` are **mutated copies of the real `drive-ledger.py`**, one source-level break each — a merge that drops sessions absent this round, one that skips the `.bak`, a non-atomic write, an unnormalised shape, an unparseable ledger accepted. `TestDiscrimination` requires each to violate **exactly its own invariant** and no other, so a variant that merely exits non-zero for an unrelated reason does not count; a set is returned rather than a boolean precisely because a boolean cannot tell "fails its own assertion" from "fails everything". ⚠️ Each variant runs in a **fresh directory**, and that isolation is the test rather than tidiness: the variants deliberately leave debris (`non-atomic-write` strands a tmp file, `skips-bak` takes none), and sharing one directory let a previous variant's leftovers decide the next one's verdict — `unnormalised-shape` was reported as also violating `consumes-the-tmp`. The fixture is the live file's measured shape, overlapping sids included, and the no-op control pins that an unrun merge leaves 5 `entries` rows and 16 flat sid keys — failing both the count assertion (18) and the shape assertion, which a canonical fixture would have satisfied.

## v0.133.1

- fix: **the repair dispatch now names its two instruments, because the positional reference it used resolved to nothing.** `commands/steps/manager-repair.md` read *"Read `live` and `target` with the instruments **the cap check above** already uses"* — but that file is a standalone router step (`user-invocable: false`), read on its own at router step 7, while the cap check lives in `manager-spawn.md` at router step 6, a **different file**. A reader of the repair step alone therefore had no instrument to follow, and the count it must read is the one place in this plugin where the population is load-bearing: `worker-sessions.py --count` counts the **worker** sessions (the spawn ledger joined to the liveness channels), while `session-liveness.py --list --json` counts **every** live session — managers and the operator's own included. Reading the latter into the cap arithmetic reports a fleet *at target* on a fleet with headroom, which withholds a whole repair round and renders `repairs held: at target (<N+M>/<target>)` on a tick that had `min(3, target − N)` capacity — a well-formed, internally consistent line that nothing in it distinguishes from a correct stop. `docs/fleet-surface.md` § *The repair dispatch* carried the same shape one hop out (*"the same instruments the tick's own cap check uses (item 5's script and its target read)"*), so both sites now name `scripts/worker-sessions.py --count` and `scripts/worker-target.py` outright. ⚠️ **This is not a restatement of the cap numbers and does not breach the single-home rule** — that rule binds `spawn.maxConcurrent` / `maxConcurrentHard`, and `scripts/check-worker-target.py` enforces it by asserting every `commands/` and `agents/` file naming `maxConcurrent` carries the pointer rather than its own copy. The **instrument's name** is already carried at `manager-spawn.md`, `manager-under-target.md` and `output-shape.md` — each names `worker-sessions.py` outright; the repair dispatch was the one call site that named neither the script nor a resolvable pointer to it. ⚠️ **`agents/manager-drive.md` clause (5) is deliberately untouched** — its delegation to item 5 is a resolvable pointer and was already recorded as correct; editing that clause would be a no-op. Change set: `commands/steps/manager-repair.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.133.0

- feat: **the fleet round now proposes a manager for a goal that holds approved work and no manager.** Measured 2026-10-12: five approved planning tasks on goal *Read CDB Data Through Weldall (MVP)* (seibert-brogrammers) sat unmanaged until the operator noticed and pushed the Fleet Manager by hand. The round was structurally blind to it — the sweep classifies **live sessions**, so a goal whose work was approved and never opened has no session to sweep from, and the unmanaged-topic candidate covers the other half (a topic whose workers are live and whose manager is not). `scripts/propose-managers.py` is the reverse index for that gap, at goal granularity: it scans every configured vault's task files for goals holding at least 2 approved, non-terminal, `phase: planning`/`execution` tasks with no live manager, ranks them by goal priority then approved-task count, and posts at most one card per free manager slot, keyed `fleet-propose-manager:<goal name>` with `start manager` / `not now`. ⚠️ **The manager-match is `fleet-board.py`'s own**, imported rather than copied: its `_norm_session_name` / `_matches_one_title` accept the registry's `⚙ ` decoration and an abbreviated title, which an equality test does not — the live manager for this change's own motivating goal is `Read CDB Data Through Weldall Manager` against a goal titled `Read CDB Data Through Weldall (MVP)`, and the naive form listed that goal as unmanaged with its manager running (measured 2026-10-12). A goal also reaches a manager through its **topic**, the second hop of the chain `docs/fleet-surface.md` § *Which manager owns a subject* states. A missing tasks dir refuses (exit `2`) rather than reading as a clean round, and two same-titled goals in two vaults **refuse** rather than share a vault-free key the store would silently swallow. ⚠️ **The slot gate reads the HARD manager cap** (`resolveMaxManagers`, called through node exactly as `scripts/worker-target.py` calls `resolveMaxConcurrent`) — the reason is the operator's ceiling, not the server's refusal: the recipe the card's answer uses spawns a wezterm tab directly (`commands/open.md` § Step 2C), which never reaches `managerLimitRefusal`, so this gate is the fleet's own, and the number it applies is the hard cap the operator's ruling names. A `0` on `spawn.maxManagers` — the documented off switch that disables both thresholds — resolves `hardLimit: null`, and that is **unlimited**, so every candidate posts; reading it as "no free slot" would turn the operator's own off switch into permanent silence. The live count is `fleet-board.py --json`'s own manager classification, Fleet Manager excluded; an unreadable board is UNKNOWN and refuses (exit `2`), never zero. The dedup key carries the goal's **name alone** — a timestamp in it defeats the store's open-scoped suppression and re-asks the same question (measured 2026-10-02 on the sibling under-target card). The card is an ASK: this change never spawns, and `commands/steps/manager-spawn.md`'s "a manager does not spawn managers" ban gains its one exception — the operator's `start manager` answer *is* the naming. Ships `scripts/propose-managers.py`, `scripts/tests/test_propose_managers.py` (50 cases) and `commands/steps/propose-manager.md`; the predicate, ranking and slot gate live in the script and neither the step file nor the runbook restates them. ⚠️ **The router row is deliberately NOT in this change** — the router table moves from `commands/fleet-tick.md` to `commands/fleet-drive.md` under a sibling change, and this one does not touch `fleet-tick.md`. Row 19 lands there once that fold merges; until it does, `commands/steps/propose-manager.md` is unreachable (`check-step-when.py` checks rows → files, not files → rows, so it cannot catch this).

## v0.132.0

- refactor: **the fleet layer's round command is now `/supervisor:fleet-drive`; the separate round verb is gone, so each layer has one `drive` verb.** The fleet side carried a three-way split — `fleet-loop` (arm), a round command, and `fleet-drive` (the by-hand act leg) — while the manager side carried two — `manager-loop` (arm), `manager-drive` (round). The manager side's shape is the one that holds: its round is both what the wakeup names and what running by hand runs, so there is no second verb to learn. The fleet round's body (router table, INLINE hard rules) moved verbatim into `commands/fleet-drive.md`, which becomes the wakeup target and keeps the drive leg as its router row 5; `commands/fleet-loop.md` arms it with `/supervisor:fleet-drive --interval <N>m`. ⚠️ **This is a breaking change for anyone who invoked the removed verb or armed it directly** — the wakeup prompt, the runbook's § Cadence mechanics and every doc reference now name `fleet-drive`. Three checks had to move with it: `check-budget.py` gains `commands/fleet-drive.md` in `WAKEUP_TARGETS` (the `*-tick.md` glob it relied on now matches nothing on the fleet side, so the strict 10 KB wakeup cap would otherwise have applied to no file while still reporting green), and `check-handback-keys.py`'s `ROUTER` points at the new file. The folded body fits the cap by dropping the by-hand act-leg prose, which `commands/steps/drive.md` already carries as the drive leg's own step. `commands/fleet-loop.md` also gains a description that describes the arm rather than the round — the two commands had shared one byte-for-byte.

## v0.131.2

- test: **the cluster spawn's retry classification is now pinned by the codes it must cover, not by one example.** `server/cluster-spawn.test.mjs` exercised `ECONNREFUSED` retrying and the four post-delivery codes (`ECONNRESET`, `EPIPE`, `UND_ERR_SOCKET`, `UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT`) *not* retrying — but the other six members of the retryable set (`EHOSTUNREACH`, `ENETUNREACH`, `ENOTFOUND`, `EAI_AGAIN`, `ETIMEDOUT`, `UND_ERR_CONNECT_TIMEOUT`) were pinned by nothing, so an edit dropping one from `CONNECTION_ERROR_CODES` would have left every test green. The set is now restated in the test from the module's own documented decision and asserted code by code — three attempts each, `[250, 500]` backoff, and the code named in the final error — so a change to the classification has to be made in two places, deliberately. A second case pins `error.code` as well as `error.cause?.code`, the left-hand branch of `isConnectionFailure`'s `??` that every existing test left unexercised. Both guard the same silent failure: an error class leaving the set with nothing to notice.

## v0.131.1

- fix: **the fleet round's router table is read more than once, and the later reads are now named as mandatory.** `commands/fleet-tick.md` carried the read-on-`When` rule with nothing saying *when* the table is evaluated, so a round honoured only the rows decidable at the head (`every tick` and the cold `rules` row) and never returned to the table once the sweep hand-back arrived — which is exactly when **every other row** becomes decidable, `reap` among them since its `When` is the drive leg. Measured 2026-10-12 on a cold round of the deployed `v0.130.0` (round `2026-10-12T05-54-15Z`): rows 3 (`orphan=46`, `collision=1`), 8 (`ledger=16`) and 11 (`blocked=3`) each had a `When` that held and **none of the three was read**, so the round omitted the `📋 Open with the operator` render that `commands/steps/output-shape.md` declares *"Never omitted"* (16 open ledger entries unrendered) and reported its blocked set while stating *"I haven't read either pane yet"* — that pane read is `commands/steps/needs-input.md`'s protocol. Across the Fleet Manager's transcript up to that point the only step-file reads were `drive.md`, `sweep.md` and that round's two batches — **no conditional step file had ever been read**. ⚠️ **The next round read all five of its held rows, and the difference was a peer's message naming them** — the manager's own reading of the first round was *"the list is never checked again, so those three can never be reached"*. The rows are therefore reachable, but only when something outside the round names them, which is what this clause removes the need for. The table gains one ⚠️ clause naming the later reads and their triggers, and scoping them to rows that have **not already run this round** — the first revision said only "reading each row whose `When` now holds", which at the before-render point would have licensed re-running the once-per-round rows — **every row whose `When` is `every tick`**, not the five the first revision happened to list — and a second sweep or drive leg duplicates sends to peers. **No `When` cell changed**, so `check-step-when.py` is unaffected, and the file stays under the 10 KB wakeup cap (8,830 → 9,736 B).

## v0.131.0

- refactor: **`commands/manager-drive.md` becomes the loop's round and `commands/manager-loop-tick.md` is deleted (40,927 → 9,064 B).** The arm (`commands/manager-loop.md`) now arms `ScheduleWakeup` with `/supervisor:manager-drive "<subject>"` instead of the tick, so **the ≤10 KB wakeup budget follows the wakeup target rather than the filename** — `scripts/check-budget.py` gained an explicit `WAKEUP_TARGETS` list beside its `*-tick.md` glob, because a glob-only rule would have applied the strict cap to nothing on the manager side while reporting green. The round carries the router table, the read-on-`When` rule, the "the rules do not live here" rule, its arguments and its pre-dispatch gate, and nothing else: the drive-leg dispatch, the per-bucket shape clause and the recording step stay in the step files they already live in, and the arm's copy of the router table — its second home — is removed. Its displaced rationale moves verbatim to `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § *Command rationale — maintained*.
- fix: **four guards followed their blocks to the step files, and one budget entry followed the wakeup target.** `check-bucket-clause`, `check-recording-step` and `check-provenance-sentence` each named `commands/manager-drive.md` as a home of a block it no longer carries — a router holds no executable rule — so each list now names the step file that does, and `check-provenance-sentence`'s byte-identity half is a no-op with one verbatim carrier (stated in its docstring rather than silently dropped). `check-subject-write-scope`'s `RELOCATED` gained `manager-drive`, because both writers now resolve through `commands/steps/manager-resolve.md`. `scripts/check-budget.py --tighten` lowers the `manager-drive` and `manager-loop` baseline entries to 9,064 and 25,579.
- refactor: **the 13 `commands/steps/manager-*.md` files shrink 157,858 → 124,151 B (−21.4 %) as their rationale moves to the runbook.** These are the loop's **only per-tick artifact** — the arm is invoked once to arm and `commands/open.md` is one-shot — so this is where the recurring cost actually falls. The baseline is master's own as of the merge, not the pre-merge tree: PR #259 rewrote three of these files, so the figures are measured against the tree this branch actually leaves. Per file: sweep −38 %, under-target −33 %, tail −27 %, spawn −22 %, readings −20 %, classify −14 %, drive −13 %, resolve −13 %, unowned −9 %, self-improvement −8 %, repair −4 %; `manager-gates.md` and `manager-handoff.md` are unchanged (the first reverted under a guard, see below; the second was already pure rule). Every file's frontmatter (each carries `user-invocable: false`), headings, fenced code blocks and `When:` line are byte-identical, and every line that left a file is present verbatim in the runbook.
- fix: **five repo guards pin blocks in the step files, and the trim had to give them back.** `check-bucket-clause`, `check-provenance-sentence`, `check-recording-step`, `check-subject-write-scope` and `check-gate-class-order` each assert that a specific block is present, or is **byte-identical across two or three files**. Condensing one copy diverged it from its twins, so the affected lines were restored from the pre-trim revision — 3,305 B of the trim given back, and **5 dated-citation lines remain in the step files by necessity rather than oversight**. This is the same class the router restructure hit, with a sharper edge: there it had to *move* the guards, here the guards' own byte-identity requirement is what bounds the trim. A future pass that wants those lines gone has to change the guards in the same change.
- refactor: **`commands/manager-loop.md` — the arm — shrinks 37,276 → 26,981 B (−27.6 %), bringing it under the 30 KB command-body budget.** Its incident narratives and dated citations move verbatim to the same runbook section. The router step table, the frontmatter and every fenced code block are byte-identical, every moved block is present in the runbook, and **0 dated citations remain outside code fences**. `scripts/check-budget.py --tighten` lowers the baseline entry from 37,276 to 26,981.
- refactor: **`commands/open.md` shrinks 101,653 → 60,659 B (−40.3 %) as its rationale moves verbatim to the runbook, and the budget ratchet tightens to match.** The body is re-injected on every `/supervisor:open` invocation, so its incident narratives and dated citations now live in `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § *Command rationale — maintained* (Personal vault) — deliberately a new maintained section rather than the banner-marked *"Historical — not normative"* one it now sits above. Every executable rule stays: all 20 headings preserved in order, all fenced code blocks byte-identical, 112 blocks moved, and **no backtick code span lost from either file**. `scripts/check-budget.py --tighten` lowers `commands/open.md`'s baseline entry from 101,653 to 60,659, so the ratchet now forbids it regrowing.
- fix: **`commands/steps/manager-readings.md` no longer cites the retired tick as a live carrier of the round's own law.** The file pointed at `commands/manager-loop-tick.md` — deleted by this change — as the example of a file whose law a misplaced per-round rule would contradict; that law now lives in `commands/manager-drive.md`, so the citation names the round. ⚠️ **Found by the local `pr-review` pass, not by a guard** — no check pins that sentence, so the earlier claim that no `manager-loop-tick` reference survives outside CHANGELOG history was false as written. The remaining occurrences are CHANGELOG history, two guard docstrings and one test-fixture constant, all historical or modelling the fleet loop's still-valid `*-tick.md` shape.
- fix: **the round no longer claims it "arms nothing and schedules nothing" — its own step 12 calls `ScheduleWakeup` and re-arms two `Monitor`s.** The sentence existed to explain why `--interval` is inert for this command, and it overreached: step 12 corrects `noop` with a fresh `ScheduleWakeup` on a no-change tick and re-arms the two watcher channels that expire mid-tick, so the round schedules plenty — it simply never *sets* the interval. It now says that, and names step 12 rather than leaving the reader to reconcile the two. The growth is a deliberate hand-raise of the `commands/manager-drive.md` ratchet entry, 9,035 → 9,407, still 833 B under the ≤10 KB wakeup-target cap.

## v0.130.2

- fix: **the under-target step's `When` drops the one clause the router could never evaluate.** `commands/steps/manager-under-target.md` gated on *live workers below target **and** no ready row **and** the ask not declined*, but the step's own text says the decline is *"an input this branch reads"* — so the third clause is produced **by the file it gates**, and a router instructed to read a step only when its `When` holds could not honour it. Measured 2026-10-11: a live tick read the step at 18:48:37Z, before its card poll returned `ANSWERED: skip`, which made the read look like an ungated one. The clause is dropped from the step and byte-identically from **both** router rows (`commands/manager-loop.md` and `commands/manager-loop-tick.md`, which `check-step-when.py` pins together), and the step's declined-ask bullet now records why so it is not re-added. The branch's behaviour is unchanged — it already handled the declined case.

## v0.130.1

- fix: **the sweep reader stops returning the rendered table it was told not to return.** `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 9 carried the hand-back contract's pointer — *"The rendered table does **not** ride the report — it goes to the payload path the render already writes"* — and then, one line below, **item 1 of the report list required that table verbatim, in a fenced block**. The leg obeyed the list: measured 2026-10-11 across two live rounds, the hand-back was **2,727 B** and **12,364 B** against a 1 KB cap whose required set is 15 bucket counts. Item 1 is deleted and the list renumbered, so the ⚠️ and the list no longer disagree, and the section names the correction so the item is not re-added. The renumber moved the `stuck`-rows section from item 7 to item 6, so the **four pointers that read it by ordinal** were repaired with it — one within this file (`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md:88`) and three in the drive leg (`agents/manager-drive.md:31`, `:100`, `:472`) — because the drive leg takes its freshness reading from that section by number, and a stale ordinal there would point its mtime source at the Necessity block. ⚠️ **The removal was one-sided until `<output_format>` was labelled too** — that block still presented the box table as the leg's output, so step 9 alone left the leg free to return it. It now carries the same *two artifacts, two shapes* statement the converted fleet legs use, and `<success_criteria>` scopes the byte-identical requirement to the **payload**. That deliberate addition raises this file's baselined max by 415 B, by hand, because the ratchet's `--tighten` lowers only.

## v0.130.0
