- refactor: **both fleet hand-backs are capped at ≤ 1 KB and the digest moves to disk.** `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` and `agents/fleet-drive.md` now return `key=value` verdict lines — the bucket counts and the drive verdicts — under the contract in `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/docs/handback-contract.md`, and write their full render to `~/.claude/state/fleet-handback/<round timestamp>.digest` and `.drive`. The fleet loop's eleven consumers (`commands/fleet-tick.md`, `commands/fleet-drive.md`, and `commands/steps/{sweep,drive,output-shape,confirm-candidates,needs-input,escalate,auto-compaction,open-items,persist}.md`) read the payload at the `payload=` path the hand-back names, and the three router `When` cells that tested a digest *section* now test its count — `orphan|collision|unmanaged > 0`, `blocked > 0`, `context70 > 0` — kept byte-identical to their step files by `check-step-when.py`.
- feat: **a guard asserts the router's hand-back count cells against the sweep reader's key set** (`scripts/check-handback-keys.py`). A `When` cell reads a hand-back *count*, so a key the sweep reader never emits makes that cell false forever and its step silently never runs — the defect that shipped when `held` was declared in the digest render but absent from the hand-back, and `held` is the class the render treats as winning over every other. Wired into `precommit` beside `check-step-when.py`, which catches the same drift one layer up: there the trigger and its body disagree, here the trigger and the data it tests do. It is a first slice, not the whole required set — it asserts only the keys a router cell tests, so it would not have caught `held` itself, and its docstring says so rather than overclaiming.

## v0.129.5

- fix: **a manager-loop tick that lands while the previous round is still in flight is now stated, not improvised.** `commands/steps/manager-readings.md` carries the rule: such a firing is **absorbed** — no reading, no sweep, no dispatch, no write — and each of the round's three writes belongs to the round that *produced* its input (the store save to the round that dispatched the sweep reader, the verdicts-cache write to the round that dispatched the drive leg, the topic-page write-back to the round whose own sweep produced the `noop: false`). A late leg's `Audit` block is **dropped**, never merged — which `commands/steps/manager-drive.md` already required in words (*"never merge in rows from an earlier tick"*). Measured 2026-10-11: a tick overran its interval by ~3× and the firing inside it was absorbed because the round was still in flight. `scripts/check-round-overlap.py` asserts the rule has exactly one home and that a **tick-router** row dispatches it every tick, and is wired into `precommit`.

## v0.129.4

- fix: **the manager loop stops asking the operator a question the manager owns, and stops reading a roster `[ref]` as a session id.** Two runtime-measured corrections to the loop's step files. `commands/steps/manager-classify.md`'s plan-gate parking cell classified a worker parked on its own `plan-task` gate as **waiting-on-human** — but sections, decomposition, DoD and SC evidence shapes are the manager's authored act (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 4), so a worker the ladder opened at 7–8/10 that raised shape gaps put them to the operator, who was never their owner: measured 2026-10-10, a worker opened at 8/10 hit its own `plan-task` ≥8 gate at 7/10 and raised three SC/evidence-shape gaps as an operator modal. The cell now routes those gaps **worker → its manager** and cards only a gap that is genuinely an operator decision. `commands/steps/manager-readings.md`'s roster bullet now states that `[ref]` is a 6-char display handle and **not** a Claude session id — reading one as an id produced a wrong assertion about a live session (a manager called `64b4a415` *"the recorder, not a manager that returned"*, an 8-char **session-id** prefix, while `session-liveness.py --check 64b4a415-906d-457f-81f9-48a842007b88` returned LIVE with 121,321 pre-stop turns) — and points at the join rule's single home in `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 5 rather than restating it.

- fix: **a cluster spawn reports the connection-level cause instead of only `fetch failed`, and retries a transient connection failure.** A spawn failed three times in a row on 2026-10-11 (19:30–20:05) with undici's opaque `TypeError: fetch failed`, while every shell probe to the same host, port and path answered in under 10 ms — and at 20:24 the identical call succeeded twice, full round trip. The real reason was on `error.cause` the whole time and the module discarded it, so the failure could not be diagnosed from its own report. `startClusterSession` now renders that cause (`ECONNREFUSED 192.168.178.34:443`) and retries a connection-level failure up to `CLUSTER_CONNECT_ATTEMPTS` with a backoff — narrowly, and keyed on an identifiable connection code rather than on undici's wrapper, which is thrown for permanent failures too (a blocked port reports `fetch failed — bad port`, and retrying that is 750 ms spent reaching the same verdict): an `AbortError` (the module's own timeout) and every HTTP response, 401 above all, still return on the first attempt, so a refusal is never tripled. The retryable set is limited to **pre-delivery** failures — the request never reached the service — because `POST /prompt` is not idempotent: `ECONNRESET`, `EPIPE`, `UND_ERR_SOCKET` and `UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT` are all reachable *after* delivery, where a retry would silently run a second turn (the 16:1x failure on this path was exactly that shape — the pod log showed `prompt intake` while the client saw a failure). `UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT` is doubly excluded: a server that accepts the connection and never answers is wedged, not busy, and retrying it would turn the module's own "a wedged pod costs one error" guarantee into three. Measured on the real path against a closed port: the original reports `fetch failed` after 18 ms; the fix reports `fetch failed — ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:45999` after 766 ms.

## v0.129.3

- fix: the tracked-set watcher no longer re-posts a park the operator has already answered. `open_store_items` reads `GET /api/1.0/attention`, which returns open items only, so an ANSWERED card left no trace in it and the watcher read that absence as a **store fault** — measured 2026-10-10, `park:beeb6128` was re-posted at 20:12:32, two minutes after the operator's 20:10:35 `skip`, and four times across ~3h20m. Candidates are now resolved against `GET /api/1.0/attention/history` (the only read that carries an answered card) and an answered key is recorded as `ANSWERED` — naming the answered instance — instead of re-posted. A key whose latest record is not an *answer* still re-posts — one with no record at all, and equally a **cleared or reaped** card, whose `closed` row carries no `answered_at` (the dominant closed shape: 9,339 of 9,454 rows per `answered-attribution.py`) and an answer about an **earlier** park on the same session still re-posts (this watcher is the only producer of `park:` rows, so an answered row would otherwise stay newest forever and silence every later lost park on that session) — so the change suppresses an answer and never a fault. Also corrects `open_store_items`'s docstring, which claimed the item read returns items in every state.

## v0.129.2

- test: **prove the budget ratchet's failure paths, not just its green one.** `scripts/check-budget.py` is a tighten-or-fail ratchet, and its baseline is seeded from this repository's own tree — so `make precommit` passes by construction on any revision of it, and a script that did nothing at all would pass identically. `scripts/tests/test_check_budget.py` covers the three failure modes the design turns on (a baselined body above its recorded max, below it, and a body over budget that is absent from the baseline), plus the strict `*-tick.md` cap at and over the line, the missing-baseline fail-closed path, a baselined file that vanished, and the property the ratchet exists for: `--tighten` lowers only under-max entries and **does not rescue a growth**.

## v0.129.1

- docs: `scripts/fleet-board.py`'s module docstring no longer restates the task-owner resolution order — rule 2 of its four tree rules still said a worker's parent comes from the task's `goals:` alone, which the tier-1 `managed_by` reader in v0.128.6 had just made false; it now points at `docs/fleet-surface.md` § *Which manager owns a subject*, the single statement of the rule, so the docstring cannot drift from `manager_on_chain` again

## v0.129.0

- feat: **`commands/manager-loop.md` becomes a router with per-round step files, and the loop splits into an arm and a tick.** The command was 184,352 B and the harness re-injected the whole body on every wakeup — roughly 96 times a day. It is now a router (a `#`/Step/When/File table plus the inline invariants and gates) whose per-round work lives in `commands/steps/*.md`, read **only when its `When` holds**; a new `commands/manager-loop-tick.md` (3.8 KB) is what the wakeup names, and the arm is invoked once. Every executable rule was **moved verbatim** — a provenance check confirms each source line now sits in exactly one artifact, with none dropped and none duplicated.
- feat: **the budget lint is wired as a tighten-or-fail ratchet** (`scripts/check-budget.py` + `scripts/budget-baseline.json`). Eight bodies are over the 30 KB budget and this change may not shrink the seven it does not own, so the gate holds each at its recorded size and fails in **both** directions — above it (a regression) and below it (a stale baseline that would let the file regrow). `--tighten` lowers only the entries genuinely under their max, so a second branch rebasing onto this one cannot undo the ratchet.
- feat: **two guards protect the restructure's own relocations.** `scripts/check-gate-class-order.py` asserts the gate class test still precedes the `attention-ask.py post` call in `steps/manager-gates.md` — the ordering was a measured fix on 2026-10-11 and nothing asserted it before. `scripts/check-step-when.py` asserts every step file's `When:` is byte-identical to its router row's `When` cell, because a trigger narrower than the body it introduces silently gates out work that used to run every tick.
- fix: **five repo guards now follow their rules to the new step files.** `check-spawn-mode`, `check-recording-step`, `check-bucket-clause`, `check-subject-write-scope` and `check-provenance-sentence` each named `commands/manager-loop.md` as the home of a rule that moved, so each would have failed on a correct restructure — a restructure that moves rules has to move the guards asserting where they live, not just the prose.
- fix: **this row's step files collided with the fleet row's in the shared `commands/steps/` directory.** Both rows had written `drive.md`, `readings.md` and `sweep.md` under the same names with different bodies (4,722 B vs 10,730 B on `drive.md` alone), so merging master would have left one row's router reading the other row's step file. No guard could see it: `check-step-when.py` compares only the `When:` line, and all three pairs were byte-identical there. This row's 13 files are renamed with a `manager-` prefix, making the two namespaces disjoint by construction rather than by luck.
- fix: **the restructure's own step paths were unresolvable, and the arm carried its step list twice.** The router tables named `steps/<name>.md`, a relative path that resolves against the session's cwd — a vault or workspace directory — rather than the plugin root; all 28 references now use the absolute `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/commands/steps/…` form the fleet row's tables already use. The arm also restated its entire step list a second time under `## Steps`, so the list had two homes and only the router table was guarded; the duplicate is gone and the arm drops from 38,553 B.
- fix: `scripts/check-budget.py --tighten` re-encoded the baseline's non-ASCII escapes, so lowering one entry rewrote the whole file; it now reproduces the file's own escaping and the diff stays on the entries that actually moved.
- fix: **the step files no longer register as slash commands.** Claude Code walks `commands/` **recursively**, so every `commands/steps/*.md` was registering as `/<plugin>:steps:<name>` — 33 entries in the `/` menu, each invocable standalone and out of sequence with none of the tick's state. Every step file now carries `user-invocable: false`; the router reads them by path and is unaffected. ⚠️ The absence of `allowed-tools` was never the lever and the earlier reading of it was inverted: `allowed-tools` is an **additive grant**, so a file without it inherits the session's permissions rather than gaining any — the cost here was menu pollution, never a permission hole.
- feat: **the hand-back contract now has one home — `docs/handback-contract.md`.** The sweep reader and the drive leg each return verdict lines and counts in `key=value` form, **≤ 1 KB** and **non-empty**, with the leg's required set present (bucket counts for the reader, drive verdicts for the leg); the full payload is written to a deterministic path instead of riding the report. Both manager agents carry a pointer to the doc rather than their own copy of the rule, and the doc is deliberately **agent-agnostic** — it names the fleet row's `fleet-sweep-reader` and `fleet-drive` as its other carriers.

## v0.128.6

- fix: both task-owner resolvers now read a task's `managed_by` as tier 1 of the order `docs/fleet-surface.md` § *Which manager owns a subject* states — `scripts/fleet-board.py`'s `manager_on_chain` and `scripts/approved-not-started.py`'s `manager_for()` previously derived the owner from `goals:` alone, so the tier the doc calls the first step of the rule was reachable by no code; an absent or unresolvable `managed_by` still falls through to `goals:` in order, so a single-goal task never needs the field

- refactor: split `commands/fleet-loop.md` into an arm, a `commands/fleet-tick.md` router and `commands/steps/` step files; the recurring payload per tick falls from 70,790 B to 31,209 B

- fix: `manager-loop`'s `waiting-on-human` bullet now leads with the gate class test, so a closer-line `pick` routes to the pane handover before the post-to-the-board instruction is reached — the instruction previously preceded its own exception, and a manager reading top-down carded a closer-line `pick` the bullet forbids carding

## v0.128.3

- fix: `/supervisor:manager-status`'s `description:` no longer reads as side-effect-free — it names the resolution record and the caller's promotion to manager that its own recording step performs

## v0.128.1

- feat: **a goal or topic page records the session of the manager that armed it, and a task's owner is resolvable in one stated order.** Two frontmatter fields close the gap the 2026-10-09 full stop exposed: recovering the manager layer meant reverse-engineering which manager ran for which subject from `~/.claude/state/worker-manager/*.json` — a directory that is never cleaned and is not in git — and one manager could not be recovered at all because nothing had recorded its subject. ⚠️ **`manager_session_id` is a lead, never a verdict.** `commands/manager-loop.md` writes it when it arms the subject, and every reader runs `session-liveness.py --check` on it first — `LIVE` → jump, `ABSENT` → resume by id, `UNKNOWN` → fresh manager, `AMBIGUOUS` → fresh manager. ⚠️ **The write sits ABOVE the recording block, not inside it, and that placement is a contract rather than taste:** `scripts/check-recording-step.py` compares that block verbatim across `manager-loop`, `manager-status` and `manager-drive`, and `manager-status` declares *"No vault writes, no loop, no TTS"* — folding a vault write into the shared block would falsify that declaration and force the guard open. ⚠️ **`commands/open.md` Step 2C's ban on keying the step to a page stamp is superseded, and its reasoning is answered rather than dropped.** The ban was right that a topic manager restarts constantly and a written-once stamp goes stale; it was wrong that the remedy is to carry no stamp at all, because the liveness lead removes the hazard the ban named — the id is checked, and an `ABSENT` id resumes the conversation instead of assuming a live session. The half of the ban that still holds is kept in the text: this is a topic-page field, not a task's `claude_session_id`. ⚠️ **`docs/fleet-surface.md` § *Which manager owns a subject* is the single statement of the order** — task `managed_by` → the task's `goals:` in order, each goal tried before the topic that lists it → Fleet Manager — and it says explicitly that it is neither the § Session stamps rule (a different field on a different page, serving a different reader) nor the `docs/subject-resolution.md` chain (which resolves a session's subject, not a task's manager); the two compose, and neither is a fallback for the other. ⚠️ **No vault-cli change was needed and none is claimed:** `vault-cli` already round-trips both fields through `SetField`'s `default:` arm, verified against the installed v0.167.1 and against a HEAD build. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/open.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: `restart-worker.py` resumes a worker through its own launcher (task `launcher:` > goal > vault `claude_script`) instead of the caller's `CLAUDE_SCRIPT` or bare `claude`, which dropped the worker's `--mcp-config` and launcher env; an unresolvable launcher now refuses before the kill

## v0.128.0

- feat: cap concurrent `role: manager` sessions fleet-wide — `spawn.maxManagers` (soft, default 3) / `spawn.maxManagersHard` (hard, default 5), Fleet Manager excluded; counted from `fleet-board.py`'s manager classification so hand-started managers count; hard-cap refusal carded by `/supervisor:open`

## v0.127.0

- feat: **sessions are now paced as they are OPENED, which is a different question from how many may be live at once — and the concurrency caps could not see the failure that motivated this.** On 2026-10-10 the fleet started **22 sessions in 2.8 minutes** (11:03:38–11:06:24 CEST, read from the spawn ledger's `spawned_at` values), and the shared model backend went down under the arrival rate while **both caps were in force and the fleet was never full** — a population bound structurally cannot observe an arrival burst, so `spawn.maxConcurrent` / `maxConcurrentHard` were never going to catch this. The new `spawn.maxStartsPerMinute` (default **4**, `0` = off) bounds arrivals, resolved through the same source order and refusal discipline as the caps (`SUPERVISOR_MAX_STARTS_PER_MINUTE` → config → default, every source validated, a bad value refuses naming its file rather than falling back). ⚠️ **DELAY IS THE NORMAL PATH AND REFUSE IS THE CEILING**, and that is the deliberate difference from the cap: the callers are automated bulk paths (the `--flagged` batch, a post-stop recovery, a manager's To-open set), so refusing a 22-row batch outright fails half of it, while pacing the same starts spreads the first-turn context loads that took the backend down. Past a fixed 5-minute ceiling a start is refused instead — delay without a ceiling sheds no load, because a caller that is only ever delayed keeps its own queue full and arrives at the same instant it always would have. ⚠️ **The reservation is a SLOT, not a window count.** Each start reserves `max(now, last + interval)` in a state file, so the spacing is a property of the store rather than of how many callers happen to arrive together; a plain "count starts in the last minute" rule would let a batch arriving against an empty window all pass at once and then all wait at once — the thundering herd arriving on the far side of the check. ⚠️ **The reservation is WRITTEN BEFORE the wait, not after**, for the same reason: a simultaneous batch that read one stale stamp would compute one slot and all sleep to the same instant. ⚠️ **ONE IMPLEMENTATION, TWO CALL SITES, and the second is the one that matters.** The wezterm `--resume` branch of `commands/open.md` § 3.1 bypasses the server entirely and writes no ledger record — it is the path the recovery runbook actually uses — so it reaches the same pacing through the new `scripts/start-rate.mjs`, which **calls the same `reserveSlot` and shares the same state file** rather than restating the rule; the write-before-wait ordering lives in that one function, because restating it at a call site is how two copies drift — a second counter a reader cannot tell from the real one. ⚠️ **The CLI call site needs a timeout of at least 300s and does not get one by default.** It may sleep to the ceiling, and because the reservation is written BEFORE the sleep, a caller killed mid-sleep has already spent a slot without spawning anything — and `|| exit 1` then aborts the row. Claude Code's Bash tool defaults to 120s, so a paced batch run under the default fails exactly where the pacing matters; the requirement is stated at all three call sites. ⚠️ **Called as LATE as possible on both server paths** — immediately before the session is created, not beside the other guards — so a call refused for a malformed argument does not charge the next legitimate start for a config typo. ⚠️ **The read-modify-write is NOT atomic and that is named rather than hidden**: a simultaneous pair can compute one slot where the rate allows one, the window is a sub-millisecond local read+write against callers spaced by whole seconds, and the fix (an exclusive lock) is deliberately not built because a lock held by a crashed caller would refuse every subsequent start — a worse failure than the race it removes. `spawn.maxStartsPerMinute` is registered in `KNOWN_SPAWN_KEYS` in the same commit that enforces it, because a key accepted but unenforced is the "silently governs nothing" failure this module family exists to refuse. Change set: `server/start-rate.mjs`, `server/start-rate.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/config.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, `scripts/start-rate.mjs`, `commands/open.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.126.1

- fix: `restart-worker.py`'s `stale-load-path` check also accepts a restart when the worker's MCP config (from its live argv) or launcher script is newer than the session start, and names which input changed

## v0.126.0

- feat: **a manager can now resume an interactive worker it needs itself, so a fleet-stop recovery stops routing every mechanical resume through the operator.** `commands/manager-loop.md`'s `To resume` bullet forbade a manager from executing a `mode: interactive` row at all — *"a row whose `mode:` is `interactive` is never executed"*, reported `Not resumed`, with the path-B recipe handed to the operator as a `you run:` line. ⚠️ **Two of its three stated reasons were written for the `To open` case and do not transfer:** a tab that "cannot produce a headless worker" is a defect when opening a *new* worker, and is exactly what an `interactive` row's own mode asks for on a *resume*. Only the third — "writes **no ledger row**" — survived scrutiny, and it is kept rather than deleted (below). ⚠️ **The ban was the outlier, not the rule:** `docs/fleet-surface.md`'s resume-path table already routed a session to path B, and `docs/session-tiers.md` § The manager boundary already read *"A manager starts or resumes a worker on a vault task with `/supervisor:open --task`"* — so `docs/session-tiers.md` needs **no edit**, and is recorded here as the surface that was correct all along rather than as a third edited file. The bullet now splits by mode: `mode: headless` keeps path A (`spawn_agent(resume=…, interactive=false)`, `cwd` required), and `mode: interactive` takes **path B via `/supervisor:open "<task>"`**, whose resume branch *is* the wezterm `--resume` path and already carries the hold check and the `session-liveness.py --check` that refuses `LIVE`/`UNKNOWN`/`AMBIGUOUS` and resumes only on `ABSENT`. ⚠️ **The row's actual producer is the drive leg, and it is edited in lockstep — editing the caller alone would have left the new branch unreachable.** `agents/manager-drive.md`'s clause 11 carried the identical refusal, and its own text declares that the vault runbook wins on disagreement and that the two must change together; unchanged, the gate still failed clause 11 and escalated `Not resumed` before any row reached the caller. The drive leg now hands such a row over as an ordinary `To resume` carrying `mode=interactive`. ⚠️ **This rule is restated in five registers, and a first pass that fixed only the obvious ones left four of them stale — so the class was enumerated rather than patched instance-by-instance:** the same file's `<output_format>` (`agents/manager-drive.md:383`) still asserted *"`mode=interactive` never appears on a RESUME row"*; the **by-hand** caller (`commands/manager-drive.md`, `/manager-drive`) carried the same unsplit `To resume` bullet and would have run path A on an interactive row — refused, or silently headless under a `spawn.mode: headless` config, which is the `mode_source=config` defect item 6 exists to remove; `commands/worker-restart.md` restated the two-axis contract as *"Liveness picks the path"*; and `docs/fleet-surface.md`'s lead-in paragraph and its two-axis framing sentence both still stated the pre-change rule in the present tense, directly above the bullets that now say the opposite. All five agree on the rule itself now, and each keeps a one-line record of what it previously said rather than overwriting it silently. ⚠️ **The `mode:`-absent case was the one input they did *not* agree on, and it is aligned in the same change:** `commands/manager-loop.md` briefly held such a row — copying `To open`'s absent-mode hold into a resume bullet — while the drive leg, `/manager-drive` and the resume-path table all routed absent → path A, so one input was held by one caller and spawned by the other. All four now route absent → path A; only `To open` holds on an absent mode, and it is the one that should. ⚠️ **`docs/fleet-surface.md`'s *Three consequences, all binding* block is rewritten throughout, not just at its third bullet:** bullets 1 and 2 had carried the pre-change rule and stood as inverses of the edited bullet 3, so the block handed a reader two binding rules that contradicted each other. The stale *"A gate clause is the cheap fix here"* paragraph, which presented the escalate-never-hand-over behaviour as the honest current rule, is rewritten too. ⚠️ **The "never fall back to the wezterm path" half is kept, scoped to `To open`**, where it remains true and is the reason it was written. ⚠️ **The one surviving concern is recorded, not dropped:** a path-B resume goes through no server, so `server/ledger.mjs` never stamps `resumed_from` on it and the fleet cap does not exclude it (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 5, *"Auto-resumes are excluded"*) — that worker **is** counted against the cap where a path-A resume is not. Accepted and conservative (it refuses spawns rather than over-spawning), stated at both sites with item 5 as the rule's home. ⚠️ **`docs/fleet-surface.md`'s resume-path table gained the determinant that was missing:** the row's declared `mode:` is now read *first* and can force path B on its own, because path A refuses `resume` + `interactive:true` by construction; liveness then decides only among the rows A can serve. ⚠️ **Two stale liveness sentences in `commands/manager-loop.md` are corrected as a side effect of editing the same bullet** — it read *"the registry shows the resumed id"* and *"Liveness is decided by the session registry"*, both stale since `session-liveness.py` left the registry for the session-heartbeat endpoint in v0.125.0. The remaining stale sites — six more in that file, plus `agents/manager-drive.md`, `manager-status.md`, `manager-drive.md`, `fleet-status.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` and `cluster-heartbeat.py` — are deliberately **not** touched here; they are the known follow-up v0.125.0 already names, enumerated into one task rather than patched instance-by-instance. ⚠️ **`65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Step 4 clause 11 carries the identical ban and is edited alongside — out-of-repo by construction**, recorded here rather than checked, in the shape the v0.124.3 entry uses for the same file. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `commands/worker-restart.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.125.0

- feat: **the mjs twin answers liveness from the session-heartbeat endpoint, so `worker-sessions.mjs` and `worker-sessions.py` are one definition again.** The server had **no** endpoint reader at all (`grep -rn 'session-heartbeat' server/` returned nothing), so while the Python instrument had moved to attention-controller's store, the server-side counter still read the session registry ∪ the local heartbeat store — the two halves of one definition disagreeing about where liveness comes from, which is the drift `liveness.mjs`'s own header exists to prevent. The new `server/session-heartbeat.mjs` is the reader, and `workerSessions()` now reads it. ⚠️ **Verified live, both twins on the same call:** `worker-sessions.py --count` → **14**, and the mjs `workerSessions()` → **14** — the pair answers identically, which is the whole point of the change. ⚠️ **`workerSessions()` is now ASYNC**, because the endpoint is HTTP where the old source was two filesystem reads; `concurrentLimitError` in `supervisor.mjs` is async with it, and both call sites `await` it — they must, because a count resolved after the spawn has already spent the budget it protects is the ordering that function's own comment forbids. ⚠️ **`null` is "could not read it" and is never folded into `[]`** — a throwing transport, a non-200, or a body that is not a row array all answer `null`, and the caller refuses a spawn on `null` and permits one on `[]`, so collapsing the two turns a store outage into permission to spawn onto live work. ⚠️ **A row is live only on an explicit `live: true`**, and a malformed row is skipped rather than thrown on — this runs on the spawn path, where an uncaught throw is a failed spawn rather than a wrong number. ⚠️ **`SUPERVISOR_ATTENTION_STORE=off` now also disables the concurrent-worker cap**, and that is a real behaviour change: the store is the fleet's only liveness source, so a deployment that turned it off has no way to answer the question, and the honest answer is `null` — which refuses a spawn rather than permitting one. The remedy for an operator who wants spawns is to run the store. ⚠️ **The union rule left with the channels, and the auto-resume exclusion stayed** — it answers to the auto-resume gate's own 30-min crash-loop cap, and it used to hold only by accident (every auto-resume is headless and held no registry entry); a channel that CAN see headless workers is exactly what would have un-excluded it silently. ⚠️ **Three prose sites that described the old source are corrected, not left to contradict the code:** `heartbeat.mjs` twice claimed `listLive` is on the spawn path (it is now reached only through `registry-rehydrate.mjs`'s startup rehydration and `supervisor.mjs`'s roster), and `heartbeat.mjs`'s `state`-reservation note cited four readers where two remain (`fleet-board.py:1096`, `adopt-orphans.py:145`) — `worker-sessions.py` and `session-liveness.py` have left the raw-stamp path entirely. `live-workers.py`'s `mode` note carried the same stale claim about the mjs twin. ⚠️ **`liveness.mjs`'s `liveSessionIds` is NOT removed** — `registry-rehydrate.mjs` still uses it for the startup roster, which is a different question from the cap's. Change set: `server/session-heartbeat.mjs`, `server/worker-sessions.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/heartbeat.mjs`, `scripts/live-workers.py`, `server/session-heartbeat.test.mjs`, `server/worker-sessions.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **the `--list` stamp-coverage guard now covers the sessions that SHOULD stamp — the spawn ledger's workers — instead of every registry-live session.** The guard exists so a partial rollout cannot be handed back as a complete fleet, but its population made it refuse forever: measured live 2026-10-09, `session-liveness.py --list --json` exited 2 with `unstamped=11 registry_live=27`, and **9 of the 11 were managers, bots and the operator's own sessions** — started by hand, holding no ledger record, never loading this plugin, and so unable to stamp by construction. A precondition that can never pass is not a precondition, and `approved-not-started.py` rendered `unknown` behind it. **Operator ruling 2026-10-09: narrow the guard to the spawn ledger's workers.** After the change the same call reports `unstamped=1 ledger_workers=15 registry_live=28` — and that one holdout is the same session the `#223` fleet-stamp gate has been measuring, so the guard and the gate now agree on one number instead of disagreeing 11-vs-1. ⚠️ **The ledger reader is EXTRACTED, and the extraction is load-bearing rather than tidiness.** `session-liveness.py` had no ledger reader, while `worker-sessions.py` held the only copy *and* loads `session-liveness.py` through `_load` to reach `read_endpoint()` — so importing it back from inside `unstamped()` would close a cycle the `_load` idiom does not expect. The new `scripts/spawn-ledger.py` is the same move this codebase already made for the registry (`session-identity.py`), and it leaves the dependency direction one-way: both consumers point at it, it points nowhere. ⚠️ **An unreadable ledger is `UNKNOWN`, never a pass** — `{}` ("read it, nobody recorded") and `None` ("could not read it") are kept apart, because folding the second into the first would let a failed read narrow the population to nothing and hand back a passing precondition, the resume-authorising direction. ⚠️ **The residual is carried openly, not hidden.** Narrowing re-opens the hazard the guard was added for, for any live session that is **not** a ledger worker: a manager started by hand holds no ledger record, so a live manager with no endpoint row no longer withholds the list — and `commands/open.md` Step 2C spawns on no match, so that is a duplicate manager onto a live topic. The ruling accepts the trade; Step 2C now records it where it is read, and says the `formerNames` match is what covers the case the guard no longer backstops. ⚠️ **`--coverage` keeps its broader denominator deliberately** — it is a fleet stamping metric, not this precondition, and its `missing` column is unchanged (`endpoint_live=23 registry_live=28 missing=6`). ⚠️ **`spawn-ledger.py` is not yet the only ledger reader, and the others are named rather than implied:** `fleet-sessions.py`, `gate-owner-filter.py`, `stuck-heal.py`, `shutdown-drill.py` and `check-spawn-ledger.py` each keep their own, with their own contract (`fleet-sessions.py` returns `{}` where this returns `None`, and degrades rather than refusing because a roster must still render). Consolidating them is a per-caller behaviour change, not a move. Change set: `scripts/spawn-ledger.py`, `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/worker-sessions.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_sessions.py`, `commands/open.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **`session-liveness.py` answers liveness from attention-controller's session-heartbeat endpoint, and the session registry leaves the liveness path entirely.** The instrument no longer reads `~/.claude/sessions`; it reads `GET /api/1.0/session-heartbeat[/<id>]` and keeps its four-verdict contract unchanged (`LIVE` 0 / `ABSENT` 1 / `UNKNOWN` 2 / `AMBIGUOUS` 3), its prefix semantics and its `--check` / `--list` / `--json` flags. ⚠️ **One channel replaces two, and the composition rule goes with it.** Until now this file composed the registry and the heartbeat store asymmetrically — a positive from either source was an answer, a negative needed both readable — because the registry is pid-keyed and local-only, so it sees every session holding a socket and is blind to one with no process of its own. The endpoint already holds BOTH populations in one store (it serves `source: cluster` and `source: mcp-timer` rows beside local ones), so the composition disappears into the store and this file reads one channel. ⚠️ **UNKNOWN is still licensed by "cannot tell", never by absence** — an unreachable endpoint, or any status that is neither 200 nor 404, is UNKNOWN; only a readable endpoint that positively reports the id not-live licenses ABSENT, which is the one answer that permits a caller to resume onto a live conversation. ⚠️ **`--check` reads the list route FIRST**, because an 8-char prefix is a legal argument this instrument's own callers produce and the per-id route is an exact lookup that cannot answer one (measured 2026-10-08: `/session-heartbeat/30fae0ae` → 404 for a live session); the per-id route survives only as a confirmation on a list miss, so a live prefix costs one round trip and a would-be ABSENT costs two. ⚠️ **`--heartbeat-dir` and `--ttl` are accepted and IGNORED, with a warning on stderr** — the endpoint computes freshness itself; removing them would break `approved-not-started.py`, which still passes one, and accepting them in silence would let a caller believe a window is in force when it is not. ⚠️ **`read_heartbeats` is removed rather than moved** — the extraction entry below kept it in this file; this change retires it — because `worker-sessions.py` and `adopt-orphans.py` read the store through `live-workers.py` directly, so each store still has exactly one reader. `--list` joins the endpoint's live rows to identity from `session-identity.py`, so the name→id join `/supervisor:open` and `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` resolve a roster row by survives the switch. ⚠️ **Prose is NOT yet updated and is named rather than left to be discovered:** `commands/open.md`, `manager-loop.md`, `manager-status.md`, `manager-drive.md`, `fleet-status.md`, `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` and the `cluster-heartbeat.py` docstring still describe liveness as *"the registry AND the heartbeat store"*, and no check validates that prose — it reads green while being wrong, so it is a follow-up rather than a silent omission. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/endpoint_fixture.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_sessions.py`, `scripts/tests/test_approved_not_started.py`, `scripts/worker-sessions.py`, `scripts/adopt-orphans.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- fix: **`session-liveness.py` restores the cluster-reachability signal, measures the `ABSENT` coverage precondition, and stops paying a second round trip for a prefix.** A not-live row whose `source` is `cluster` is `UNKNOWN` again while the local reachability marker (`_cluster-reachability.json`, read through `live-workers.py`) is stale — the endpoint's `state` is the session's ACTIVITY, never reachability, so the marker is the only place that fact lives, and folding it into `ABSENT` authorised a resume onto a cluster worker alive behind a network fault. `--check` now fetches the list route FIRST, so a prefix costs one round trip rather than the old 404-then-list pair, and only a would-be `ABSENT` pays for a per-id confirmation. A new `--coverage` mode counts endpoint-live against registry-live sessions and exits non-zero while a registry-live session has no live endpoint row, so the precondition `ABSENT` rests on is measured rather than asserted. The empty-endpoint case is now decided before the identity registry is read, so a readable-but-empty store no longer reads as `UNKNOWN` when the registry is unreadable.

- fix: **`session-liveness.py` can no longer answer `ABSENT` by crashing, and neither `--list` path presents a partial rollout as a complete fleet.** `check()` is now guarded end to end, so an unexpected exception becomes `UNKNOWN` (exit 2) instead of escaping `main()` as exit 1 — this file's `ABSENT` code, which every caller reads as "not live, resume is authorised"; a row whose `session_id` is not a string, and an unimportable `live-workers.py` on the cluster-reachability path, each return `UNKNOWN` on their own terms. ⚠️ **The empty case was only half the hazard, and the smaller half.** A NON-empty `--list` is the more dangerous one to hand back unqualified: during a partial rollout the endpoint holds a row only for already-stamped sessions, so live-but-unstamped sessions were dropped while the command still exited 0 — and `commands/open.md` Step 2C matches `<topic> Manager` against `name`/`formerNames` from exactly that output and spawns on no match, which is a duplicate manager onto a live topic, the 2026-09-18 failure the names rule exists to prevent reached by a different route. Both paths now pay one precondition, and `commands/open.md` Step 2C is told to read a non-zero exit as *"cannot tell"* and refuse rather than fall through to spawning. ⚠️ **The precondition is `unstamped()`, NOT the `--coverage` figure, and the difference is load-bearing.** `coverage()`'s `missing` is `registry_live - endpoint_live`, which folds two unlike facts together: a rollout gap, and a registered session whose row reads `live: false` — a dead or hung session the endpoint can see and is answering about. Measuring with it refused an ordinary steady state, and `--list`'s own suite caught the regression (`test_list_shows_only_live_sessions` plants exactly that shape and expects the live session listed). `unstamped()` asks the narrower question the precondition actually needs: does every registry-live session have a ROW at the endpoint, live or not. `--coverage` keeps its broader definition as the fleet stamping metric, deliberately. ⚠️ **Both measurements are now reached through `safe_coverage()` / `safe_unstamped()`** — `coverage()` reaches the registry through a lazily `exec_module`d sibling with no guard, so a missing `session-identity.py` raised straight out of `main()` as exit 1, the same defect class the `check()` guard closes; a diagnostic that cannot be taken is `UNKNOWN`, never a crash wearing `ABSENT`'s exit code. Change set: `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `commands/open.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- feat: **`worker-sessions.py` answers liveness from the session-heartbeat endpoint, so the count of live worker sessions stops depending on the session registry.** The fleet's spawn-cap instrument no longer reads `~/.claude/sessions`; it reads `GET /api/1.0/session-heartbeat` through `session-liveness.py`'s `read_endpoint()` and keeps its unit — the **spawn ledger joined to live**, which is what makes it a count of *workers* rather than of every session on the machine. Operator ruling 2026-10-09: keep the join, move only the liveness source. ⚠️ **The number goes UP, and that is the fix rather than a side effect.** The registry is pid-keyed and local-only, so it is structurally blind to a worker with no pid of its own — a headless worker is an in-process SDK `query()` inside the server, a cluster worker is a process on another machine — while the endpoint holds both populations in one store, serving `source: cluster` and `source: mcp-timer` rows beside local ones. Measured on this host 2026-10-09: **11** under the registry join, **23** under the endpoint. `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 5 already carries the warning: re-read `spawn.maxConcurrent` before the first sweep after upgrading, because the first tick can read **at or over** the target with no error on either side. ⚠️ **`--dir` and `--heartbeat-dir` are accepted and IGNORED, with a warning on stderr** — callers pass them, so removing the flags would break those callers, while obeying them in silence would let a reader believe a path or a window is in force when neither is. ⚠️ **A row is live only on an explicit `live: true`** — a renamed or absent key is never read as liveness, and a malformed row is skipped rather than allowed to crash a count whose non-zero exit is how a caller learns the fleet could not be read. ⚠️ **The mjs twin `server/worker-sessions.mjs` still reads the registry** and is NOT moved here: the server has no endpoint reader at all, so one has to be written first; the three prose sites that name this counter as a raw-stamp reader (`scripts/live-workers.py:146`, `server/heartbeat.mjs:108`) move with it. Change set: `scripts/worker-sessions.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_sessions.py`, `scripts/tests/endpoint_fixture.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.124.3

- fix: **the pane-title liveness clause now matches a normalized task name, so it stops missing the tabs it exists to withhold.** The clause required the literal `⚙ <task name>` key, but the gear is set by the **caller's `-n` argument**, not by the spawn mechanism: `server/supervisor.mjs` passes `` `⚙ ${label}` `` at spawn, while path B (`commands/open.md`) is a raw `wezterm cli spawn … --resume <id> -n "<title>"` — and `-n` is what writes the registry `name` — so a revive is geared **iff its caller passed a geared title**, and a direct `wezterm cli spawn` is gearless. ⚠️ **The clause's own cited occurrence was gearless** — the 2026-09-18 rescue matched on `✳ Point the Notification Telegram Bot Token at @bens_notify_bot`, which the `⚙ ` key would not have matched — and a 2026-10-09 census read **13 of 36 live panes** carrying no `⚙ `, including every manager session's tab and three revive panes. ⚠️ **The fix is normalized equality, not a widened substring**, which is what the clause's own prohibition actually forbids: the match now strips a leading decoration run (`^[^\w(]+` — the same strip `_norm_session_name` applies in `scripts/fleet-board.py`, mirrored in `scripts/resolve-task-file.py` and `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`), collapses whitespace, casefolds, and tolerates the trailing ` (N)` that `uniqueTabName` adds, so `✳ <task name>`, `⚙ <task name>` and `⚙ <task name> (2)` all match while a stale or unrelated title still cannot withhold a row. ⚠️ **Repairing the spawn path instead was considered and rejected, and the code is why:** `-n` writes the registry `name`, which is the join key for every roster↔registry read (`findRegisteredByName("⚙ " + label)`, `session-liveness.py --list`), so repairing a *liveness read* by rewriting *session identity* is the wrong direction; it would also fix only the revive path, leaving manager tabs, direct `wezterm cli spawn`s and the gearless panes already live unmatched; and every name-join consumer **strips** the gear rather than reading it as a supervisor marker, so the premise that the gear is load-bearing does not hold. All three clause homes change together — `agents/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, and the vault's `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md`, the last of which is **out-of-repo by construction** and recorded here rather than checked, in the shape the v0.122.2 entry uses. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.124.2

- fix: **a dead session is no longer reported as a gate, and the close line is no longer emitted for a session that has already closed clean — two manager surfaces that disagreed about the same row in one tick.** Measured 2026-10-09 on the `vault-ui-ultra-fast-reads-and-writes` tracked set: a completed row whose session had closed clean at `07:39:39.925Z` was handed a runnable `✅ DONE — CLOSE:` line at `07:43:21Z` — **void at BIRTH, not decayed**, four minutes after the close it asked for — while the same tick's tracked-set watcher counted a *different* dead session in its gated set. Both halves ship here. ⚠️ **`is_gated` now settles the dead session BEFORE its `stuck` limb, and that ordering is the whole watcher-side fix:** the limb ran first, so a session with no registry entry **and** no fresh heartbeat — a worker that is simply gone — read `(True, "stuck:<cause>")` and entered the GATED set whenever its frozen transcript tail still carried a `stream-closed` marker. That is a gate nothing can answer: the row is offered to the operator, the heal ladder is told a headless resume is owed, and there is no session left to resume. Reproduced live as `GATED e4fd1919 [stuck:stream-closed]` against `gated: 1  held: 13`, while the drive leg had already reported of that same session that *"no session remains to receive it"*. ⚠️ **The discriminator is the HEARTBEAT, never the registry alone, and that is what keeps the limb alive** — a headless worker is not in the registry *by construction*, so gating `stuck:` on registry presence would delete the reason it exists. A live headless worker re-stamps every `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS` (30 s) against a `HEARTBEAT_TTL_MS` (60 s) read, so it always holds a fresh stamp; both intended readings survive unchanged (`stuck:` for headless, `stuck-tab:` for registered), each pinned by its own positive control so a fix that deletes the limb outright fails. ⚠️ **One test's fixture was the shape it claimed not to be:** `test_stuck_fixture_without_the_registry_entry_is_still_stuck` asserted the *headless* reading while omitting the heartbeat a headless worker always has — i.e. it pinned the dead shape as `stuck:`, which is how the defect survived a suite that already covered this ground. The narrowing this accepts is stated in the code rather than left to be discovered: on a host whose heartbeat store is unreadable, a stuck headless worker now reads `unregistered` (HELD) instead of gated, which is the safe direction and the same narrowing `heartbeat_live` already documents. ⚠️ **`close-line.py` gains the caller's second verdict, `--closer`.** The registry answers *is the process alive*; it cannot answer *has the turn finished*, and a session that has run its own close stays live for as long as its process holds the socket. `--closer closed` renders the candidate even on a confirmed-live verdict; `open`, `unreadable` and **omitted** all leave the liveness verdict deciding — so a caller that has not adopted the second read is unaffected, and a failed read never suppresses a notice (the rule `agents/manager-drive.md` already states for its own reap). The closed case carries its own candidate wording — *"session already closed clean — the close it asks for has run"* — because the liveness there is CONFIRMED and reusing `liveness UNVERIFIED` would be a false statement about the probe. `commands/manager-loop.md`'s close-me clause takes the read (`reap-closer.py` on the row's FULL id, never a prefix) and passes the flag, and its note now records **both** failure directions: a line that decays after emission is settled by the reader's re-check command, a line void at birth by not emitting it at all. Change set: `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/close-line.py`, `scripts/tests/test_stuck.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_line.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.124.1

- fix: **the sweep's `ready-to-start` offer and its `orphaned` probe now key on the phase and the id set, so an approved row is offered whether or not `vault-cli task approve` rewrote its `status`.** `approve` is a PHASE move (`todo → planning`) and writes **no `status` key at all** from vault-cli's next release — an approved row keeps whatever status it had: `next` when it was filed queued, `in_progress` when it had already been promoted. Both must reach the offer, or removing that write silently removes **every** approved row from the spawn offer, which is the bug spec 058 was written to fix. ⚠️ **The two halves are one change and neither is safe alone.** `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`'s ready-to-start bullet now records the primary vault's corrected clause — it admits `status: next` **or** `status: in_progress` at `phase: planning` — in place of the *"not yet ported to the primary copy"* note that had been stale since 2026-09-29, and `commands/manager-loop.md`'s orphan probe gains the id-set half: an approved, never-started row carries **no id at all**, so the probe finds *nothing*, and *nothing* is not *a session that died*. Without that guard the row is offered as `ready-to-start` **and** printed `ORPHANED` in the same tick, with a resume it cannot support — the regression recorded in [[Approving a Task Sets status in_progress, So Every Approved-But-Unstarted Row Renders as a Problem in the Manager Sweep]]. ⚠️ **`phase: planning` is deliberately the narrow arm, and `status: next` is deliberately kept.** An ownerless `in_progress` row at `phase: execution` is one whose worker died mid-work and belongs to the orphan path, not the offer; and a `phase`-only predicate would drop the 135 rows the primary vault carries with `next` and no `phase` key at all. The vault-local gate (`.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py`) carries the same predicate, pinned in `sweep-gate-fixture.py` with the `planning` positive plus both controls.

## v0.124.0

- feat: **a Claude session can send a follow-up to a cluster worker, so `spawn_agent(target:"cluster")` is no longer one-shot.** The cluster path minted a fresh session id on every call (`server/supervisor.mjs`), so a cluster worker could be started and never spoken to again — measured 2026-10-09: a spawn returned session `6bc052b0`, answered once, and there was no route to a second turn. `send_agent_message` refused it by construction, because its guard read `agent.status !== 'interactive' || !agent.paneId`, and a cluster record is written with `paneId: null` — so *"does this have a pane?"* wore a general guard's clothes and rejected the one worker kind it was never written for. The tool now branches on the worker's own record: a **tab** worker is typed into its pane as before, a **cluster** worker gets one more turn posted on the session id it already holds, and a **headless** worker is still refused — with a refusal that names the channel which *does* reach it (`answer_permission`, or `SendMessage` mid-task). The decision is a new pure module, `server/message-channel.mjs`, with `server/message-channel.test.mjs` (7 cases), because what has to be pinned is the refusal's **reason**, not the boolean: the old guard's failure mode was refusing a reachable worker while reading as a general check. ⚠️ **The bearer token is resolved inside the server on every call and never cached on the agent record** — a value cached at spawn would keep presenting a token the operator has since rotated — and no refusal on that path echoes it. ⚠️ **A 2xx is not evidence the SAME conversation ran, and the tool description says so.** The service takes the session id from the caller and echoes none back, so a turn against an id the pod no longer holds is indistinguishable from this side from a real continuation, while looking exactly like a successful send; the proof is the pod's own `turn start id=<id>` / `turn end id=<id>` pair, which is the caller's to read — the discipline `cluster-spawn.mjs` already states for a spawn, carried to every later turn. ⚠️ **A cluster worker's `result` is its LAST finished turn, and a follow-up overwrites it on the agent record and in the ledger**, so `agent_status`, `list_agents` and a peer manager reading the ledger cannot disagree about which turn they are looking at — unlike a local worker, whose result is written once at turn end. ⚠️ **`spawn_agent(resume)` with `target:"cluster"` is still refused, and its refusal now names `send_agent_message`** — the old wording left the caller with no route at all, which is how this gap survived a spawn, a proof and a goal Success Criterion without anyone being told where to go. Two blocks that would otherwise have been duplicated the moment a second caller appeared moved to one home: `clusterTarget()` (the target every cluster call resolves, so a spawn and a follow-up cannot address different services) and `clusterTurnResult()` (the answer/outcome/truncated shape, so they cannot report one turn two ways). Docs reconciled in lockstep: `README.md` § Tools + § Configuration, `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker item 7 + § Adopting an existing session, `CLAUDE.md` § Reaching a running tab worker + § Status, `llms.txt`, `skills/supervising-workers/SKILL.md`. Change set: `server/message-channel.mjs`, `server/message-channel.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `llms.txt`, `skills/supervising-workers/SKILL.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.123.2

- fix: **the server's Claude home comes from the supervisor's own variable, and an inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` that disagrees with it now refuses the boot instead of being adopted silently.** `server/config.mjs:29` read `const CLAUDE_HOME = ENV.CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR || join(homedir(), '.claude')`, so a server started from a parent carrying a different config dir resolved its session registry (`sessionsDir`) and transcript root (`projectsDir`) there — and both halves of that failure are silent. Measured **2026-10-09**: `cc-private` handed `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=~/.claude-verify` (the openbrain scenario test dir) to the server, whose `spawn_agent` then returned a `transcript_dir` under `~/.claude-verify/projects` with `session_id` and `bind` **null** — it looked for the registration in a `~/.claude-verify/sessions` that held nothing — so every worker it spawned was invisible to attention-controller, `list_agents` and Vault UI, and unreachable by `SendMessage`. Nothing errored: the server ran and reported success while the fleet saw nothing. ⚠️ **The healthy case is the pinned one, and it must not refuse.** The `cc-*` launchers export `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR="$HOME/.claude"` (bborbe/scripts `69d41cc`), which equals the default; the comparison runs after `resolve()`, so a trailing slash is the same home, and the guard fires only when the inherited value genuinely differs — a guard that fired on spelling would refuse every correctly-pinned server, which is an outage rather than a fix. ⚠️ **`SUPERVISOR_CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is the deliberate opt-in, and the refusal names it:** the inherited variable is never the signal that a non-default home was intended, so an operator who wants one declares it there, and declaring it settles an inherited variable too. ⚠️ **The refusal is surfaced rather than thrown from `config.mjs`**, so the configuration surface stays enumerable and testable — the module's stated purpose — and `server/supervisor.mjs` is what acts on it, exiting non-zero on stderr before the transport exists. Change set: `server/config.mjs`, `server/config.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.123.1
- fix: **the `parked-on-unregistered-gate` cell's staleness threshold is derived from the cadence the manager armed, instead of a fixed 30 minutes every copy of the rule described as `2 × interval`.** The cell's first discriminator branch reads a threshold, and until now every carrier *described* it as `2 × the cadence interval` while the value actually used was a constant: `.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py`'s `STUCK_SECONDS = 30 * 60` — the executable that decides the wake — the reader's `working (file)` branch, `commands/manager-loop.md`'s own step-4 discriminator, and the vault's `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Step 4. The two definitions agree **only** at the 15-minute default, so the defect was invisible there and scaled with the tick. Measured **2026-10-07** on the manager loop for *Attention Controller Ultra-Fast Reads*, running `--interval 60m`: a **66-minute-old** row rendered `⌛ parked-on-unregistered-gate` — correct per the executable, wrong per the rule, which promises **120** minutes at that tick — and the manager reported it to the operator as a false fire before establishing which half was wrong. ⚠️ **The gate now reads the cadence the manager actually armed**, from `<slug>.cadence` in `~/.claude/state/sweep-gate` — written by `manager-liveness.py --arm --interval` at every re-arm, and resolved through the **same `STATE_DIR` and the same `slug()`** `sweep-gate.py` already uses, so nothing new crosses a repo boundary. ⚠️ **The threshold is park-only, and that is load-bearing:** `STUCK_SECONDS` is untouched and still feeds the `stuck` bucket, because `stuck` **is** a `digest_of` input — deriving it would move the digest and break the lockstep with `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, which therefore needs no matching edit and the digest stays byte-identical. ⚠️ **The fallback is announced, never silent** — with no cadence record the threshold reverts to `STUCK_SECONDS` and the gate prints the basis to **stderr**, deliberately not stdout, whose `CHANGE` lines drive the Monitor and would wake the manager every tick. ⚠️ **The reader half is the same input at the dispatch sites:** the cadence becomes declared input **14** (`none` when the caller holds no tick, rendering the cell **UNVERIFIED** rather than assuming a default), passed by `commands/manager-loop.md` step 4 as `2 ×` the clamped interval and as `none` by `commands/manager-status.md` and `commands/manager-drive.md`, which hold no tick. ⚠️ **The vault-local half ships separately:** `.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py` (with its `sweep-gate-fixture.py` controls) and `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` are git-tracked in `private-personal`, not here, so this PR carries the four plugin surfaces only. Change set: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.123.0

- feat: `spawn_agent` honours a `launcher:` frontmatter field on the task, else its goal, else the vault `claude_script` (bare name resolves beside the vault launcher; conflicting goals and unreadable frontmatter are refused); response adds `launcher_source` (`task`/`goal`/`vault`/`override`); `/supervisor:open` resume path applies the same rule

## v0.122.8

- docs: **the rendered-snapshot roster join has one home, and it now says the name is `⚙ <label>` — not the task title — and that a task-file rename leaves it stale.** Five surfaces stated this one contract and two of them contradicted each other on the same caller-passed roster, and a sixth — `commands/fleet-status.md`, whose roster bullet read *"The name is the task the session is on"* with none of the caveats — stated it flatly: `agents/manager-drive.md`'s *not on roster* bullet read *"the name match **is** the join — and it is the one that misses"*, while `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 5 read *"Join on the name here"* because *"the session's name is the task's title, which is what `/rename` sets"* — and that same reader file restated the **retired**, shape-blind rule (*"the roster… carries **no id column**, so the key is the **name**"*) fifty-nine lines below the rule that retired it. ⚠️ **Both named clauses were wrong, in different ways, and a live read is what settled it.** The registry name is neither the bare title (the reader's claim) nor a plain truncation of one (the drive leg's): `server/supervisor.mjs` sets it to `` `⚙ ${label}` `` at spawn, and `uniqueTabName` (`server/liveness.mjs`) appends ` (2)`, ` (3)` … on collision — so the join is **normalized, not equality**, through the shared `_norm_session_name` (`scripts/fleet-board.py`, mirrored in `scripts/resolve-task-file.py` and `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`), whose decoration strip is `^[^\w(]+` — **leading only**, so a **collision-suffixed** name (`⚙ <title> (2)`) matches nothing. The ` (N)` tolerance exists in the *tab-title* clauses and was never carried into any join. ⚠️ **And a task-file rename does not rewrite the name — it goes STALE.** Measured **2026-10-09** on this change's own anchor task: `notesmd-cli move` renamed the file (exit 0) while `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` kept the old `name` **and** `nameSource: user` verbatim — so after any rename the join reports a **live** session's task as an orphan candidate, the same artifact class as the caller's-own-session case step 5 already documents. ⚠️ **The remedy is single-home-plus-pointer, not a guard** — the v0.119.1 precedent, and no `check-*.py` is added. `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 5's rendered-snapshot bullet owns the rule; `agents/manager-drive.md`'s *not on roster* bullet, `commands/manager-loop.md`'s orphan-candidate bullet, `commands/fleet-status.md`'s roster bullet and `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § The sweep → *Auto-resume gate* condition 6 each now **point at it** rather than restating it. The runbook half is **out-of-repo by construction** — it lives in the vault, so nothing in this change set can pin it, and it is recorded here rather than checked, in the shape the v0.122.2 entry uses. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/fleet-status.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.122.7

- fix: **the in-flight check's limb 2 reads a marker a hook writes again, and the drop line names the limb that answered.** `~/.claude/hooks/attention-log.py` had written no `<sid>.tool.json` since it was rewritten as an open-close event log on 2026-09-21, so limb 2 of `agents/manager-drive.md`'s check 2 answered nothing for seventeen days — the file was simply absent, and the limb's absent branch reads absence as *between turns*, which made *"no marker because the session is between turns"* and *"no marker because the writer is gone"* the same observation. The hook writes it again: `PreToolUse` writes `state: "open"`, `PostToolUse` removes the file, and `SessionEnd` clears whatever a session left behind. This restores the one signal limb 1 cannot give — a `busy` session executing a tool and one parked on a prompt are indistinguishable by transcript, and the registry reports both as `busy`. ⚠️ **The marker's `detail` is scrubbed where the retired writer's was not.** `tool_detail()` returns the raw `command`, and the four markers the old writer left on disk carried full shell command lines verbatim; the new writer applies `redact()` **plus** `_ASSIGN_SECRET`, because `redact()` alone was measured passing `export TOKEN=…` straight through — its `_SECRET` matches header-shaped names only. ⚠️ **The marker is cleared only by the tool that owns it.** One assistant message can carry two `tool_use` blocks, so an unconditional clear would delete the marker of the call still running, and limb 2 would then read *between turns* for a session that is demonstrably executing — the silent direction the marker exists to close. ⚠️ **An `in flight:` drop now names the limb** — `limb 1 (registry: shell)`, `limb 2 (tool.json state=open 4 min, Bash)`, `limb 3 (transcript 2 min)` — rather than only the value it read. The value alone cannot show that a limb has gone silent: for the seventeen days limb 2 was dead, every drop still read `in flight: <a transcript age>`, which is indistinguishable from a working check, so the defect was invisible in the report and visible only in the source. ⚠️ **A second live consumer revives with the same writer, and it was dead for the same seventeen days.** `scripts/who-needs-me.py`'s **Probably stuck** section is sourced solely from `load("tool")` (`:1767`), which globs the same `<sid>.tool.json` store and filters on `live(r)`; `:1783` renders a row only when `time.time() - r["ts"] > a.stuck_min * 60`. With no writer that section was structurally empty for every session started after 2026-09-21, and its `:15` header docstring named a file nothing wrote. It revives with the writer and needs no edit — the docstring becomes true again, which is why it is recorded as unchanged rather than corrected. ⚠️ **Its rendered rows change text, and that is the one operator-visible consequence of the scrub.** `who-needs-me.py:1817` prints `r["detail"]` directly on each stuck row, so an operator reading that section now sees a redacted command line where the retired writer would have shown it verbatim. Nothing in-repo assumes an unscrubbed value — `fleet-board.py:700` reads only `session_id`/`ts` and `context-usage.py:124` only `pane` — so this is a change of display, not a contract. ⚠️ **`65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Step 5 carries the same clause — including the limb-naming requirement itself, verified in place, not merely the pre-existing marker description** — because that runbook wins over this agent file on disagreement. That half is **out-of-repo by construction**: the runbook is not in this change set and `scripts/check-inflight-limb.py` pins `agents/manager-drive.md` only, so nothing here can see it. Recorded rather than checked, in the shape the v0.122.2 entry uses for the vault-local `verify-topic` divergence. If § Step 5 ever loses the naming rule, the runbook wins and the drop lines go back to being unnamed with no in-repo signal. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py` (the last four curing a stale `manager-drive.md:132` citation that four sites carried for a sentence at `:134`), `scripts/check-inflight-limb.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_inflight_limb.py`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md` — plus the hook half at `~/.claude/hooks/attention-log.py`, which ships in place with no PR path of its own.

## v0.122.6

- fix: **a member goal renders as its own row unqualified, so the sweep's goal-row set is single-valued.** `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` carried the counter-reading — *"a goal row is structural, derived from its tasks' placement"* — and no unqualified render clause at all, so a member goal owning **0** tracked tasks had a defensible reading on each side: render it (the runbook's *"renders as its own row"* is unqualified) or drop it (the agent's placement-derivation). Measured 2026-09-26 on `23 Topics/Unattended Execution.md`: `[[An Observed Defect Reaches human_review Without Me]]`, declared in `## Goals` and owning **0** tracked tasks, was **rendered** as a row (`0/5`) at 17:43 and **omitted** at 17:10, 17:26, 18:17 and 18:33 — one topic, unchanged files, two goal-row sets tick to tick. ⚠️ **The clause is now stated once in this repository and once in the runbook** — *"a member goal renders as its own row even with 0 tracked tasks"* — in `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` (step 7's frame rules, a `<success_criteria>` bullet, the optional-split paragraph that carried the counter-reading, and **input 8's contract widened** from step-8-only to the two steps that now consume it) and in `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Sweep output, with the same clause mirrored in that file's § The sweep and § Command rationale. ⚠️ **The runbook is NOT in this repository** — it lives in `bborbe/obsidian-personal`, the same out-of-tree copy the v0.122.5 entry names, and carries the same clause; this diff reaches the agent alone. ⚠️ **The section/existence split is stated rather than left to inference:** a goal row's *section* follows its tasks' placement — a required goal owning an optional task renders in **both** boxes, unchanged — while the row's *existence* follows only the topic's `## Goals` declaration, so no member goal's row is dropped for want of tasks beneath it. ⚠️ **The rule now has four carriers in one file, so a drift guard joins them:** `scripts/check-goal-row-clause.py`, wired into `make precommit`, asserts that **no fewer than four** statements of the clause survive — matched by pattern rather than by an exact string, because the input-8 contract lowercases the subject and the optional-split paragraph bolds the zero, so an exact count sees only **two** of the four and passes a tree that deleted the clause from either of the others. ⚠️ **That is a floor, not parity** — it is a total hit count, so deleting one carrier and adding an incidental mention elsewhere still reads four; the module docstring states that limit rather than the stronger claim the count reads like. It also asserts that the goal branch's exemption from the absent-input rule is still stated, and that none of the three retracted readings reappears across `agents/`, `commands/` and `docs/`. It is a **drift guard, not a semantics check** — the review round that produced it is the evidence that no grep catches four carriers of one rule disagreeing, which is why the guard is scoped to what a grep can honestly prove. `scripts/tests/test_check_goal_row_clause.py` exercises every branch — a dropped carrier, the missing goal-branch exemption, a missing carrier file, each of the three retracted phrases in each scanned directory, and the stated out-of-scope boundary — as every sibling guard's test does. Change set: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/check-goal-row-clause.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_goal_row_clause.py`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.122.5

- fix: **`checkbox_count()` now excludes a struck row, so the sweep's `Met` cell returns one answer per file.** Both rule carriers state the unit as *"TOTAL checkboxes (SC + DoD + Tasks, **struck rows excluded**)"* — `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Sweep output and `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` — and no producer excluded anything: `_CHECKBOX` matched `- [ ]` / `- [x]` / `- [/]` regardless of `~~`, so a struck row — `- [ ] ~~[[Task]]~~`, the goal page's convention for a task deliberately removed from the tracked set — was counted as an **outstanding** subtask, in the direction that reports work where there is none. Measured 2026-09-24 on `24 Goals/a separate task.md`: **10 rows = 7 ticked + 3 struck**, so a literal count scored **7/10** where the truth is **7/7**. ⚠️ **The anchor is `^\s*-\s*\[ \]\s*~~\[\[[^\]]+\]\]~~` — trailing prose allowed, box UNTICKED — and both halves of that are measured rather than assumed** (2026-10-08, whole vault): of **33** struck-wikilink rows, **0** are the bare whole-row `- [ ] ~~[[Task]]~~` the rule's own example shows, **10** are `- [ ] ~~[[Task]]~~ — <reason>`, and **23** are `- [x] ~~[[Task]]~~ — <reason>`. An anchor requiring the whole row matches **nothing**, so the bug survives it untouched; requiring `[ ]` is what keeps those **23 ticked** struck rows counted, because a struck row that was already ticked is a met box and dropping it would hide completed work. **The exclusion is never keyed on the presence of `~~`.** A ticked criterion carrying inline strikethrough over a clause that was later narrowed is still a live, met box: an exclusion keyed on `~~` anywhere in the line dropped a ticked `- [x]` and reported `13/14` where the truth was **14/15** — off by one, in the direction that hides completed work (measured 2026-09-22). `checkbox_count` filters line-wise rather than `findall`-ing the tick characters, which had discarded the row shape it needs to test. ⚠️ **In THIS repository the function has exactly ONE call site** — `read_task` → `met` — so this diff reaches the task row's total and nothing else. The goal row's `# Success Criteria` and the topic row's `SC` / `Gate` halves are computed by the vault-side twin `.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py`, which is **not in this repository** (it lives in `bborbe/obsidian-personal`, the same out-of-tree copy the note further down this file names) and carries the same change. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py` gains `TestCheckboxCountStruckRows` — **7** cases pinning both measured fixtures, the two shapes the vault actually emits, the section-scoped branch and the deliberate `[/]` choice, each verified to FAIL against the implementation it exists to catch (the whole-row anchor yields `1/2`; a `~~`-keyed exclusion yields `13/14`), where the file previously had no direct coverage of `checkbox_count` at all. ⚠️ **The in-repo rule carrier moves in the same change** — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md:457-458` still specified the whole-row shape the code deliberately abandoned, which is the same producer/carrier divergence this entry exists to remove; leaving it stale would have re-created it one file over. Lines are split on a newline only, never `str.splitlines()`, which also breaks on separators `re.M` does not anchor on. The four `Met`-variance task files are unchanged at `17/17 · 15/15 · 13/13 · 10/10`, so no pinned value moves. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.122.4

- fix: **a cluster spawn now returns the child's own answer instead of discarding it, so a turn that failed for a stated reason no longer reads as a turn that did nothing.** `server/cluster-spawn.mjs` has returned the pod's `text/plain` answer as `answer` since the cluster target landed, and the call site in `server/supervisor.mjs` used `started.sessionId` alone — so the answer was dropped, the agent record was seeded `result: null, error: null`, and every cluster turn was read from the outside as an empty one. Measured cost: three cluster runs on 2026-10-08 (sessions `9fac5053`, `38a2bdae`, `a3be5a1b`) each returned a complete answer — one of them a **success**, whose write landed inside the pod — and all three were reported as *"a clean turn end with no effect"*; naming the real cause took a hand-built probe against `/prompt`, because the ordinary path shows nothing. ⚠️ **A failed turn and an empty one are separated by a new `outcome`, because the pod answers both with a 200 carrying text.** `classifyClusterAnswer` reads the child-side convention observed on the deployed pod — one JSON object, `{"status":"failed","message":"…"}` or `{"status":"success"}` — and returns `failed` / `succeeded` / `answered` / `empty`. The parse is deliberately NARROW (a parsed OBJECT carrying a string `status`, nothing else) and the raw answer is ALWAYS returned beside the verdict, so a child answering in prose, or in some other JSON shape, is reported `answered` and none of its text is reinterpreted; `{"error":"boom"}`, `[{"status":"failed"}]` and `"the build failed"` are all `answered`. ⚠️ **The value reaches all three surfaces, and that is a decision rather than a default.** The cluster turn is SYNCHRONOUS — `startClusterSession` holds the request open until the pod answers — so by the time `spawn_agent` returns there is a final answer and no later one: the tool result is read by the caller NOW, `agent_status` / `list_agents` by the manager later, and the ledger by a peer manager after this server has exited, so leaving any of them null puts three reads of one finished turn in disagreement. `answer` is bounded at `CLUSTER_ANSWER_MAX` (**4000**, the same bound `agent-loop.mjs` applies to an SDK result) and carries a `truncated` flag rather than being cut silently. ⚠️ **The record's shape is deliberately NOT the SDK envelope** — `subtype`, `is_error` and `num_turns` are facts about an SDK query and a cluster turn has none of them, so borrowing the shape would put three invented values into a field readers already trust; the record's `mode: "cluster"` is what tells the two apart. `buildRecord` gains a `result` parameter (default `null`, so every local mode is byte-identical) because hard-coding `null` there discarded the answer from the durable half as well. ⚠️ **The answer also rides out on the task-binding failure path** — the turn ran, and its answer is the one thing that cannot be recovered from the session id, so dropping it there would reintroduce the same silence on the rarer of the two paths and therefore the one nobody would notice. Tests: `server/cluster-spawn.test.mjs` 30 → **37**, including a wiring assertion against `supervisor.mjs`'s own text — it starts an MCP server at import, so it cannot be imported behaviourally, the remedy this repo already uses in `attention-poll.test.mjs` — pinning that `started.answer` is read and reaches both surfaces; the whole server suite reads **485** on this branch. Change set: `server/cluster-spawn.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/ledger.mjs`, `server/cluster-spawn.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.122.3
