- refactor: **the session registry read is extracted into `scripts/session-identity.py`, and `session-liveness.py` imports it instead of globbing the directory a second time.** ⚠️ **Behaviour is unchanged on this branch** — `--check` still composes the registry and the heartbeat store with the same asymmetric rule (a positive from either source is an answer; a negative needs BOTH readable), the four verdicts stay `LIVE` 0 / `ABSENT` 1 / `UNKNOWN` 2 / `AMBIGUOUS` 3, and prefix semantics plus the `--check` / `--list` / `--json` flags are untouched. ⚠️ **The split exists because one function answered two questions.** `read_registry` fed the liveness verdict *and* `--list --json`, which is the fleet's **name→id join** — `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` resolves every roster row by matching `name` against exactly that output, and `commands/open.md` reads `formerNames` / `cwd` / `nameSource` from it — so the registry machinery (`read_registry`, `read_registry_entries`, the `ps` start-time probe and its file cache, `REGISTRY_DIR`) moves verbatim with its measured-fact docstrings intact: the per-pid `ps` cost curve, the pid-reuse window that bounds the cache TTL, and the UTC-vs-local zone split between a record's `procStart` and `ps -o lstart=`. ⚠️ **`read_heartbeats` and `_live_workers` STAY in `session-liveness.py`** — the heartbeat store keeps exactly one reader, `live-workers.py`, and this file is still the caller that wraps it. ⚠️ **Six callers re-point their import and nothing else** — `worker-sessions.py`, `adopt-orphans.py`, `restart-worker.py`, `who-needs-me.py`, `manager-predispatch.py`, `fleet-board.py` — so no verdict, default or code path moves with the import; `scripts/transport-read-check.py`'s `SITE_LIST` entry moves with the `ps` call it names. ⚠️ **The tests move with the code and exist in exactly one place:** `scripts/tests/test_session_identity.py` takes the registry-shape, pid-identity and start-probe cases, and they are REMOVED from `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, which keeps the verdict cases (prefix, ambiguity, the heartbeat composition, `--list`, `--json`, cluster stamps). Change set: `scripts/session-identity.py`, `scripts/session-liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_identity.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_liveness.py`, `scripts/tests/test_worker_sessions.py`, the six callers above, `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.122.2

- feat: **a worker now spawns only into the vault that owns its task, and a task filed in another world is refused rather than spawned.** `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker gains a **`World precondition`** block — its own marker, its own extraction boundary, placed *beneath* the readiness block rather than nested under it — stating the own-vault rule and the mis-filed case, and carrying **no** `world:` frontmatter field: the file's own path already answers which world a task is in, and a second source of truth would drift the moment a task moved. ⚠️ **The rule is a comparison the manager can already make** — the task file's path names its vault and `spawn_agent` requires `vault` or `cwd`, so the spawn's vault is known before the call is made — and the reported incident was a task created and started in the wrong world which therefore had no access to `kubectl staging`. ⚠️ **The mis-filed case is the one a happy-path rule performs rather than prevents:** *"spawn into the owning vault"* alone would run the worker in the owning vault and still leave it unable to reach the resource its task needs, so the block refuses that spawn and hands the task back with the unreachable resource named. ⚠️ **Both refusals are stated as manager-side obligations with no mechanical gate, and that is a placement fact rather than an impossibility:** `spawn_agent`'s resolver (`server/spawn-cwd.mjs`) receives only `vaults`/`vault`/`cwd` and never the task file's path, while the spawn call itself holds the `task` and the resolved `vault` in one frame — so a gate would have to move ahead of the spawn, into that function, which is a different change to a different function. ⚠️ **Both spawn sites now send the reader to § Spawn a worker as a whole rather than to item 8 alone** — `commands/manager-loop.md`'s spawn-mechanics bullet and `commands/open.md`'s `vault`/`cwd` requirement — because item 8 is the `vault`/`cwd` rule and a manager who read only that item never reached the spawn-time preconditions governing which vault a worker may run in. The consumers reference the section and name no marker, because the rule's home is single by construction. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/open.md`.

- fix: **the necessity rule is narrowed to two sources — a goal's one-line summary sentence no longer counts as `needed`.** `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` § step 8 now reads *"serves one of **two sources** of a member goal: a specific Success Criterion (cite `SC<n>`), or a specific Definition of Done item (cite `DoD<n>`)"*, with the sentence explicitly excluded and the reason stated: completion is the closure contract, nothing closes against a sentence, and a sentence is broad by design, so accepting it re-admitted the resemblance link this read exists to catch — a task could read as `needed` by a goal whose criteria it advanced none of. This aligns the supervisor read with vault-cli, which narrowed `verify-goal`, `verify-task`, `goal-manager-agent` and `task-manager-agent` to the same two sources and deleted `parse_goal_sentence` outright; the supervisor read was the last of the texts the rule lives in. The **quoting bar** and the separate **`product` verdict** are deliberately unchanged — neither depends on the retired source, and leaving them byte-identical keeps before/after comparisons of this read valid. The two `not needed` rows now read `checked against {Success Criteria, Definition of Done}`, the `<SC<n>|DoD<n>>` placeholder replaces `<goal sentence|SC<n>|DoD<n>>` in both `needed` rows, and `manager-verify`'s third-shape note drops *"its declared goal's three sources"*. Change set: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `agents/manager-verify.md`, `scripts/check-necessity-templates.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_necessity_templates.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`. ⚠️ **The guard matched by prefix, so it passed unchanged through the narrowing — that gap is now closed rather than documented.** `check-necessity-templates.py` gains a `RETIRED` assertion: the retired `<goal sentence|SC<n>|DoD<n>>` placeholder must not reappear anywhere in the reader. Every other check there matches a row's *prefix*, so none can see a source deleted from *inside* a row, and a uniform re-widening at all three sites would satisfy every count. ⚠️ **And this PR does not narrow `verify-topic`.** That command is **vault-local** (`.claude/commands/verify-topic.md`) — shipped by neither vault-cli nor this repo — and its step 5 still counts the topic's own sentence and a member goal's sentence as sources, so the two disagree until it is narrowed too. Recorded here rather than silently implied, because a reader deciding whether a topic-level link counts would otherwise take this change for system-wide.

## v0.122.1

- fix: **the under-target branch's declined-ask re-post bound is live rather than fail-closed, and two rule-text findings against it are closed.** `scripts/under-target-state.py` now records a **per-row startability snapshot** at post time and gains a `compare` verb that owns the bound's derivation. The bound is a **change**, never a state — *"a row that was UNSTARTABLE in the declined snapshot and can start now"* — so without the bit no row can be shown to have CHANGED and the branch suppressed: the same silence the bound exists to remove, arrived at deliberately. Measured 2026-10-08: the branch fired and withheld its card on five unstartable rows that were a subset of a declined ask. `… write` takes one of `--startable` / `--unstartable` / `--unknown` for **every** row in the set and **refuses to write a state missing one**, because a snapshot without its bits cannot be told from a decision; `… compare` takes this tick's per-row classification and prints the verdict — `0` suppress, `10` re-post naming **every** reason it fired, `3` nothing declined yet — and an **unreadable state file is a refusal** (exit 2) rather than a traceback, because exit 1 is not in that contract and a caller reading only `0` / `3` / `10` would take it for "not suppressed", i.e. post. `read` renders a row carrying **no recorded bit** as `(unrecorded)` rather than `unknown`, and the suppression line says so too — `unknown` re-posts and absence fails closed, so collapsing the two in the render made one file read two ways. ⚠️ **`compare` exists because the two arms a hand-rolled set-difference gets wrong are exactly the subtle ones.** A **blank** audit verdict is **unknown**, and unknown re-posts: *"a verdict other than `✅ Ready for approval`"* is satisfied by a blank verdict, so reading that phrase as the unstartable predicate let a degraded audit read withhold the card — the predicate in `commands/manager-loop.md` now requires the verdict be PRESENT, and the unknown token carries the third state. A row that was unstartable in the snapshot and can start now re-posts **even though every row can start now**, which is the one arm a row set alone cannot answer. ⚠️ **`commands/fleet-loop.md`'s covered-subject claim now names its key form** — `--row '<the subject the invariant named>'`, the slot `skills/asked-ledger/SKILL.md` requires and a `<subject>` is not — and says which askers that key actually joins: the claim refuses a second claimer on the same key, while the live-manager half is closed by the invariant's own zero-card conjunction, since `manager-loop.md`'s under-target branch keys on the **row**, not on the subject. Change set: `scripts/under-target-state.py`, `scripts/tests/test_under_target_state.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/fleet-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. ⚠️ **The rewrite also caught a stale sibling predicate, which is this task's own defect class:** `commands/manager-loop.md`'s claim-release bullet still defined the suppressed tick as *"a subset of the declined set **AND whose ranked rows all still carry a non-`✅ Ready for approval` verdict**"* — the **inverse** of the bullet this PR rewrote, and satisfied by the same blank verdict. It is a pointer now. ⚠️ **And the guard covers the whole file, not its top level:** `isinstance(data, dict)` admits any field *type* inside, so a `startability` that is a list or a string raised out of `was.get(r)` / `was[r]` and a `row_set` that is a string made `set()` iterate its characters — every real row reading as "not in the declined batch". Both fields are type-checked now, and a bit recorded `unknown` that reads startable now gets a suppression line that says so rather than claiming the row is unchanged. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_under_target_state.py` 13 → **32**; the whole suite reads **1917** on this branch (1898 before this change). ⚠️ **Both totals are the runner's own `Ran N tests` line from `python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests`, and they are deliberately not a `def test_` census** — a census reads 1877 here, because the suite's parameterized cases (`subTest` / `load_tests`) count once each at run time and zero times statically. The per-file delta is the check that reconciles: 13 → 32 is +19, and 1898 → 1917 is +19.

## v0.122.0

- feat: **an `asked-of-you` ledger entry now carries the board card its ask lives on, and that card's answer closes the entry without a manager noticing by hand.** `scripts/open-items.py` gains a `card_item_id` field plus the option set the card was built from, a `card-sync` verb that backfills and reconciles, a `card-answer` verb that closes one entry from the board item id, and an `options` verb that repairs an entry predating the link. This is the last hop of `answered-watch.py` → `attention-ask.py poll` → here: the watcher already emitted `ANSWERED <item-id>` for a card this session posted, but nothing mapped that id back to a ledger entry, because the entry had no field to match on — so every `asked-of-you` still needed a manager to notice the answer by hand. ⚠️ **The link is only ever made by a POSTED card, and a card carries at least two options** — the same floor `bborbe/vault-cli`'s `commands/prepare-compact.md` sets for a resume block's `Open decision:` entries, restated here because this is where a card is now built, and a card with one option, or none, is a notification wearing a question's shape and the operator cannot answer it. `add` therefore posts the card when the ask carries an option set, and **warns, never refuses, when it does not**: the ledger records an ask before anything else about it exists, and an entry that vanished because a store was down would invert exactly that reason. Measured 2026-10-08 across the store's 42 ledgers: 230 `asked-of-you` entries, 39 open, and **not one carrying a structured option set** — the options lived inside the free `text`, where nothing could post them. `card-sync` therefore **reports** such an entry rather than inventing an answer space, and `options` is the repair path. ⚠️ **`not-answered` is not `answered`:** a card the store holds a NON-operator answer on renders `NOT_OPERATOR_ANSWERED:`, which reads like a resolved gate, and closing on it would forge the operator attribution `answer` exists to protect — pinned by a discriminating test pair, not by the positive case alone. ⚠️ **Both passes are OPEN-only:** a closed entry's card is history, and re-posting for it would resurrect a question the operator has already answered. ⚠️ **`card-answer` carries the same guard from the other side** — its four close writes are unconditional, so it REFUSES an already-closed entry rather than replacing that entry's `closed_evidence` with a new claim (the hazard `withdraw` refuses on an already-closed entry for, and for the same reason: a later reader cannot tell the replacement from the original). It likewise refuses a card matching more than one entry, which is a ledger defect rather than a caller error. Change set: `scripts/open-items.py`, `scripts/tests/test_open_items.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Tests: `scripts/tests/test_open_items.py` 69 → **84**; the whole suite reads **1898** on this branch (1869 before this change, plus master's 14 from the merge).

## v0.121.0
- feat: **four Claude Code hooks record what a session is doing, so the heartbeat stamp can carry it.** `UserPromptSubmit` -> `busy`, `Stop` -> `idle`, `Notification` -> `waiting-on-operator`, `SessionEnd` -> `clear`, each calling a new `scripts/heartbeat-state.py` that writes a per-session record. The stamp timer answers *is it alive* from a stamp age; it cannot answer *what is it doing*, because it fires on a clock rather than on an event - this is the event half; the stamp half is already merged on master, and it is what carries the value onto a stamp. ⚠️ **The state is passed as an ARGUMENT per manifest entry, not read from the hook payload:** the entry already knows which event it is, so making the script infer it from `hook_event_name` would add a dependency on a field this repo has never verified is present. ⚠️ **The state directory is a SIBLING of the heartbeat store, never inside it** - everything in the store is a stamp, read for its age and listed as a session, so a state file living there would be listed as a session that does not exist. ⚠️ **The id becomes a FILENAME, so it is shape-checked on BOTH the write and the clear path** (`^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$`, refusing bare `.` and `..` explicitly), mirroring the precedent in `resolve-task-file.py` - a `../` component or a leading `/` would otherwise write outside the state directory. ⚠️ **Membership is checked on READ as well as on write**, because the directory is shared: an unrecognised state degrades to `null` rather than reaching a reader as an out-of-set enum. ⚠️ **The hook is fail-open and silent** - it runs inside the operator turn on every prompt and every stop, so every failure path returns 0 having done nothing. ⚠️ **`SessionEnd` clears the record**, because the hook fires for every session on the machine and nothing else unlinks one; a `SIGKILL`ed session still leaves its record behind, which is a known and accepted gap rather than a remedy. ⚠️ **`scripts/tests/test_hooks_manifest.py` pins the event-to-state mapping**, which nothing else reads: swapping `busy`/`idle` between the `UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop` entries would invert what every session reports and still pass the whole suite.
- fix: **review round 1 returned CHANGES_REQUESTED, and the Critical was an artefact of the split: the release notes still documented the server half** (`heartbeatStateDir`, `readState`, JS test-count movements) that this PR had just moved to its own branch. The entries now describe only what this diff ships. ⚠️ **Round 2 corrected an over-correction in that same direction:** round 1 rewrote the notes and the script docstrings to call those mirrors *forward references*, which held only while the two halves were unmerged — `#216` has since landed on master, so `readState` (`server/heartbeat.mjs`) and `heartbeatStateDir` (`server/config.mjs`) are on this tree and the consumer is live; both docstrings now name the real mirror. From the same round: **the temp file carries the PID**, because a per-session name alone is not enough for the atomicity the comment claims — `write_text` truncates before it writes, so two overlapping hooks (`Stop` and `Notification` land together) could publish the second writer's partial record; **a `SUPERVISOR_HEARTBEAT_STATE=off` kill switch** mirrors the sibling hook's, so four `python3` spawns per turn can be stopped without editing a manifest; and three test gaps close — the traversal assertion now lists **every** entry rather than only `.json` ones and covers a leading `/`, the unwritable-directory case uses a chmod-0500 temp dir instead of Linux-only `/proc` (where the path is creatable, so the assertion passed while exercising nothing), and the manifest test asserts the `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/` prefix rather than merely stripping it.

## v0.120.0
- feat: **the heartbeat stamp carries the session's activity, and the supervisor stamps a heartbeat for the session it runs INSIDE.** `stampRecord` accepts `task`, `vault`, `location` and `activity` alongside the existing `sessionId`/`pid`/`mode`/`at`/`source`, and a 30 s timer stamps `mode: 'local'` for this server's own `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` — the interactive tab the server runs in was covered by nothing, so it read dead while a headless worker it had spawned read live, the exact asymmetry the session-liveness work exists to remove. ⚠️ **Additive by construction, and that is the property the tests pin:** every new field is written only when the writer sets it, so a stamp from a writer that knows none of them is byte-for-byte what it was, and the suite asserts the old shape's exact key set rather than merely that the new fields appear. ⚠️ **`task` and `vault` are written together or not at all** — a task name collides across vaults, so half an anchor cannot be resolved later. ⚠️ **A server started outside a session stamps nothing** rather than stamping under an invented key, which would be a permanent phantom reading Live until its TTL with no session able to clear it.
- fix: **the stamp's activity rides on `activity`, never on the readers' `state` key — a LATENT collision removed while it is still cheap.** `state` on a stamp is the name the liveness VERDICT uses, and four readers gate on `stamp.get("state", "live") != "live"`, where a MISSING key must read as `live` rather than vanish. ⚠️ **Those four gates never see a raw stamp, and an earlier revision of this entry claimed they did** — asserting a `state: "busy"` row "reads as NOT-live" and frees a duplicate auto-resume. Verified wrong by running the reader: the readers' single chokepoint parses a raw stamp and builds a fresh dict carrying its OWN verdict, never reading the file's key, so the field is WRITE-ONLY today and the rename is PREVENTIVE rather than a repair. Pinned by a test asserting the stamp's exact key set, because a key name is what a later refactor restores by accident. ⚠️ **`stopSelf` now clears the stamp as well as the timer** and is called from the `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` handler, so a restarted server does not leave its own row reading live for a full 60 s. ⚠️ **The event half of this work — `hooks/` and `scripts/` — ships as its own PR**, so each half fits the reviewer's chunk budget; neither half is sufficient alone, and a reader who deploys only one should expect the activity to arrive empty rather than wrong. Tests: 10 → 18 JS.

## v0.119.4

- fix: **a manager that reports itself operator-blocked now has to raise an answerable card.** Measured 2026-10-08 on the `MDM Bugs` topic: the loop reported operator-blocked for hours while the attention board carried nothing the operator could answer, and his own words were *"It's waiting for me, but on the tension board or here, I have no questions hitting my face."* Three changes. (1) The `waiting-approval` card was suppressed by a rule scoped to a **different** branch — the under-target bullet's declined-ask suppression, imported through an unscoped quote on a topic page. The suppression policy's home is now that bullet in `commands/manager-loop.md`, **branch-scoped by construction**; the bullet states that `waiting-approval` carries its own and different guard and no subset suppression at all, and the topic page keeps the operator's two sentences as **history, not policy**. (2) The under-target suppression test changed from **set membership to startability** — a candidate set that is a subset of the declined ask *and whose ranked rows all carry a verdict other than `✅ Ready for approval`* posts no card; a row that was **unstartable in the declined snapshot and can start now** makes it a different ask, and it re-posts. ⚠️ **This half is RULES-ONLY at merge.** `scripts/under-target-state.py` records only `card_item_id` / `row_set` / `posted_at` — no per-row startability — so the re-post arm is **unsatisfiable today** and the branch stays fail-closed, i.e. in exactly the suppressing behaviour the MDM Bugs silence came from. The bullet says so itself; extending that script to record the snapshot is what makes this half live, and that is a separate change. (3) `commands/fleet-loop.md` now runs the model-free open-card invariant over **covered** subjects too, so a subject cannot go silent because its manager has — manager-first is a routing rule, never a trust rule, and the covered-subject path claims the subject through `supervisor:asked-ledger` first, so the fleet and a live manager cannot both post for one subject. ⚠️ **The invariant's own script is NOT in this repo:** `sweep-gate.py` lives in the active vault's `.claude/scripts/` and is delivered by that vault's own git, so this release carries the rules that *call* it while the script ships separately — a plugin release without the vault edit names a flag that resolves to nothing, and the vault edit without this release has nothing calling it. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/fleet-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Tests: unchanged at **1869**.

## v0.119.3

- fix: **`fleet-board.py`'s task-file match stops being an equality test, so a session whose registry name is a *truncated* title is no longer filed under `Unmanaged`.** Measured 2026-10-07 on the live board: `fleet-sweep-reader Misresolves Sibling-Vault Tasks` rendered as a child of `Unmanaged` with vault task `—`, though it owns `fleet-sweep-reader Misresolves Sibling-Vault Tasks and Its Header Counts Do Not Sum` — the registry holds a **truncated** name (46 characters against a 108-character title) *and* a `⚙ ` marker a task title never carries, so `VaultIndex.has_task_file()`'s exact test returned False and rule 4 classified a session that owns a task as having none. The name is now normalized (NFKC, leading decoration stripped, whitespace collapsed, casefolded) and accepted when it is a **prefix** of a title — under two guards, because a prefix is a guess about which title was truncated and this predicate feeds a classification, so a confident wrong answer is worse than the unmanaged row it replaces: the prefix must identify **exactly one** title, and must be at least `_TRUNCATION_MIN_PREFIX` (20) characters, so a short role name that happens to open a title (`boss`) is not read as a truncation. ⚠️ **Verified before/after against the live registry:** the row sat at index 25, a child of the `Unmanaged` header at index 24; after the change it sits at index 23, the last child of the root group. ⚠️ **This is the id-less half of the fix, and that row's vault-task column is still `—`** — the column is read from the task's `claude_session_id` stamp, which this task does not carry, so the row is now classified correctly as a worker but still cannot name its task. Where a stamp exists the id join already holds and the name/title divergence is cosmetic: this session's own row proves it, resolving the truncated name `⚙ A Renamed Task's Session Becomes Unaddressable` to its full 108-character title. The stamping half — writing `claude_session_id` at spawn so id-less rows stop being created — lands elsewhere. ⚠️ **The same rule is applied to the sibling id-less fallback, `manager-predispatch.py`'s `roster_owner()`** — it compared a registry name to a task title with the same equality test, and its failure direction is the worse of the two, because a false match there **withholds a legitimate spawn** rather than merely mis-rendering a row. It gains `_names_the_same_session()`, whose guard sits on the *held* side for exactly that reason (an exact match is still accepted at any length, so a genuinely short title resolves). `_TRUNCATION_MIN_PREFIX` is deliberately duplicated across the two scripts — neither imports the other — and a test pins the two values together, since drift would make the board and the pre-dispatch gate disagree about whether a row has an owner. ⚠️ **A third reader joined the same rule, and it arrived mid-PR.** `origin/master` cut `v0.119.1` while this branch was open, adding `scripts/resolve-task-file.py` — *"one home for the fleet sweep's name-based task-file resolution"* — which matched by **exact basename** and therefore carried the same defect. It gains the same prefix tier, reached **only** when the exact tier finds nothing, so its *"Never guesses"* contract is untouched: `resolve` still requires exactly one hit in the tier it reaches and announces the rest as `AMBIGUOUS`. ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-10-08 — that claim covers the tier *ordering* and not the exact tier's match predicate, and the round-3 review was right to catch it.** The same change also ran the on-disk basename through a normalizer, which folded `- Notes.md` into `Notes.md` and could turn a previously unique resolution into a false `AMBIGUOUS`. The basename now collapses whitespace and casefolds but is **not** decoration-stripped — that strip is a session-label rule, not a filename rule — so the exact tier stays exact, and `test_a_decorated_filename_is_not_folded_into_its_plain_twin` pins it. `_TRUNCATION_MIN_PREFIX` is now duplicated across **three** scripts, and one test pins all three together. ⚠️ **A local review of this PR found one Must-Fix, and it was in the third reader.** Its prefix tier for tasks ran *before* the goals **exact** tier, so a prefix *guess* displaced an exact goal match — the one ordering in which the new tier changes behaviour where an exact match exists, against the file's own "never guesses" contract. Both exact tiers now run before either truncation tier (`exact-tasks → exact-goals → prefix-tasks → prefix-goals`), pinned by `test_an_exact_goal_beats_a_prefix_task`. Four Should-Fixes landed with it: `hits()`'s docstring now describes the prefix tier instead of still claiming an exact-only contract; the pinning test covers `_norm_session_name` as well as the constant, since a normalizer drift breaks the same agreement and would pass a constant-only test; `_names_the_same_session` normalizes **both** sides, so a second caller passing a raw title cannot silently no-match; and the on-disk basename is normalized the same way as the lookup, closing a one-sided comparison. Two stale cross-references now name all three copies. ⚠️ **The mismatch-vs-absent discrimination now exists, as `fleet-board.py --mismatches`.** A session whose registry name no longer names the task its `sessionId` resolves to is reported as a **mismatch** — the held name **and** the row it resolves to — never as an absent worker; a session whose id resolves to no task is absent and is **not** listed, so the two cases can no longer read alike. ⚠️ **It lives in `fleet-board.py` deliberately** — that file already joins the registry to the vault, and a *fourth* reader of the registry is the defect the sibling `Give the Session Registry's Join Reads One Home Too` owns. ⚠️ **Three bot-review rounds shaped this mode, and every finding was fixed rather than merged past.** Round 1 (APPROVED) filed two MAJORs: the comparison ran against the **vault-wide** title set, so a session stamping task X while holding task Y's *exact* title was silently skipped (it now compares against the session's **own** titles, which also drops the vault index — and a private-attribute reach-in — entirely); and `resolve-task-file.py`'s prefix tier dropped the exact tier's `.md` requirement, so an Obsidian attachment (`….png`) or a sibling (`….md.gpg`) could resolve as the task file and the caller would then run `grep -cE` and `date -r` against a binary (the tier now requires the entry to end in `.md`). **Round 2 (CHANGES_REQUESTED) filed the CRITICAL:** the mode tested names by *equality* while `has_task_file`, in the same file and the same PR, accepts a truncated *prefix* — so every long-titled, correctly managed session read as drift. Fixed by extracting **`_matches_one_title`**, one rule with two callers, which is the only shape in which the two can never disagree again; live count **10 → 8**. ⚠️ **The PR's own session stays on the list, deliberately:** its registry name reads `…Renamed Tasks Session…` against a title reading `…Task's Session…`, so the name names the row under *neither* tier and the session is addressable by id only — that is the drift this mode exists to report, not a false positive. Round 2's remaining items landed with it: `_names_the_same_session` now documents *why* it carries no exactly-one-title guard (`roster_owner` holds no vault title set to be unique against, and it returns a liveness verdict rather than an owner identity); `docs/subject-resolution.md`'s *"two matches inside one folder are impossible by construction"* is scoped to the exact tier, which the truncation tier falsifies; the `.md.gpg` sibling case is pinned alongside the `.png` one; and the `MISMATCH` TSV row folds tabs and newlines, since it is the script's only tab-separated output path. Tests: **1854 → 1869** — 1854 is the post-merge total and 1869 the count at this change's head; the `1820 → 1835` reading earlier in this entry described only the first two files, before the merge brought master's 19. ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-10-08:** the endpoint read `1868`, which the `docs:` bullet above contradicted — and the suite genuinely runs **1869**, so the sibling figure was the stale one, not the newer. Change set: `scripts/fleet-board.py`, `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/resolve-task-file.py`, `scripts/tests/test_fleet_board.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_resolve_task_file.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

- docs: **the two prose sites that keyed an orphan candidate on a task title now say the id is the verdict.** `commands/manager-loop.md`'s candidate list read *"or whose title matches no roster name"* as an independent trigger, and `agents/manager-drive.md`'s *not on roster* clause named only the name match — so a reader could key a renamed task's ownership on a title the registry name no longer equals. Both now scope the title match to a row that carries **no id to resolve instead**, and name the reason: a registry name is a *truncated* form of a long task title, so a name-keyed match is the fallback and never the verdict. ⚠️ **Behaviour is unchanged, on both roster shapes** — on the **JSON** roster the id probe was already the verdict, and on the **rendered snapshot** (which carries no id column) the name already *was* the verdict; each clause was already subordinate to the shape that governs it, so this is the prose catching up to what ships. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`. Tests: unchanged at **1869**.

## v0.119.2

## v0.119.1
- fix: **the sweep reader resolves a task file across every configured vault, and its digest header is arithmetic rather than prose — two read-half misreports the drive leg found on 2026-09-23.** (1) `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` step 4 resolved `<name>.md` under the caller's `tasks_dir` then `goals_dir` alone, and the caller passes **one** vault (`<inputs>`: *the vault path and its tasks dir*), so a session whose task lives in a sibling vault resolved to nothing and was reported as unowned — a false *unowned* being the same class of defect as a false verdict, because it reads as a finding. It now enumerates the configured vaults via `vault-cli config list` and resolves **tier by tier** — `tasks_dir` across all vaults first, then `goals_dir` — taking a match only when it is the sole hit in the tier reached, and returning no resolution on zero hits **or on ambiguity inside that tier**, with the candidates named in NOTES. ⚠️ **The tier-by-tier rule was found by testing, not by reading:** a first cut that treated any two hits as ambiguous regressed `BRO-22032 MDM Merge of Parties based on names`, which exists as a task in `seibert-brogrammers/25 Tasks/` **and** as a goal in `seibert-brogrammers/24 Goals/` — the task-over-goal precedence the single-vault rule already carried. Verified against five inputs: that pair → the task; `Vuln Fix Agent Bumps @latest…` → its `seibert-brogrammers` task (the sibling-vault case, previously unresolvable); a `private-personal` task → itself; `Fleet Communication` → its goal; an invented name → no resolution. (2) The digest's `CLASSIFICATION` line was free-form prose (`<counts per class>`) that nothing pinned and nothing required to reconcile with the rows beneath it — measured twice, as v0.39.1's `idle=22 (21 parked, 1 finished, 2 unclassified)` where 21+1+2 = 24 ≠ 22, and as v0.46.0's `parked 9` against 7 listed rows. It is now `<total> classified · <class>=<n> · …`, the terms must sum to the total, and for every class that renders rows the count must equal the rows actually printed; counts that cannot be made to agree print `UNKNOWN (counts do not reconcile)` rather than a figure contradicting the section below. ⚠️ **The rule is single-homed rather than guarded:** `commands/manager-loop.md` restated it in a parenthetical, and that copy now points at the agent file instead — the remedy `docs/subject-resolution.md` records for a rule carried in several commands, so no `check-*.py` is added. ⚠️ **Bot review round (2026-10-07) returned APPROVED with three Should Fix and two Nice to Have, and the Should Fix items were fixed rather than merged past.** (1) The `vault-cli` call was unguarded — no `returncode` check, no `timeout`, no `except` — so a missing or failing `vault-cli` would have raised a traceback through a pass that is explicitly not skippable; it now mirrors `vault_dirs_from_cli()` at `scripts/fleet-sessions.py:110`, which the reviewer named as the repo's existing guarded implementation of the same call. (2) The ambiguity branch printed an empty string and left no channel for the colliding paths, while the prose promised they would be named in NOTES and the cited authority requires *every* candidate path to be printed — they now go to stderr as `AMBIGUOUS <path>` lines. (3) The prose read *"else exactly one `goals_dir` hit"*, which parses as fall-through from an ambiguous `tasks_dir` tier, while the code required `not tasks`; the code is the intended rule, so the prose now says *"and with no `tasks_dir` hit at all"* — the two readings diverge on a real input, and an agent implements this rule. Also fixed from the Nice to Have list: `v["path"]` was indexed directly while its sibling key used `.get()`, so a config entry carrying a dir but no path raised `KeyError` and aborted the whole pass. ⚠️ **One Nice to Have is deliberately NOT fixed here:** `commands/fleet-status.md` § step 4 carries the same drift — its prose says *each vault's* while its snippet loops a single `$VAULT` — but that is a different command's fallback path, and the coherent repair there is either a shared helper or a pointer, a choice that belongs with that command rather than smuggled into this diff. Re-verified after the fixes: the snippet parses, and all five cases still resolve as documented, plus a synthetic two-vault collision that now emits both candidates and resolves to nothing. ⚠️ **Bot review round 2 (2026-10-07) also returned APPROVED, and its Should Fix items were fixed too.** (1) **The `hits()` loop sat outside the `try/except`**, which wrapped only `subprocess.run` + `json.loads` — so a `vault-cli` payload that parsed but was not a list of dicts reached `.get()` on a string and raised `AttributeError` straight through a pass the reader declares *"not skippable, and not partially skippable"*. The snippet claimed parity with `vault_dirs_from_cli()` and did not hold it: that loop sits **inside** the guard there. (2) **The rule is now a script rather than a snippet in the agent file.** The finding was that nothing prevented the next edit from re-breaking the task-over-goal precedence — the cases that caught the first regression lived only in a PR body — and this repo's answer to a prose contract is a test beside the code. `scripts/resolve-task-file.py` carries the rule with the guards (and the payload loop inside them), `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` calls it, and `scripts/tests/test_resolve_task_file.py` pins eleven cases: the same-vault task/goal collision that regressed, a sibling-vault task, a goal-only name, an invented name, a two-vault task collision that must name both candidates *and* resolve nothing, an ambiguous `tasks_dir` tier that must not fall through to `goals_dir`, a config entry carrying no dirs, and four degradation paths — non-list payload, non-zero exit, malformed JSON, and a list containing non-dicts — each of which must reach the no-resolution path rather than raise. ⚠️ **One Should Fix is deliberately NOT fixed here:** the `CLASSIFICATION` header contract remains prose-only, with no `check-classification-header.py` guard. The reviewer is right that `check-bucket-clause.py` is the precedent, but a guard over a rendered-digest fixture is a separate change with its own fixture design, and this PR is the reader's. ⚠️ **Bot review round 3 (2026-10-07) returned CHANGES_REQUESTED with one CRITICAL and five Should Fix, and all six were fixed.** The CRITICAL was the sharpest finding of the three rounds and it was this diff's own: `vault_dirs()` degraded to `[]` **silently** on every failure — a non-zero exit, a timeout, a missing binary, a malformed payload — so a broken `vault-cli` was indistinguishable from an empty vault list and would have rendered *every* session unowned. That is the same false-*unowned* defect this whole change removes, reintroduced on the failure path. The guard had been copied from `fleet-sessions.py`'s `vault_dirs_from_cli()`, and the reviewer named precisely why that copy inverted its meaning: the helper is safe **at its site** only because `fleet-sessions.py:150` falls back to `PROBE_DIRS`, a fallback this script did not carry. It now emits `DEGRADED <reason>` on stderr, and `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` renders the whole task-file pass `UNKNOWN (pass not run)` on that marker rather than a blank. The five Should Fix items: (1) the `claude_session_id:` stamp leg was described as "scoped the same way" without saying *by what* — verified that `fleet-sessions.py`'s `_walk_task_stamps()` already walks every vault under the Obsidian root, so the sentence now names that leg's home instead of implying this script implements it; (2) **the `CLASSIFICATION` arithmetic rule is now stated in the file as deliberately unguarded**, with the reason — it constrains a line the reader *renders*, not a lookup it *performs*, so a check would have to validate a fixture it defines itself and would pin nothing — which is the reviewer's second offered option and the honest one; (3) **the per-session cost is gone:** step 4 now calls the script once per round with every name on stdin, removing ~46 `vault-cli` invocations and ~46 interpreter starts per sweep on a pass declared non-skippable; (4) `commands/fleet-status.md` § step 4 carried the identical hand-rolled single-`$VAULT` loop with the same false-*unowned* defect and was the third documented caller — it now points at `resolve-task-file.py` instead of restating the rule, which is what the review asked for and what this PR had deferred; (5) `stem = name.casefold() + ".md"` turned an already-suffixed `Foo.md` into a search for `Foo.md.md`, resolving nothing and silently rendering the session unowned — the exact failure this change exists to prevent, reachable from a plausible input; a trailing `.md` is now stripped. Two Nice to Have were taken as well: `hits()` now requires `os.path.isfile`, so a *directory* named `<name>.md` no longer resolves into a path that downstream `grep -cE` errors on and `date -r` reports the wrong mtime for; and an absolute config-supplied `tasks_dir` is refused rather than honoured, since `os.path.join` would discard the vault path and probe an arbitrary directory. Tests: **11 → 16** — the new cases are the already-suffixed name, a directory named like a task, an absolute `tasks_dir`, the stdin batch form, and four degradation paths each asserting the `DEGRADED` marker plus a negative case asserting a healthy read carries none. ⚠️ **Bot review round 4 (2026-10-07) returned CHANGES_REQUESTED with two Should Fix and two NIT, and all four were fixed — one of them a defect round 3 introduced.** (1) **`commands/fleet-status.md`'s repointed snippet read stdout only, and `DEGRADED` went to stderr** — so on a failed vault read `cut -f2` yielded an empty value, which that step's own prose reads as "no resolution", rendering the peer `—`: the false-*unowned* defect arriving through a stream the caller was not watching. Fixed at the root rather than at the call site: **the degradation is now carried on stdout as the value `UNKNOWN`**, which is not a path and must never be rendered as one, with `DEGRADED <reason>` still mirrored to stderr. Any caller reading one stream now sees it. (2) `vault_dirs()` appended one entry per config record with no dedupe, so a config listing one vault twice — or twice through a symlink — made `hits()` return the same file twice and `resolve()` read two identical hits as a collision, printing a **false `AMBIGUOUS`**; it now dedupes on `os.path.realpath`, as `fleet-sessions.py:131` does. From the NIT list: the ambiguous candidate list was `tasks + goals`, so an ambiguous `tasks_dir` announced goal-tier paths for a tier that was never consulted and cannot be the collision — `resolve()` now returns the reached tier's candidates alone; and two tests were added for exactly those paths (a duplicated config entry must not read as an ambiguity; an ambiguous task tier must announce no goal path). Tests: **16 → 18**. ⚠️ **Bot review round 5 (2026-10-07) returned APPROVED — `merge: CLEAN`, both checks green — with three Nice to Have, and all three were taken rather than merged past**, because two of them are this PR's own subject: prose claiming more than the code does. (1) `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md` said the test file "pins sixteen cases" while it held eighteen — the number is now **removed** rather than corrected, since a count in prose goes stale the moment a case is added, which is exactly how it went stale. (2) The `tasks_dir` guard refused an absolute path but not a relative `..` component, so `os.path.join(vault, "../elsewhere")` still escaped the vault root while the guard's comment claimed that escape was "the failure this guard exists to prevent"; it now refuses both and the comment states only what it enforces. (3) `resolve()` computed the `goals_dir` hits eagerly, before the short-circuit that returns a unique `tasks_dir` hit, so every name whose task tier already resolved paid a full goals-tier `listdir` across every vault for nothing — it is now computed only when the task tier is empty or ambiguous. Test for the `..` case added. Tests: **18 → 19**. Change set: `agents/fleet-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/fleet-status.md`, `scripts/resolve-task-file.py`, `scripts/tests/test_resolve_task_file.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.119.0

- feat: the necessity verdict now names **which** of three sources a task serves — the goal sentence, a specific Success Criterion (`SC<n>`), or a specific Definition of Done item (`DoD<n>`) — instead of testing success criteria alone. `manager-verify` step 1 echoes the serving item per task rather than a count, and `manager-sweep-reader` step 8's `needed` verdict now requires the source kind, the served line **and** the task line that advances it; a match inferred from a task title, a shared theme, or a loosely related criterion is `not needed` with `unproven` as the stated reason, never rounded up. ⚠️ **The three-verdict shape and the `product` rows are deliberately unchanged** — `unproven` is reported inside `not needed`, never as a fourth bucket, so before/after comparisons of this read stay valid. Tests: **1810 → 1820** (Python suite; the Node suite is unchanged at 463). Change set: `agents/manager-verify.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `scripts/check-necessity-templates.py` (new), `scripts/tests/test_check_necessity_templates.py` (new), `Makefile`. Consumes the widened necessity output: `verify-goal` via bborbe/vault-cli#270, and `verify-topic` via its vault-local command (the primary vault's `.claude/commands/verify-topic.md`, which no plugin ships — see `agents/manager-verify.md`'s dependency-gap note).

## v0.118.0
- fix: **`gate-owner-filter.py` gains a poll + change-detect loop, so the ownership-scoped attention arm can actually be armed as a `Monitor` — and the rule's three pointer sites stop naming a home that does not carry the snippet.** The rule's arm was `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you | gate-owner-filter.py --feed --self <sid>`, and `--feed` reads stdin **once**: a `Monitor` over that pipeline fires on the first read and the process exits, so the manager's push channel dies with no error, no marker and no stale-loop signal — the one failure § Sweep output's marker cannot catch, because the marker only answers *alive or stuck?* while ticks keep arriving. Measured 2026-10-07 against v0.115.5: `--help` exposed no watch flag and the source had **zero** hits for `while True|time.sleep`. New surface: `--interval SECONDS` (poll mode; **omitted** keeps the one-shot stdin read byte-for-byte, so nothing existing changes behaviour), `--producer CMD` (default the sibling `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you`, resolved **by path** so the two scripts cannot drift across installs), `--max-polls N` (bounds the loop, matching `manager-attention-watch.py`, and the only way to test an unbounded loop without racing the clock). ⚠️ **The loop is a CHANGE-DETECTOR, not a re-emitter, and that distinction is the whole design.** A shell `while true; do … | gate-owner-filter.py --feed …; sleep N; done` wrapper — the obvious fix, and the one a manager hand-rolling this reaches for — re-emits every kept pane on every poll, turning a push channel into a 1/NHz poll of the manager's entire turn budget, which is the cost this filter exists to remove (measured on Fleet Manager `64b4a415`: ~12 peer-owned wakes at a median 345,344 cache-read tokens per turn). So a pane is emitted when it **ENTERS** the kept set and never on a re-poll. ⚠️ **That is only safe because hop 3b — PRE-EXISTING, untouched by this change — already drops peer-manager panes.** ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-10-07:** this sentence read *"hop 3b **now** drops"*, which reads as "this change" when it means "currently"; `git show origin/master:scripts/gate-owner-filter.py` already carries hop 3b and this diff touches none of it. While hop 3b could not reach a peer manager's own pane, peer churn reached the kept set and a naive diff fired on it, which is the measured cause of the three foreign wakes Defect 1 was filed on — so the change-detector's safety is a property of code that shipped **before** it, not of anything here. ⚠️ **A `LIVENESS  <counts>  new: <n>  change|no change` line goes to stderr on EVERY poll**, because a change-detector is silent when nothing changes and silence is byte-identical to a dead arm — the defect `manager-attention-watch.py` closed for its own arm with the same record. Its presence answers *alive or stuck?*; the age of the newest one is the arm's last heartbeat. ⚠️ **A refusal ends the loop non-zero rather than being retried**, preserving the one-shot contract (*"do not arm a watcher on this pipeline's output"*); on a self-polling arm that means the refusal arrives **mid-run** rather than at arm time, so the missing `LIVENESS` line is the signal to re-arm. ⚠️ **The rule's three bare pointers are qualified.** `commands/manager-loop.md` § step 3 and § step 7 each read `Rule: the Manager Session runbook § Cadence mechanics.` and `commands/fleet-loop.md`'s attention-watcher bullet ended on the same unqualified form — none of them naming the vault, while `65 Runbooks/` exists in **two** vaults (`private-personal` carries the `gate-owner-filter` snippet 9×, `seibert-personal` 0×). All three now name `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Cadence mechanics, each calling out the Personal vault. ⚠️ **Corrected 2026-10-07 in the second bot review round, because the first draft of this entry overstated the diff in two places.** (1) It said the vault call-out had landed at all three sites; it had landed at **two** — `commands/fleet-loop.md` carried **zero** occurrences of `Personal`, so the ambiguity the entry claimed to remove was still live at the very site the entry named. The call-out is now there in both of that file's pointers. (2) It said `fleet-loop.md`'s *"one home, `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Cadence mechanics"* "was already qualified and is unchanged" — true of **that sentence**, which is why it was written, but the same bullet's **trailing** pointer read the bare *"the Manager Session runbook § Cadence mechanics"* and **did** change, so a reader checking the diff against the entry found it contradicted. Both are named here rather than quietly fixed: a changelog that misreports its own diff is the artifact the release reads. ⚠️ **Step 7's arm now prints the `--interval` form**, so SC3's "the rule names a runnable, armable watcher" is satisfiable by running what the rule prints. ⚠️ **A disambiguation the change forced:** both command files carried *"`--interval` does not govern this watcher"*, which became ambiguous once the filter had an `--interval` of its own — both now say **the tick's** `--interval` and name the filter's poll period as the watcher's own. ⚠️ **A refactor bug the existing suite caught, recorded rather than hidden:** the first cut passed `(…, claims, self_id)` into the new `evaluate_panes`, whose signature is `(…, self_id, claims)`; 3 of 90 tests failed with `'str' object has no attribute 'get'` — the two parameters are adjacent and both truthy, so nothing but the suite would have caught it. Tests: **90 → 103**, all passing; the one-shot empty-feed contract (`exit 0`, `gates: 0`) is unchanged and now pinned. ⚠️ **Local review round (2026-10-07) found one Should-Fix, fixed in the same change:** the new stderr `LIVENESS` shape was documented in step 7 while the bullet names § Cadence mechanics as the one home — and that section's `LIVENESS` is a *different* contract (the tracked-set arm's `events.jsonl` record, not this arm's stderr line). Step 7 now says so explicitly rather than leaving a reader to find the mismatch. The review's other finding (the new helpers repeat a six-positional-parameter signature, the adjacency that produced the `self_id`/`claims` swap above) is left as-is and recorded: it matches the file's existing `evaluate()` shape, so changing it here would be a refactor of the surrounding style, not a fix. ⚠️ **Bot review round (2026-10-07) returned CHANGES_REQUESTED with one CRITICAL, and the CRITICAL was right.** `poll()` re-read the *feed* every iteration but judged it against ownership state loaded **once at arm time**, so over a 30-minute arm a claim recorded by a peer manager was invisible (its pane emitted — the foreign wake Defect 1 is named for) and a peer manager that died mid-loop stayed in the stale `live` set (its own gate still dropped, then owned by nobody, contradicting the invariant stated at `verdict()`). Fixed by re-reading **inside** the loop. ⚠️ **The one cached source is `items`, and the exception is measured rather than assumed:** `log_items` globs and parses ~1 589 event-log files into ~25 000 items in **195.8 ms**, against 37.4 ms for the ledger, 11.7 ms for the claims file and 1.3 ms for the registry, so everything cheap is re-read per poll and the one expensive source is read once per arm. Its residual is named in the docstring: a pane whose first event-log appearance lands mid-arm reads `unowned` until the next re-arm — the conservative direction, bounded by the 30-minute re-arm. Four further findings fixed: a producer exiting non-zero was read as a **quiet fleet** (empty stdout, no refusal marker, so `gates: 0` every poll forever — the false reading `refusal_exit` exists to prevent, reintroduced one layer up; `producer_exit` now ends the loop non-zero); `--interval` had no lower bound, so `-1` reached `time.sleep` and died with an unhandled `ValueError` traceback while `0` was an unbounded busy-loop; `--interval` combined with `--explain`/`--json` printed every row on every poll, which a `Monitor` would fire on each time — both now refused at the parser; `POLL_SECONDS` was dead code once `--interval` defaulted to `None`; and the producer default now resolves with `realpath`, since `abspath` does not follow a symlink and a symlinked install would have raised `FileNotFoundError` at the first poll. ⚠️ **A second bot round the same day returned CHANGES_REQUESTED again — two doc-correctness defects and no code**, which is the useful signal: the CRITICAL it raised in round one was gone. (1) `commands/fleet-loop.md` printed the new self-polling arm while keeping the **old arm-time refusal semantics** (*"the manager does NOT arm the Monitor on that output … a `Monitor` armed on a refused pipeline never fires and still looks alive"*) — so a fleet reader was told not to arm the very output the block directly above tells them to arm. The mid-run correction `commands/manager-loop.md` already carried is now mirrored there, and the stale sentence is explicitly marked as describing the old one-shot shape rather than deleted, because that shape is still what a hand-rolled pipe produces. (2) This entry's own vault-qualification claim, corrected above. One NIT with them: a lowercase sentence start the `--interval` rewording left at `commands/manager-loop.md:67`. ⚠️ **A third round APPROVED the change and filed two Should-Fix and three NIT findings — all documentation precision, no code — and they were fixed rather than merged past**, because this task exists to make these docs accurate. The Should-Fix pair is the one worth recording: both command files claimed *"a quiet fleet costs no turns at all"* for the change-detector while **mandating a ≤30-minute re-arm in the same paragraph**, and `poll()` opens with `previous = None`, so the first poll of every arm treats every still-open gate as newly entered — a re-arm therefore costs one manager turn **per held gate**, at the measured 345,344 cache-read tokens per turn. Both carriers now state the re-arm cost next to the claim it qualifies. With them: the `--interval 60` choice is now given its rationale (fixed, not derived from the tick, so a slow tick cannot slow the push channel), the two adjacent refusal paragraphs in `manager-loop.md` are reconciled explicitly as `fleet-loop.md` already did, and *"hop 3b **now** drops"* was corrected — hop 3b is **pre-existing** and untouched by this diff, so "now" read as "this change". Tests: **103 → 110**. Change set: `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/fleet-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.117.0
- feat: **the tracked-set attention watcher re-registers a parked worker's card when the store has lost it, and escalates on park AGE at 15 and 60 minutes — so a gate the store dropped cannot sit unseen for 84 minutes again.** Measured 2026-10-07: a worker on `Widen the Dev mdm-contact-v1 Read Allowlist` raised an `AskUserQuestion` at **12:28:45** and the registry read `waiting` from then on, while by 15:29 `agent_status` returned `open_cards: []` (read and empty, not unreachable), `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you` did not list its pane, and `notify-gate.py` printed *"no open store item for session 15fce333; cross-layer de-dup is NOT in force for this gate"*. The watcher's ONE signal had been `NEW GATE` at 14:30:30 — it emits on a transition, never again on age — and the first thing the operator got was a hand-built phone push at 15:31, 84 minutes in. ⚠️ **The producer-side cause (why the store drops a still-open item) is a separate row and is NOT addressed here;** this is the manager-side defence that holds while the store is lossy. ⚠️ **Re-post:** `repost_empty_parks` posts via `attention-ask.py post` with the stable `--dedup-key park:<sid8>` whenever a parked session has no open store item. ⚠️ **The producer is the WORKER, not the manager** — a store item's session is its `producer_id` (`who-needs-me.py:268`), so posting as the worker is what makes the re-posted row attribute to the session that is actually parked and routes the operator's answer back to it; it also collapses "is there already a card?" to one field. ⚠️ **`liveness_ref` stays the `owner:` default, and that is load-bearing:** `attention-ask.py:506-532` records that the store PRUNES an open asked item whose liveness subject is not live, so a `session:<id>` subject deletes the card on the first read after the poster exits — and a re-post is by construction posted by something other than the parked worker. ⚠️ **The store read is filtered to `state == "open"`, and that filter is load-bearing** — `GET /api/1.0/attention` answers with items in **every** state, not only open ones (three sibling docstrings that call it open-only are wrong; `who-needs-me.py:271` maps a non-`open` state to `answered`, which is only reachable if one can come back). An ANSWERED card counted as open would suppress the re-post for a worker still parked on a fresh question — a **missed** escalation rather than a noisy one. ⚠️ **A failed store read re-posts nothing** (`open_store_items` returns `None`, never two empty sets): an empty result is a positive claim — *"the store holds nothing for anyone"* — and the re-post acts on it, so a dead store read as empty would re-post a card for every parked worker on every poll. ⚠️ **Park age is keyed on CONTINUOUS PARK per registry status, never on the gated-set transition, and that is the other half of the fix.** A worker that answers one question and asks the next stays in the watcher's gated set the whole time — `is_gated` holds it through the `busy` interval by design — so a clock keyed on set membership would run once from the first park and never restart, and the second park would never surface. Measured the same afternoon: the arm ran its full 30 minutes and delivered **0 events** while two tracked sessions parked on fresh `AskUserQuestion`s inside that window (`3a324639` at 15:58, `15fce333` at 16:05), because neither park was a set transition. So a `busy` poll ENDS the park and the next parked poll STARTS a new one. The parked reasons are `registry:waiting`, `busy+parked-modal` and `headless+closer`; `idle+closer` is deliberately excluded — that is the shape the ownership-filtered feed already carries. ⚠️ **`headless+closer` is the one of the three the registry cannot reach at all** — a headless worker is an in-process SDK `query()` with no pid, so `registry_status` returns `None` and `is_gated` decides on the `headless_live` branch — and `who-needs-me.py:822` records a measured case of that class (a parked headless worker's item, `pane: ""`) rendering no row in ANY feed section. So it is precisely the park the feed provably cannot carry, and excluding it would have made it the one park this arm ignored. ⚠️ **`AGED` goes to STDOUT, unlike `LIVENESS`:** every stdout line is a `Monitor` notification and therefore a full model turn, which is why the liveness record is log-only — but `AGED` is the opposite case, because the manager's instruction is to VOICE it and a line written only to the log is durable and useless. ⚠️ **The park-age clock starts at first observation, so restarting the arm resets every age.** The registry carries no timestamp for when a session entered `waiting`, so a park already an hour old when the arm starts reads as age 0 and earns its escalation late. Stated rather than hidden: the alternative is inventing an entry time, and an invented age is worse than a late one. ⚠️ The park-age state is a SIBLING file (`park-<scope>.json`), never a wider record inside the gated-set file — that file is a plain JSON list read with `set(json.load(fh))`, and widening it would make every existing state file unreadable, which this file's own read path answers with a `NEW GATE` burst for the whole tracked set. `tracked_ids` becomes a projection of a new `tracked_entries`, which additionally returns the FULL session id the `producer_id` join needs; `probe` takes that projection as an optional argument, so the tracked set is still parsed once per poll. Change set: `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_attention_watch.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`.


## v0.116.2
- fix: **the drive leg's `recorded_at` provenance is the manager-predispatch store, not the sweep-gate snapshot — one number, one producer.** The dispatch named the snapshot as the source of the leg's `recorded_at` while the value a manager read and passed came from the store (`~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/<slug>.json`), and the two files advance on different cadences — the snapshot only on CHANGE-WAKE ticks (§ Session end), the store on every `--save` — so the value the leg was handed was routinely not the value its named source held. Measured 2026-10-05 21:21–21:29 on the BRO-21389 manager tick: after a successful `--save` (exit 10, *"digest differs"*) the store read `recorded_at 2026-10-05T21:21:14+02:00` while the snapshot read `20:22:06+02:00` (`snapshot.frozen-t2208.json`, 49 tasks), and the first drive-leg dispatch of that tick held **all 49 rows** at clause (0); re-dispatching with the snapshot's own value cleared it. Operator decision 2026-10-07 (board card `7e4ee158c0302de40f9ef7d9cb495890`, option 1 of 2): **promote the store's `recorded_at` to the leg's provenance** — rejecting *rewrite the snapshot each sweep*, which keeps two producers of one number and adds a second write to keep them agreeing. ⚠️ **Clause (0) gains the half it enforced but never documented.** The deployed text named a **missing-half** trigger alone, while the leg's measured behaviour held on a **value-vs-named-source mismatch** (the 49-row hold above). Clause (0) now states both — (a) a missing half, (b) a value that is not its named source's, read and compared against the named file rather than taken on the caller's word — so the rule the leg enforces is the rule the contract states. ⚠️ **Four dispatch sites carried the old naming, not the three the row listed:** `commands/manager-loop.md` step 4 and `commands/manager-drive.md` step 4 each carried the *"snapshot provenance … `recorded_at` from the snapshot the sweep wrote"* placeholder and producer sentence, and `agents/manager-drive.md` carried it five times (`<constraints>`, clause (0), the failure-mode bullet, the report template, the closing summary). All now name the store as the single producer of both halves. ⚠️ **`docs/fleet-surface.md` § Session end keeps the snapshot's `recorded_at` definition and gains a boundary:** it is the snapshot's own identity, never the leg's provenance. ⚠️ **The fix is not contract-only, and the first cut of this change was wrong to say it was.** `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`'s `print_provenance` — the one machine-readable statement of where the dispatch's `recorded_at` comes from, printed on **every** `--save` — still labelled the snapshot `(recorded_at source)`: it said the opposite of what the contract now requires, at the exact point the caller reads it, so a manager following that label would hand the snapshot's value and hold every row in the batch — the 2026-10-05 failure above. The store line now carries `(recorded_at source, …)` and the snapshot is labelled `(row source)`, its actual role in the drive-leg flow. `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py` pinned the old pairing, so it moves with it — and is now pinned as two whole lines rather than four independent substrings, because asserting only that both labels appear would pass with them **swapped**, which is the defect itself. ⚠️ **A drift guard is added, because this contract is carried at four dispatch sites and the drift was found by eye:** `scripts/check-provenance-sentence.py` asserts the shared provenance paragraph is byte-identical across `commands/manager-loop.md` step 4 and `commands/manager-drive.md` step 4, and that neither carrier names the snapshot as the leg's provenance — wired into `make precommit` beside its `check-bucket-clause` and `check-recording-step` siblings. Making that assertion possible required unifying one gratuitous wording split inside the paragraph (`this run's own` → `this sweep's own`) — the same drift class one level down, and the guard failed on it on its first run, which is how it was found. ⚠️ **Review round 2 then widened it, because the first version covered 2 of the 4 dispatch sites:** the paragraph comparison stays on the two *verbatim* carriers (the others state the contract in their own wording, so there is no span to compare), but the negative half now runs over **all five** files that state it — `agents/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-status.md` and `docs/fleet-surface.md` included — and each was probed to confirm the assertion fires rather than passing vacuously. The same round fixed the **no-change-branch** sibling test, which asserted only the two path prefixes and therefore passed under *any* labelling including a swap: it now pins the same two whole lines the change-branch test does. `scripts/sweep-gate-classify.py` remains unchanged: nothing in this change asks it to write or emit anything new, and the snapshot keeps its role as the leg's *row* source. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `scripts/check-provenance-sentence.py`, `Makefile`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.116.1
- fix: **the voice-mode gate in `commands/worker-drive.md` now greps the label the TTS server actually stores, so a session that has spoken is detected instead of reading as silent.** The detection matched `"sender":"$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID"` against `/state`'s `recent[]`, but a spoken entry is tagged with the **resolved session name**: the `mcp__tts__say` tool replaces whatever `sender` it is handed with the name it resolves from the caller's `X-Claude-Code-Session-Id` header (`src/session_attribution.py` — `attribution_label` returns the name whenever it resolves, `sender` only as the miss fallback). The UUID therefore never appears, the count was **always 0**, and the gate read "voice off" in exactly the sessions voice was turned on for — the failure this section already documents as observed 2026-09-16, whose fix introduced the grep. Measured 2026-10-07 on one session: a call passing `sender: "<uuid>"` was stored as `⚙ worker-drive Voice Gate`, the raw UUID appeared **0** times in the buffer, and of the 9 distinct senders present **not one was a UUID**. ⚠️ **The UUID is matched too, and that is not belt-and-braces:** the HTTP fallback this same command documents (the `curl` under *If `mcp__tts__say` errors*) sends no session-id header, and a name is resolved **only** from that header — so a verdict spoken through the fallback lands under the raw UUID, and a name-only grep would go quiet on exactly the sessions that had to use it. ⚠️ **An unreadable registry is now its own outcome.** `CLAUDE_PID` unset, the file absent, or `.name` empty used to collapse into an empty pattern and print a bare `🔇 voice off`, indistinguishable from a healthy negative; it now prints `🔇 voice unknown`, because a gate that cannot read the session's own name has decided nothing. The name is matched **verbatim** — `/state` carries the registry name byte-for-byte, a leading `⚙ ` included, checked across all 9 live senders — and both patterns use `-F`, since session names carry regex metacharacters. ⚠️ **The UUID pattern is appended only when the id is non-empty, and that guard is load-bearing rather than defensive.** A spawned child is stripped of `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` (`scripts/attention-ask.py:61` names it as a real case; `commands/open.md` unsets it before spawning a manager), and the HTTP fallback interpolates that same empty variable into its body — so one child's fallback utterance lands in `/state` as `"sender":""`, after which a bare second pattern would read **non-zero in every child** and have them speak in sessions that have never spoken, which line 518 forbids. Found in review; the exposure predates this change (the old one-liner interpolated the same variable) and is closed here rather than carried forward. Verified live on the same session: the new block returns **1**, the old line returns **0**, `CLAUDE_PID` unset returns `🔇 voice unknown`; with the id unset the pattern array stays at one pattern and the block still returns **1** under both `bash` and `zsh`. Change set: `commands/worker-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.


## v0.116.0
- feat: **`spawn_agent` takes a per-call `env`, so ONE session can be launched against a non-production backend without redirecting every session in a vault.** The only run-scoped redirect reachable before it was a vault's `claude_script`, and that applies to every session started from that vault — so it cannot express *"this one session, for this one run"*, and the alternative, editing a default every other session depends on, is how one run's fixture reaches the live board. ⚠️ **Both spawn paths carry it, and they carry it by different mechanisms — a headless-only implementation is the laziest passing one.** A headless worker is an in-process SDK `query()`, so its values ride the existing `env` option: `workerEnvFor` gains an `overrides` layer that sits ABOVE the inherited environment and BELOW the mode handover, because a per-call value exists to win over the ambient one, while `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MODE` is the server telling the worker a fact about ITSELF — a caller able to overwrite it could make a headless worker believe it has a tab, the exact mis-model that handover was written to end. A tab worker is a separate process started by `wezterm cli spawn -- bash -lc <inner>`, so its values are EXPORTED into that command line: `export NAME='value'; `, placed BEFORE the `cd` so it survives the login shell's profile files, and an `export` rather than a `VAR=value` prefix on the `cd`, which would apply to that one command and be gone before the `exec` that actually starts the worker. ⚠️ **`env` is REFUSED with `target: "cluster"`** — that path creates no local process, so there is no environment here to set — matching the refusals `resume`, `policy` and `interactive` already carry. ⚠️ **Malformed input REFUSES rather than dropping the key:** a name that is not a legal shell variable name, a value that is not a string/number/boolean, or an `env` that is not an object each refuse the spawn, because a silently dropped key is a value the worker does not have and reads exactly like one that was never asked for — the same refuse-rather-than-default rule `resolveWorkerTarget` and `resolveSpawnTarget` follow. ⚠️ **The spawn response reports NAMES only, never values** (`env: {keys: [...]}`): a caller may legitimately pass a credential, and this response is quoted into ledger records, PR bodies and `# Progress` entries, so the value would land where it must never appear. ⚠️ `shellQuote` moves to `spawn-mode.mjs`, the pure, importable half — `supervisor.mjs` cannot be imported by a test at all, since it connects a stdio server at load. ⚠️ **Review round 1 (2026-10-07) then fixed a silent-drop bug in this same function:** `resolved['__proto__'] = …` on a plain `{}` is a NO-OP — `__proto__` is an accessor on `Object.prototype` whose setter ignores any value that is not an object or null — while `JSON.parse` DOES create it as an own enumerable key, so a caller reaching this path through MCP args really did produce it and the key vanished with no error anywhere: the exact failure the refuse-never-drop rule exists to prevent, sitting inside the function that states it. Resolution now collects pairs and returns `Object.fromEntries`, which defines an own property; measured 2026-10-07, `o['__proto__'] = 'x'` on a plain `{}` leaves `Object.keys(o)` empty. The same round documented that a **tab** worker's env VALUES are visible in the host process table (`ps`, `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`) for the life of the pane — inherent to exporting into a command line, and the one surface the keys-only rule does not cover, since that rule governs the response and not the process table (the headless path is not exposed) — and added a **source-level assertion that both spawn paths are wired**, because a headless-only build passes every pure-helper test and `supervisor.mjs` is unimportable. ⚠️ **Round 2 (same day) closed the other half of that same invariant, which held on only one of the two paths:** `workerEnvFor` makes the SERVER's mode win on the *headless* path, but the tab path had no handover to win with — `shellEnvExports` exported whatever the caller passed — so a caller-supplied `SUPERVISOR_WORKER_MODE` was inert on one path and live on the other. Live is not cosmetic: `scripts/permission-answer-poll.py:287` returns early on `headless`, so a caller telling a **tab** worker it is headless silences that worker's attention-store relay. Both reserved names are now **REFUSED** in `resolveEnvOverrides`, so the rule reads the same wherever it is found rather than being silently outranked on one path. The same round corrected `docs/fleet-surface.md` — the canonical spawn-shape home, which still enumerated only `interactive`, `resume` and `policy` as cluster refusals — and pinned the tab path's quoting through a **real shell round trip** (newline, backtick, `$(…)`, trailing backslash, semicolon, empty) rather than the single-quote case alone, since that is the claim the tab path rests on. Change set: `server/spawn-mode.mjs`, `server/spawn-mode.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.


## v0.115.6
- perf: **both attention poll loops now read the store only while something is parked, cutting a park-free server's idle request rate against the board by ~93 %.** Measured 2026-10-07 on the live board (host `burn`, pid 96646): **46** `supervisor.mjs` instances held ~94 connections and issued **~46 req/s** against `/api/1.0/attention` while the store held **zero** permission-class items open, and **53.8 %** of the board's CPU sat under `net/http.(*conn).serve`. A marginal-cost A/B against the board's own `process_cpu_seconds_total` (+48.4 req/s → +3.30 pp, 18.86 % → 22.16 %) prices the pollers at **~0.068 pp of CPU per req/s**, i.e. ~3.1 pp of the board's ~19 % — the lever is real and bounded, which is why the read is skipped rather than the interval merely widened. ⚠️ **Skipping and slowing are not interchangeable here:** `GET /api/1.0/attention` returns **OPEN** items only (`scripts/answered-watch.py`'s header measures this), so an item that opens *and* is answered between two reads is never watched, never settled, and its park auto-denies at `PERMISSION_TIMEOUT_MS`. A park can appear at any moment, so a longer interval would widen that window — instead `pending` is re-checked every `idleCheckMs` (**250 ms**, a Map lookup, no HTTP) and the read fires **immediately** once anything is parked, bounding the window at 250 ms, **narrower** than the 2 s it was before. ⚠️ **The idle path is not silent, and that is deliberate:** `recordAttempt(…, 'failed')` — the one record that says an answer reached nobody — needs the item in `watching`, and only a read populates that, so a server holding no park still reads every `idleIntervalMs` (**30 s**). That is 1 read / 15 s per server rather than 1 / s, not zero; dropping to zero would silently delete a record `attention-poll.test.mjs` pins. ⚠️ **`lastReadAtMs` is `NEGATIVE_INFINITY`, not `0`** — the first tick must always read, and `0` does not guarantee it under a caller-supplied clock that starts at 0, which would let every spec pass for the wrong reason. ⚠️ **Which specs discriminate and which are guards, stated exactly because an overstated verification is the one error this paragraph exists to prevent.** Run against the unmodified `server/attention-poll.mjs`, the suite reports **39 pass / 3 fail**. The three failures are `a server holding no park reads once, then stops reading until the trail is due` (the old loop read on every tick, so it observed 2 reads where the spec requires 1), `a store that throws while idle is retried on the idle cadence, not every idle check` (the old loop re-read on every tick), and `an unreadable watched item is dropped, and does not wedge the rest of the tick` (the guard added below).
