The remaining cadence specs pass against the old code as well and are therefore **guards, not regressions**: they pin the 250 ms re-check, the parked fast path, and the delay the new `parked ? intervalMs : idleCheckMs` line chooses — all of which this change must preserve rather than introduce. ⚠️ **The delay is captured by the harness, not inferred**: `setTimeoutImpl` records the delay beside the callback, so replacing that expression with a constant now fails a spec instead of passing silently. ⚠️ **`elapsedMs >= 0` is an explicit guard, not a redundancy** — `nowImpl` defaults to `Date.now`, a wall clock, and a backward NTP step would otherwise make the difference negative, read as "not due yet", and suppress the trail read until the clock caught up. ⚠️ **This PR also closes a pre-existing permanent-wedge bug the reviewer surfaced, because the fix is the sibling file's own guard applied to the byte-identical read.** `attention-poll.mjs`'s per-item `GET /api/1.0/attention/{id}` was **unguarded**, so one unreadable id — the store 404s a pruned item, which is how it happens in the field — escaped to the outer catch and skipped every LATER item in that tick; and because `watching.delete` is only reached after a successful read, the id stayed in the set and re-threw on every subsequent tick, wedging attention delivery for that process permanently and silently. `message-delivery.mjs` already guarded its copy of that read and documented this exact failure in its own comment (*"One pruned item would wedge delivery permanently, silently"*). The read is now guarded per item, the dead id is dropped and logged, and the failure is bounded to one drop per tick — the spec pins the bound rather than the absence, because the next list read legitimately re-adds an id the store still lists as open. Change set: `server/attention-poll.mjs`, `server/attention-poll.test.mjs`, `server/message-delivery.mjs`, `server/message-delivery.test.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.


## v0.115.5
- docs: **the snapshot schema's single home now states that `recorded_at` advances on digest change and not on every tick, and names the heartbeat as the liveness signal.** `docs/fleet-surface.md` already said a freshness reading cannot be taken from the snapshot, but not *why* the field freezes nor where to look instead — so the one number that cannot answer *"is this subject's loop alive?"* was the one a reader reached for. Measured 2026-10-07: `~/.claude/scripts/sweep-gate-notify-tick.sh:137–141` invokes `sweep-gate-classify.py` — the only writer of `<vault>/<subject>.snapshot.json` — **exclusively on the CHANGE-WAKE branch (gate exit 10)**; the EMPTY branch does `rm -f "$streak"` and nothing else, per the script's own header (*"gate exit 0 → EMPTY: nothing changed: heartbeat + ledger only"*). So `recorded_at` is a **last-digest-change identity**, and a quiet subject's `recorded_at` freezes for hours while its loop is healthy. Corroborated to the second on two live subjects: `manager-layer`'s last CHANGE-WAKE `2026-10-07T12:55:01Z` = its frozen `recorded_at` `2026-10-07T14:55:09+02:00` (330 EMPTY ticks), and `sentry-agent`'s `12:18:46Z` = `14:18:53+02:00` (635 EMPTY ticks). ⚠️ **The heartbeat is written on every tick (`sweep-gate-notify-tick.sh:161`) whatever the digest does**, which is exactly why the two diverge; the doc now names `<vault>/<subject>.heartbeat` as the liveness signal. ⚠️ **The cost of the gap was measured, not hypothetical**: a task row was filed, scoped, and carried through three planning audits on the misreading that a frozen `recorded_at` meant a stopped writer, and only reading the source refuted it — the row is cited in the new text. ⚠️ **Four further in-repo sites asserted the same false cadence and are corrected in the same PR** — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` and `commands/manager-loop.md` both read *"rewritten every tick"*; `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`'s module header read *"written every 900 s"*, and its `--compare` print labelled the value *"gate snapshot recorded_at"*, advertising staleness on the healthy quiet case. The freeze-a-copy advice built on the cadence claim survives — the file can still move between choose and read on a CHANGE-WAKE tick, which is the 2026-09-30 measurement those two cite — but the stated reason is now right, and the print reports *when the digest last changed* rather than how old the snapshot is. The heartbeat is qualified as *the gate loop's*, keeping it apart from the supervisor's worker heartbeat store. Change set: `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.


## v0.115.4
- fix: **the tracked-set attention watcher writes a `LIVENESS` record on every poll, so a correctly quiet arm stops being indistinguishable from a dead one.** Measured 2026-10-06: an arm emitted nothing for **82 minutes** while a gate was present, and the filing could not separate an inert arm from an over-suppressing gate from a genuinely quiet one. Read against the deployed code it was the **third**, and the commit path was never wrong: `is_gated` (`:633`) returns `waiting` and `idle`+closer as gated, but **`busy` mid-turn and `shell` as HELD — membership preserved, never cleared** — so a `waiting → busy` transition is not a clear and owed no `CLEARED`, while the one transition that *was* owed (a genuine settle to `idle` with no ask) **was** emitted. `stable_sessions` (`:781`) likewise only *delays* a real `CLEARED` by one poll, never swallows it — pinned by `test_a_departure_needs_two_polls`. ⚠️ **So the defect was not a wrong event but a missing one.** `events.jsonl` carried transitions only, so a quiet window left the last `NEW GATE` as the newest line and its age was unreadable as anything but a fault — the hazard the runbook names at § Sweep output (*"a quiet loop and a dead loop look identical from the outside"*), which the manager's own tick already answers with a per-tick marker and this arm did not. ⚠️ **`events.jsonl`, never stdout, and that is load-bearing:** every stdout line is a `Monitor` notification and therefore a full model turn, so a per-poll line there would cost ~30 turns per 30-minute arm; the event log is the surface `commands/manager-loop.md` step 7 already tells the manager to read, and nothing consumes it programmatically, so the fourth `kind` is additive rather than a schema change. ⚠️ **The record reports both halves** — `N gated · M held · change|no change` — because a HELD session is exactly what makes a quiet poll *correct* rather than suspicious: a gated count alone reads `0 gated` on a fleet where 24 sessions were mid-turn, which is the incident's own shape reproduced live. `LivenessRecordTest` pins that N quiet polls produce N records, that the subject names the tracked set, and that a committing poll does **not** report `no change`. Change set: `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_attention_watch.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`.

## v0.115.3
- fix: **the manager sweep's topic row now has a defined `Gate` cell for a topic whose `# Completion Gate` is not checkbox items — it renders `—`, matching the gate's own producer on the empty case.** `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` § the `Met` cell stated the topic row's two labelled sets (`SC n/m · Gate n/m`) but never what the `Gate` half contains when the gate is an ordered list rather than `- [ ]` items, so two consecutive ticks rendered the same row two different ways — `Gate n/a` at tick 18 (2026-09-25 23:26 CEST) and `Gate 0/4` at tick 19 (23:30 CEST) on `23 Topics/Unattended Execution.md` — with neither traceable to a written rule. ⚠️ **The frame's own source was the deeper gap:** the Manager Session runbook § Sweep output is what both sweep commands point at as canonical, and it defined only the **task**-row unit, never the topic row at all, so the cell's definition lived solely in the agent downstream of it. **This PR carries the agent half; the companion runbook edit (`65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Sweep output — per-vault, not tracked in this repo) lands separately and states the same rule plus the goal-row unit.** The rule: a `# Completion Gate` that is **not entirely checkbox items** renders the `Gate` half `—` — whether it carries none at all or mixes `- [ ]` items with an ordered list — never `0/0`, never `0/<n>`, and never `n/m` over the checkboxes alone, which would silently drop the numbered items from the denominator. `—` is this frame's value for *nothing recorded*, because counting an ordered list as a denominator with a `0` numerator asserts a reading no source supports. The rule is stated **per half and at both levels**, so a topic row's `SC` half and a goal row's `Met` cell render the same `—` when either carries no checkbox items, rather than being left to a reader's judgement. ⚠️ **The TASK row's `Met` cell is deliberately not covered** — its unit is the TOTAL-checkbox count, and a task carrying no checkbox items at all is a separate question this change does not decide. ⚠️ **`—` is not a new reading for the empty case — it is the one the shipped gate already had:** `.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py`'s `checkbox_count()` returns `—` for a section with no checkboxes *"rather than `0/0`, which would read as a real reading of nothing"*, so the reader is brought to the gate there rather than a third answer being introduced. ⚠️ **The MIXED case is the one place the two producers diverge, and the divergence is named rather than hidden:** `checkbox_count()` finds checkboxes and returns `n/m` over them — silently dropping the numbered items — while this rule renders `—`. Bringing the gate to parity is a follow-on, explicitly not part of deciding the frame, and the rule must not be narrowed to the empty case to restore agreement. ⚠️ **A gate in that shape is out of compliance, not a second supported shape:** `Topic Writing Guide` § Completion Gate's canonical template is `- [ ] <condition>`, so `—` is the frame's signal of that and the fix belongs to the page — no second counting rule was added here. Measured 2026-10-07: of **13** topic pages carrying a `# Completion Gate`, **12** use `- [ ]` items and exactly **1** renders `SC 1/7 · Gate —`. Change set: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.115.2
- docs: **`/supervisor:read-guides`'s printed summary now names the three session tiers and the `/open` rule, and `/supervisor:open` is labelled as the manager's spawn path rather than one option beside a raw `spawn_agent`.** The routing was never missing — `commands/read-guides.md:13` already names `docs/session-tiers.md` as carrying the tiers and the `/open`-is-manager-only rule, and Step 1 reads every `docs/**/*.md` — but the **summary** a session acts on named none of them, so a session that read only its own summary never saw the rule. Measured 2026-09-25: a goal manager proposed `vault-cli work-on` plus a raw `spawn_agent` call before the operator pointed at `/open`. ⚠️ **The fix is a pointer, not a copy.** `commands/read-guides.md:23` forbids restating the command → tier table in terms (*"point the user at `session-tiers.md`; do NOT restate the command → tier table here"*), so Step 3 names the three tiers and the rule and points at the doc for the authoritative wording — naming the tiers is not the table, and the edit must not cross into one. `docs/session-tiers.md` § The manager boundary gains the dispatch rule (`/supervisor:open --task` for a worker on a vault task; a bare manager-spawn only for a brief with no task file — a rule about dispatching a worker onto a task, not a general ban on `spawn_agent`), and `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker gains a lead line labelling it `/open`'s internals rather than a parallel path — the section previously read as an alternative, and its option A calls `spawn_agent` "preferred". Change set: `commands/read-guides.md`, `docs/session-tiers.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.115.1
- fix: **the plugin-version census's `--reload` lever now reaches an idle session instead of refusing every one — the TUI's placeholder hint counts as an empty composer.** The lever shipped in v0.115.0 gated readiness on `server/tab.mjs:isReady()`'s rule verbatim (glyph at line-start on the last glyph line, empty remainder), and the **post-release E2E caught it within minutes of the install**: an idle Claude Code composer reads `❯\xa0\x1b[0;2mTry "how does … work?"`, so `isReady()` answers **false for every idle session** — `node -e "isReady('446')"` against a real pane returns `false` — and all **38** stale sessions came back `composer not empty — pane not idle at a prompt`. ⚠️ **`isReady()`'s rule is right for its own job and wrong for this one**: it gates a **spawn**, where the TUI may still be painting and a submit sent into that phase is swallowed (measured 2026-09-20, two sends stranded), whereas the lever's whole purpose is a **long-idle** session, where the placeholder is always present and the send lands — measured 2026-10-07 on a session spawned for the purpose, `/reload-plugins` into a placeholder composer executed and moved the marker `0.114.1 → 0.115.0`, and the pane echoed `Reloaded: 13 plugins · 127 skills · 126 agents · 1 hook · 1 plugin MCP server · 2 plugin LSP servers`. ⚠️ **The placeholder is drawn dim (SGR attribute 2) and typed text never is**, which is the discriminator the gate now uses: a composer whose remainder is empty **or** dim is ready, so the hazard the gate exists for survives — the operator's unsent answer (2026-10-05, `❯ draft ok` parked 3h22m) is not dim and is still refused rather than concatenated with a reload command. ⚠️ The readiness read is now `get-text --escapes`, because the styling is the discriminator and the plain read does not carry it; `composer_line()` strips SGR before matching the glyph so the position rule is unchanged. ⚠️ **The SGR parse is positional and scoped to the visible text, and the first draft got both wrong** — found in review. A membership test for a bare `2` reads **every truecolor escape as faint** (`38;2;r;g;b` and `38;5;2` both carry a `2` that is a colour sub-parameter), and testing the whole remainder rather than the visible runs calls `❯ \x1b[0;2mghost\x1b[0mdraft ok` empty — each one flips the gate to ready on a composer the operator wrote into, which is the single thing it exists to refuse. `38`/`48` now skip their sub-parameters, `0` resets and `22` cancels faint, and faint must hold for **every** visible character. ⚠️ **A non-zero `get-text` exit is reported as a read failure**, not as a busy pane: discarding the return code made `stdout` empty, `pane_is_ready("")` false, and the loop report `composer not empty` for every stale row — the same collapse-into-empty `_pid_ttys` was fixed for, re-entered under a new cause (an older `wezterm` rejecting `--escapes` lands exactly there). ⚠️ And the suite now asserts `--escapes` is on the argv: without that pin, dropping the flag restores the always-false gate with every test still green. ⚠️ **Three more of the same shape, all from the second review:** the escape pattern now covers what a real read carries — CSI with private/intermediate bytes (`\x1b[?25h`, cursor visibility, which ink emits around the composer), OSC/DCS, charset selection and the remaining two-char escapes — because a narrower pattern leaves those bytes in the visible text where they read as non-faint characters and turn an empty composer into `not ready`; `\x1b[m` is read as the reset it is (an omitted SGR parameter is `0`, not a dropped one — filtering empties left `[]`, no state change, so a bare reset failed to clear faint and a dimmed-then-reset composer kept reading as empty); and a `get-text` **timeout is retried inside the readiness deadline rather than aborting the lever**, since `TimeoutExpired` is a `SubprocessError` and a loaded machine — exactly when a pane is least likely to be idle — would otherwise end the whole run at the first slow read. Change set: `scripts/plugin-version-census.py`, `scripts/tests/test_plugin_version_census.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.115.0
- feat: **`scripts/plugin-version-census.py` reads the whole live fleet's plugin version at once, and `--reload` is the lever that moves it.** The per-session probe already existed (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § *A plugin install does not reach a running session — the probe and the lever*); what did not was the **fleet** reading — how many live sessions are behind, which ones, and how to reach each. Measured 2026-10-07 on the live fleet: **installed 0.114.1 · live 44 · stale 21**, spread over eight versions back to `0.104.0`, with every stale session's jump target resolved through the tty join (`ps -o pid=,tty=` against `wezterm cli list`). ⚠️ **The marker count is not the session count, and reading it as one is the trap.** A reload writes a new `.in_use/<pid>` entry and never removes the old one, so counting markers over the cache reported **17 versions / 81 markers** against a fleet of **44 sessions / 8 versions** — a five-fold overstatement in the direction that looks like more evidence. The reading is the **newest marker carrying a live pid**, the same `-t` rule the per-session probe uses; `NewestMarkerTest` pins it. ⚠️ **Liveness comes from `session-liveness.py`, never from the cache** — the cache keeps a marker for every session that ever ran, so a second registry reader here would be the defect that file's own header documents; the census shells out to it and holds no reading of its own, and an unreadable probe exits 2 rather than reporting `live: 0 · stale: 0` as a healthy fleet. ⚠️ **The same rule now covers the installed-version half**, which the first draft of this entry claimed and the code did not do: `installed_version` returning `None` fell through the `installed is not None` guard and marked **every** row `current`, printing `installed: unknown · live: 44 · stale: 0` — a clean fleet derived from a probe that could not read its own baseline. `unknown` now covers both facts, and `panes_for_pids` propagates `None` from either transport half instead of flattening it into `{}`, so a failed pane read is reported once rather than printed as `no resolvable pane` on every row. ⚠️ **`unknown` is not `stale`** — a headless worker is an in-process `query()` and a cluster worker runs in a pod, so neither necessarily holds a local marker, and naming a fix for a session the lever cannot reach is worse than saying nothing. ⚠️ **The lever reaches TAB sessions only, and says so per row**: `--reload` activates each stale session's pane and types `/reload-plugins`, and a session with no resolvable pane is reported `no resolvable pane` rather than skipped silently — measured 2026-10-07, `boss` runs on a tty no wezterm pane owns and is the standing example. ⚠️ **Success is the NEWEST marker for that pid moving — never the pre-reload version directory's mtime, and never the pane's echo.** The first draft watched `<cache>/<plugin>/<row['loaded']>/.in_use/<pid>`, the **old** version's directory; a reload writes into the *installed* version's directory and leaves the old entry untouched, so that comparison could never fire and every successful reload would have burned the full settle timeout and reported `reload unconfirmed` — per pane, serially. It was invisible in the live measurement because the test session was already on the installed version, where both paths coincide; `ReloadPaneTest` now pins the realistic case (a new marker in a new directory, the old one's mtime unchanged). ⚠️ **Readiness is an EMPTY composer, mirroring `server/tab.mjs:isReady()`, not a substring glyph scan** — the glyph appears while the TUI is still painting a placeholder suggestion, and a submit sent into that phase is swallowed (measured 2026-09-20, two sends stranded); the substring test also matched any echoed prompt in scrollback and so passed on the first poll whatever the pane was doing. ⚠️ **`--reload --no-panes` is now refused at parse time** rather than running a fleet-wide no-op that reports `no resolvable pane` for everything, and `--sessions-json` validates that it holds a list of records instead of dying on an `AttributeError`. ⚠️ **Measured 2026-10-07, and it is why the lever is `send-text` and not a peer message:** a cross-session `SendMessage` asking a session to reload is delivered and *cannot be acted on* — `/reload-plugins` is a built-in CLI command, not a Skill, so the recipient has no tool for it and answers so in as many words; the marker did not move. ⚠️ `_pid_ttys` shipped returning `{}` for a failed `ps` and `scripts/transport-read-check.py` caught it on the first run — the same collapse-into-empty the check exists for, in a script whose entire output is a count; the site is now enumerated and driven three-state alongside `wezterm_panes`. Change set: `scripts/plugin-version-census.py`, `scripts/tests/test_plugin_version_census.py`, `scripts/transport-read-check.py`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `llms.txt`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.114.2
- fix: **a board Allow/Deny on a headless worker's park now releases the gate, instead of recording an answer and releasing nothing.** The board renders the pair on a headless park (`attention-controller` v0.37.0, the 2026-10-01 narrowing) and its JS sends `decision` but never `resolved_by` — so `armDelivery` refused every board answer, `selectParked` never ran, and the worker auto-denied at `PERMISSION_TIMEOUT_MS`. `armDelivery` is now two-sided: an arm answer (`resolved_by`, unchanged), **or** a board answer on a park whose producing agent is `mode: 'headless'`. ⚠️ **The amendment answers this guard's own stated premise rather than deleting it.** Its comment justified the refusal with *"the board renders no answer controls on a `permission` card at all and its JS never sends `decision` … This hardens the path against a DIRECT API POST."* The 2026-10-01 narrowing falsified the first half, so refusing a board answer no longer hardens anything — it removes the only answering surface that park has, which is #296's rule verbatim: a gate the product's primary flow cannot satisfy is not a fix, it is the feature deleted. ⚠️ **Scoped positively, with no fallback** — `mode === 'headless'` exactly. A tab worker's park (whose card renders no answering control at all) still refuses, and an absent, null or unrecognised `mode` refuses rather than defaulting to headless. ⚠️ **A cluster row refuses by ABSENCE, not by recognition** — `cluster` is a spawn *target*, not a spawn mode (`SPAWN_MODES` is `['interactive','headless']`), so a cluster agent record carries no `mode` at all. Same refusal, different mechanism, named so a later reader does not go looking for a place that matches the word `cluster`. ⚠️ **`agent.mode` is now written on the spawn record**, beside the `modeSource` that decided it: `status` cannot answer this — it carries liveness (`running`, `blocked-on-permission`, `done`) and a tab's `interactive` is assigned several lines later, so deriving headless-ness from it would read a not-yet-assigned tab as headless. ⚠️ **The guard still runs BEFORE `selectParked`.** The headless half needs the producing agent, so that join was extracted into `producingAgent()` and is now resolved once for both callers rather than borrowed from `selectParked`'s result. ⚠️ **The placement is load-bearing, not incidental:** `selectParked`'s owner branch records a `failed` delivery attempt, and running the guard *after* it made that record fire for board answers the guard would always have refused — a carrier failure for a delivery nothing attempted, the invariant `attention-poll.test.mjs` pins. The extraction is what lets the guard keep its position and still read the agent. ⚠️ **`agent.mode` is authoritative for this guard and is NOT the ledger's `mode`**, which `writeLedger` still derives from `status`; the two are not yet one derivation, and `agent_status` surfaces only `mode_source`. Recorded rather than smoothed over — a later change should give the mode one home. ⚠️ **The accepted cost, stated rather than implied:** a scripted click on a headless park's Allow now releases a gate that executes a command. That is the same residual the `message` class already carries and the one the falsified-probe finding records, and it is accepted here for the headless case only. Change set: `server/attention-poll.mjs`, `server/attention-poll.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.114.1
- fix: **`permission-answer-poll.py` reuses one connection for the whole poll instead of opening a fresh one per pass.** `urllib.request.AbstractHTTPHandler.do_open` hardcodes `headers["Connection"] = "close"`, so every 2 s pass opened a TCP connection and explicitly asked the store to tear it down — the store offers keep-alive, sends no close signal and frames its responses correctly, so the churn was entirely client-chosen and no server-side change is involved. Measured 2026-10-07 on the live board (`127.0.0.1:18080`): two instances at the 2 s default is ~1-2 connection setups per second, `netstat -an` read **105 `TIME_WAIT` against 124 `ESTABLISHED`** on a bursty sample (34 and 80 minutes earlier — which is why the board's own criterion takes three samples), and the residual idle CPU is `syscall`/`kevent`-dominated rather than handler-dominated, the signature of many cheap requests. The transport is now `http.client.HTTPConnection`, held for the poll loop's whole life by `store_connection()` — a context manager, so it is released on the deadline, the kill switch, an early exit and an exception alike — and **injected** into `get_json()` / `resolve_item_for_session()` rather than constructed per call site. ⚠️ **A transport failure closes the pooled socket before returning**, so a connection the server has since dropped is never reused on the next pass; a non-200 is reported rather than raised, and the body is read *before* the status check so the connection stays reusable. ⚠️ **The test seam moved with the transport, and leaving it would have been silent:** the existing cases patched `get_json` itself, so they never reached a transport and would have kept passing with the old code in place. New `TransportTest` pins the replacement directly — no `Connection` header is sent, one connection carries every request, a good response does not tear the socket down, a failure does, and `store_connection` closes on every path. ⚠️ **The scheme is honoured, not assumed** — `urlopen` supported `https://` and followed redirects, `http.client` does neither, so `store_connection()` branches on the scheme (`HTTPSConnection` for `https`, `HTTPConnection` for `http`, and a `ValueError` for anything else rather than a silent downgrade to plaintext on port 80). ⚠️ **Only a transport failure drops the pooled socket** (`OSError` / `http.client.HTTPException`) — a malformed body on a healthy 200 leaves the connection reusable, so a payload problem no longer pays a reconnect. ⚠️ **The reuse claim is covered by a real server, not a fake** — `test_one_socket_carries_the_whole_poll` drives a genuine `HTTPConnection` against an in-process HTTP/1.1 server and asserts every request arrived from the same client port; a hand-rolled connection double can only show the client does not *ask* for a close. ⚠️ **The dropped-socket recovery — the one failure mode pooling introduces — is pinned against a real server too:** `test_a_socket_dropped_between_passes_recovers` drops the connection **unsignalled** (no `Connection: close` — a keep-alive timeout, the case the client cannot see coming) and asserts the next pass reports rather than raises while the pass after it reconnects; `test_a_signalled_close_is_handled_by_the_client` pins the easier half, that a signalled close costs nothing at all because `http.client` closes its own side and reconnects transparently. Change set: `scripts/permission-answer-poll.py`, `scripts/tests/test_permission_answer_poll.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.114.0
- feat: **the drive leg's cost model names the dispatcher's subagent concurrency cap as a third, transient bound — with re-dispatch as its sole recovery.** Clause (1)'s cost model told a cold tick to audit the full set and named only clause (5)'s two *terminal* bounds (the fleet-wide concurrent limit and the audit budget) as reasons a walk may stop. It was silent on the bound a large set meets first: auditing the full set is one `Task` dispatch per row, and a dispatch past the runner's concurrent-subagent ceiling fails **before the audit runs** (`Concurrent subagent limit reached`). ⚠️ **The cap delays a walk; it does not stop one** — the two existing bounds are terminal (a row walked-and-held is correct), while the cap is transient and its recovery is to **re-dispatch the refused row once slots free**. ⚠️ **`held-on-cap` is not an alternative outcome** — reporting a row held, or letting it reach the end of the tick unaudited, is the exact withholding the cost model already forbids, and it narrows the set by the leg's own plumbing rather than by any property of the row. Clause (5)'s attribution rule now accepts the cap as a bound a tick may *name*, with the verb changed from "stopped" to "delayed". ⚠️ **The carve-out reaches every site that could absorb a refusal** — the `<error_handling>` did-not-bind branch, the `<process>` rule that routes an undecided row to `Held`/`Escalated`, and the sibling `tools:` bind failure — because from the leg's side a cap refusal looks like each of them, and a carve-out applied to only one is satisfied while the others still withhold the row. The class was enumerated rather than patched instance-by-instance — every `Held`/`Escalated` route in the file was classified, and the four unreachable ones (batch provenance, a refused spawn, the below-bar card, the two terminal-bound templates) are named so the next reader need not re-derive them. The `Audit` block's dispatch line gains the refused/re-dispatched count as the cap's named render slot, and the cost model states the retry is **unbounded**: a tick ending with a row unaudited is reported as a failure, never as a `Held` line. A `Cap failure` block in `<output_format>` is that failure's render slot, and the one row shape `<success_criteria>`'s "every row left with exactly one verdict" admits as an exception. The ceiling's own value is deliberately not restated in this file — clause (5)'s single-home rule applies to it too, and the two defaults coincide today, so a copy would be the second counter a `grep` cannot tell from a real one. Measured 2026-09-30 on a 29-row cold tick: 10 first dispatches refused at 20 concurrent, each re-dispatched once, no row audited twice, tick completed in ~5 min. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.113.0
- feat: **the sweep's `✅ DONE — CLOSE:` line gets one stamped producer, so a line that goes stale between emission and the operator's read carries its own re-check.** The line is correct when emitted and can decay to false before it is read — the session can end in between and a printed line cannot be withdrawn. Measured 2026-09-27: a Manager Layer sweep printed a close line for `Extract fleet-loop's Per-Row Pane Read Into fleet-sweep-reader [b7d4c0fe]`, the caller had verified the session live at emission, and ~10 minutes later the registry entry was gone, `ps` reported nothing and no window pane matched. New `scripts/close-line.py` is that line's single producer: it emits `✅ DONE — CLOSE: <task> [<sid8>] — verified <ISO-ts> · re-check: <cmd> · run /vault-cli:sync-progress + /vault-cli:session-close`, so the three render sites — `sweep-gate.py`'s `action_lines()` and the two manager print steps (`commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`) — cannot drift and a bare close instruction has no door left. ⚠️ **`--liveness` is the caller's own verdict, taken this run, and this script never re-derives it** (`session-liveness.py` is the instrument): `live` and `parked` emit the runnable line at exit 0, `absent` and `unknown` emit the candidate at exit 1 — the two share an exit code because they share a rendered shape, and the caller holds the distinction and is the one that acts on it. ⚠️ **The re-check embeds the FULL session id, never the 8-char prefix** — a prefix is the measured trap on the sibling read, returning `UNREADABLE`, which is the branch that says "act as before". ⚠️ **The plugin root is resolved, not inherited**: the gate is vault-local and does not inherit `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, so a path it must embed in a *runnable* command is resolved the way that file already resolves its `BOX_TABLE` — an absolute `expanduser` path, overridable by `--plugin-root` / `CLOSE_LINE_PLUGIN_ROOT` so a fixture need not read the operator's real install. ⚠️ **The stamp is the emission moment, taken here, not the caller's verdict time** — a caller whose verdict is already minutes old emits a line that says so honestly, and the embedded re-check is what settles it for the reader. 11 tests in `scripts/tests/test_close_line.py`, including the one that pins the defect itself: no liveness value yields a close instruction a reader cannot settle. Change set: `scripts/close-line.py`, `scripts/tests/test_close_line.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.112.2
- fix: **a pane-less store-vouched gate now reaches the feed, so a parked headless worker's permission is visible to the operator instead of dying with its 15-minute auto-deny.** `is_live()`'s pane clause (`str(rec["pane"]) not in pmap`) is what leaked the orphans, and it is correct for a row that HAS a pane — but `""` is never a pane key, so it also dropped every row whose session owns no pane at all. A headless worker does that by construction: `owned_pane()` refuses an unprovable pane, so its hook record carries `pane: ""`. Measured 2026-10-06: a parked headless worker's item sat `open`, `permission`-class, `provenance_class: hook` in the store, and **three consecutive reader runs started inside its open window** rendered no row for it in any section (`Needs you (0)` · `Rendered panels (0–1, the pane-9 closer line only)` · `Probably stuck > 20m (0)` · `Reapable (0)` · `Nothing needs you.`), with the probe's session id, item id and probe-file name absent from all 30 captured runs. ⚠️ **The fix is an exemption, not a removal.** The pane clause still drops every row that carries a pane, and the exemption needs BOTH an empty pane AND `store_vouched` — the second half is load-bearing: the store resolves producer liveness server-side and drops dead askers as a side effect of the same read (`store_items()`), so a row it returned has already had its liveness decided by the component that owns that question. Re-judging it here is the same worse-opinion defect `store_vouched` already corrects for transcript staleness (`quiet_session_ids()`), and an unvouched pane-less log row therefore still follows the orphan rule. ⚠️ **No new rendering was needed** — `row()`'s pane-less branch already emits `unroutable — no pane recorded for this item` followed by `answer_handover()`'s `answer: python3 … answer <store item_id> --decision allow|deny`; the row simply never reached it, one filter earlier. ⚠️ **Two existing assertions moved, deliberately, and they are not the same kind of change.** `test_store_row_without_a_pane_is_dropped_today` pinned the defect as current behaviour and its own docstring named it *"the current, wrong behaviour the fix must change"* — renamed `test_store_row_without_a_pane_renders_pane_less`, now asserting the row renders. `test_unnamed_session_claims_no_pane` keeps its safety assertion (`assertIsNone(rec["pane"])` — no pane is ever guessed) and only its *consequence* flips: such a row now renders with the answering command where it has no jump to give. ⚠️ **The regression check is discriminating, verified against the pre-fix reader**: the new `OrphanLiveness` tests fail there **on the assertion** (`AssertionError: False is not true : a pane-less store-vouched gate must render`), not by failing to import and not on a symbol-existence check — while the unvouched-drop guard and the pane-backed control pass on both sides, so the check cannot be satisfied by "render everything". ⚠️ **The predicate is shared, and one sibling consumer is pane-keyed.** `scripts/jump.py` borrows `is_live()` for its rotation queue, so admitting pane-less rows there would have made a bare `jump` resolve `pmap[""]["tab_id"]` → None → print `attention row has pane , not in the live pane list` and exit 1 — on a fresh headless gate, which is exactly the row this change adds. `jump` narrows the borrowed predicate at its own boundary (`and bool(r.get("pane"))`) rather than the feed widening the shared one: the feed renders such a row with the `answer:` handover, while `jump` has nothing to jump to. **Caught in review, not by the tests** — and the review's second finding named why the tests missed it: `test_pane_backed_row_still_carries_its_own_jump` carries no `store_vouched` and its pane is a key of the map, so the pane clause was never reached in the failing direction; `test_store_vouched_row_with_a_dead_pane_is_still_dropped` now pins it. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/jump.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_me.py`.

## v0.112.1
- fix: **a failed closer read no longer drops a gated session's membership, so the `CLEARED` its later settle earns is no longer lost.** `is_gated()` returns `(None, "closer-unknown")` from two producers — the registered `_CLOSER_UNKNOWN` short-circuit ahead of the status dispatch, and the `status is None` headless-live limb — whenever the transcript tail holds no text block at all. `transitions()` preserved membership only for reasons prefixed `held:`, so `closer-unknown` took the UNREGISTERED arm: it emitted a `HELD … ; no CLEARED emitted` record and `main` discarded the session from `prev`. The gate was then forgotten, so the genuine settle at `idle` with no ask was not a transition at all and printed nothing — a false clear replaced by no clear. Measured at the commit loop before the fix: `NEW GATE` 1, `CLEARED` 0, committed membership `[]`; after, `CLEARED` 1. New `is_hold(reason)` names the membership-preserving set — `held:<status>` (mid-turn) and `closer-unknown` (a failed read) — so `transitions()` agrees with the HELD verdict `is_gated()` already returned for it ("a failed read must not be representable as an empty result"). ⚠️ **`unregistered` is deliberately NOT in that set, which is why this is a named predicate rather than a `verdict is None` test:** it is also a `None` verdict, but there is nothing left to hold, so it keeps dropping its membership and emitting its record. Collapsing every `None` reason would swallow that record — a different defect from the one this closes. ⚠️ **Both producers preserve, and they coincide because the operative fact is the same, not because the code paths were unified** — each keeps its own return site and its own reason string, so a later divergence stays possible without unpicking a shared branch. The accepted failure direction is a gate left open by a tail that never becomes readable again (a fixed 400 KB window); the rejected alternative cost the genuine answer outright. Change set: `CHANGELOG.md`, `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_attention_watch.py`.

## v0.112.0
- feat: **an exited manager's live workers are adopted on the fleet tick, so their gates stop reaching every watcher and being owned by none.** `gate-owner-filter.py` resolves ownership from the spawn edge, so a worker whose `parent_session` points at a session that is gone keeps reading as a manager's worker: hop 4 finds the spawner absent from the registry and the pane is emitted `dead-manager` — deliberately kept rather than dropped, because a dead manager's panes are nobody's to route. Measured 2026-10-06 20:27–21:01: spawner `b34530bd` (exited) stranded panes 192 and 188, spawner `f37d6f6a` (exited) stranded pane 283, and Attention Manager and UI Manager each escalated the same gates independently. New `scripts/adopt-orphans.py` runs on `/supervisor:fleet-loop`'s new Step 3a and records each adoption through `ownership-claim.py claim` — the store the filter's claim arm already reads — so no second routing path is introduced, and `parent_session` is never rewritten (the filter documents re-parenting as falsifying provenance). ⚠️ **The adopter is a same-subject live successor when exactly one exists, else the session running the tick.** Subject comes from `~/.claude/state/worker-manager/<sid>.json`, the record `docs/subject-resolution.md` § Recording writes; zero matches, **more than one** match, and a missing subject file all fall back rather than pick, because two same-subject managers are indistinguishable and guessing would silence one of them. The no-subject case is the common one, not an edge: measured against the two managers above, `b34530bd` has a subject file and `f37d6f6a` has none. ⚠️ **Liveness is registry ∪ heartbeat, built from `session-liveness.py` rather than `worker-sessions.live_workers()`** — that function's `resumed_from` exclusion belongs to the fleet's spawn-target count, and an auto-resumed worker whose manager exited is stranded like any other. ⚠️ **A worker a LIVE manager already holds is not stranded, and the round goes quiet.** The stranded set is derived from the spawn edge, which does not change when a worker is adopted — the manager stays exited — so without reading the claims store every later tick would reprint the same exit line and a zero adoption count forever. A claim held by a manager that is GONE is not ownership: the filter fails open on a dead holder, and so does this. ⚠️ **Three refusals exit 1 and adopt nothing, and their stderr lines differ**: an unreadable registry or heartbeat store (a liveness-unknown reading — reading it as empty would make every manager look exited and hand the fleet's workers to the fallback on no evidence), an unreadable spawn-ledger directory, and no tick session id at all, which is checked before any store is read. ⚠️ **A tick session id is required** — `--self`, or `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` by default: it is the fallback adopter, so a round that cannot name itself refuses rather than adopting into the void. Verified against the live fleet before wiring it in: the script reproduces the incident exactly — `b34530bd` → 2 workers, `f37d6f6a` → 1. 21 tests in `scripts/tests/test_adopt_orphans.py` — all three exit-1 refusals pinned, plus the empty-ledger control that keeps a clean round distinguishable from a refusal; the exit-3 (already-held) branch is driven **in-process**, because `ownership-claim.py` resolves a holder's liveness against the real registry and offers no override, so no fixture can reach it through the CLI — and the same fact is why the dead-holder test asserts only that the row is *reported*, never that the claim was taken over: a takeover assertion passes on a laptop and fails in CI, which is exactly what it did (measured 2026-10-06, one failure out of 1682). Change set: `scripts/adopt-orphans.py`, `scripts/tests/test_adopt_orphans.py`, `commands/fleet-loop.md`, `CLAUDE.md`.

## v0.111.6
- fix: **the resume guard prunes the rehydrated roster before it reads it, so a surviving rehydrated row holds the guard instead of being filtered out wholesale.** The rehydration change excluded rehydrated rows from `findLiveHolder` (`[...agents.values()].filter((a) => !a.rehydrated)`) to stop a DEAD worker's row asserting it was still mid-turn and refusing the resume that is its only recovery; `pruneRehydratedAgents()` ran on the read paths only, so the resume path never saw it, and the guard's comment then asserted the opposite of what its code did. The fix is the shape the filter was standing in for: **prune first, then let the guard see every row**. ⚠️ **This is defence in depth, not the repair of a live-worker resume — an earlier draft of this entry claimed the latter and was wrong.** `spawn_agent`'s resume path already calls `checkLiveness(resume)` (`server/supervisor.mjs:1113`) *before* the guard, over the same registry ∪ heartbeat channels and the same TTL, and refuses a live session at `:1116`; `supervisor.mjs`'s own comment already calls the guard "a second line, not the first". So no live worker was reachable through this path. What the guard is the only line for is a worker the registry cannot see — and its contract still had to be correct. Measured 2026-10-06: after a `/mcp` reconnect a parked headless worker appeared in the rehydrated roster as `rehydrated: true`, `status: running`, `current_tool_call` held 201 s, `awaiting_input: true`; that row is what the guard now sees. ⚠️ **"Every surviving row is live" is an age proxy, not a proof.** Liveness is a heartbeat stamp read against the 60 s `HEARTBEAT_TTL_MS` or a pid-checked registry entry, so a dead worker's row can hold the guard for up to 60 s after its owner dies — the deadlock the deleted filter existed to prevent, now bounded and self-healing on retry rather than immediate. And when `liveSessionIds()` answers `null` every row is kept, which is the fail-closed direction but a weaker property than the comment used to claim. Change set: `CHANGELOG.md`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `server/registry-rehydrate.mjs`, `server/registry-rehydrate.test.mjs`.

## v0.111.5
- docs: **the `Makefile` now names the install step `make precommit` needs, so a fresh worktree's failure points at its own fix.** `node --test` dies with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND: Cannot find package '@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk'` when `server/node_modules` is absent — while every `check-*` guard above it still passes, so the run reads as a broken test rather than a missing install, and the error names neither the step nor the directory. Measured 2026-10-06 in a fresh worktree off `origin/master`: all guards green, Node suite failed. ⚠️ **The install belongs inside `server/`, not at the repo root.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:24` (`npm install --no-audit --no-fund`) is paired with `working-directory: server` on the line after it, and the repo has no root `package.json` — only `server/package.json` — so a root install *succeeds* while resolving nothing and the identical error returns, with no feedback that the directory was wrong. `server/package.json`'s own `start` script (`bun install --no-summary && node supervisor.mjs`) works from `server/` too. ⚠️ **The first draft of this entry stated the opposite directory** and was caught in review; the correction is recorded because the wrong version reads exactly as confidently as the right one. Comment only; no behaviour change.
## v0.111.4
- fix: **a reconnected supervisor server rebuilds its roster from the ledger, so a manager stops losing sight of its own workers.** `agents` in `server/supervisor.mjs` is populated only by `spawn_agent`, so `/mcp` — which starts a NEW server process — left `list_agents` answering `[]` and `agent_status` answering `unknown agent <id>` while the worker sessions themselves were still running (measured 2026-10-05, BRO-21389 goal manager). The durable half already existed and nothing read it: `ledger.mjs` carries `agent_id` on every record, and `park-record.mjs` states the principle for a park — "the spawner exiting is exactly the case that matters, so the park must also land on disk". New `server/registry-rehydrate.mjs` joins the ledger to liveness at startup; `grep -rni 'rehydrat|restorePending|loadPending' server/` returned zero hits before this. ⚠️ **The ledger's own `status` is never liveness** — 1110 of 1372 records measured 2026-10-06 still asserted `running`, because nothing closes an entry whose server went away, so adopting on the ledger's word would resurrect every dead worker and make `agent_status` answer for ids that must read `unknown agent`. Identity comes from the ledger, liveness from the registry ∪ heartbeat — the same split `worker-sessions.mjs` already makes. ⚠️ **The join is shared with `workerSessions()`; its `resumed_from` FILTER is not** — that filter is right for the fleet-wide cap counter and wrong for a roster, where a resumed worker is still one the manager has to see and drive. ⚠️ A rehydrated record carries empty `transcript`/`permissions` arrays, because `agentView` dereferences `a.transcript.length` unconditionally and an absent field would take the whole roster down rather than one row. ⚠️ **`answer_permission` is deliberately NOT part of the recovery** — its `settle` is a closure over a promise that died with the spawning process (`server/supervisor.mjs:415`), so a rehydrated `pending` would hand back a record whose settle is inert, which is worse than the bug; the supported path is carry-not-settle via `settleFromCarried`. ⚠️ **A rehydrated row is a BOOT-TIME reading, so three call sites had to learn about it** — all three found in review, not written correctly the first time: the row is **pruned at read time** (`pruneRehydratedAgents`, before `list_agents`/`agent_status` answer) so a worker that dies after boot leaves the roster; it is **excluded from `findLiveHolder`**, because its status is never terminal and leaving it in would let a dead worker assert it is still mid-turn and refuse the resume that is its only recovery, permanently; and it is **skipped by `stampUnobservedWorkers`**, which would otherwise write `unknown` about a worker this process never watched. ⚠️ **The registry half of the join is pid-checked**, deliberately diverging from `workerSessions()`: that reader takes presence as liveness because over-counting a *cap* fails safe, while adopting a row into a *roster* is the opposite direction and would resurrect a crashed session's left-behind file. ⚠️ **A duplicate `agent_id` is resolved first-wins over a sorted ledger and logged** — `spawn_agent` mints ids from a per-process counter, so a reconnect restarts it and two live records can share one id, which would otherwise put one Map key on two workers. ⚠️ **`shipping` is hardcoded `false`, and it is unrecoverable rather than merely unmapped** — `buildRecord` never persisted the flag, so a rehydrated row for a worker running under `SHIPPING_PERMISSION_MODE` reports `shipping: false`; named here because it is the one gap this change cannot close. ⚠️ **The second review found the half of the id collision the first one missed.** The `claimed` map resolved a clash between two LEDGER records, but the bigger half was unhandled: adopted ids share a namespace with this process's own mints. `spawn_agent` numbers workers from a per-process counter that restarts at 0 on every reconnect, so the first spawn after a reconnect would mint an id a rehydrated row already held, and `agents.set` would silently overwrite a **live** worker's row — `list_agents` dropping it and `agent_status` answering `unknown agent`, the exact symptom this change closes. The mint now advances past anything already held (`do … while (agents.has(id))`), which is collision-free whatever populated the Map rather than only for the ledger-vs-ledger case. ⚠️ And the loser of a genuine ledger-vs-ledger clash is now **disambiguated (`agent_1~<sid8>`) rather than dropped** — dropping it left a live worker invisible to `list_agents`, `agent_status` and `send_agent_message` for the process's whole life, which is the same availability outcome this change exists to remove, only deterministic and logged. ⚠️ **The third review's two nits are taken rather than waived**, because each names a hazard this change is otherwise careful about: the registry ∪ heartbeat union now has **ONE home** (`liveSessionIds` in `liveness.mjs`), so the cap counter and the roster cannot drift into answering "is this session live?" differently — `isAlive` is the single documented input on which they differ (presence for a *cap*, which fails safe by refusing a spawn; pid-check for a *roster*, which must not resurrect a crashed session's left-behind file); and `log` now defaults to `console.warn` rather than a no-op, so the duplicate-id warning cannot be silently discarded by a caller that forgets the argument. ⚠️ **The fourth review's `Should Fix` is taken as well:** the `rehydrated` marker now reaches `agentView`, so a manager can tell a row adopted from the ledger from one this process spawned. Without it, three fields a caller *does* see read as measurements when they are not — `status` is never terminal, `shipping` is unrecoverable, and `pending_permissions: []` is indistinguishable from "no gate outstanding", which is the exact reading behind the v0.110.4 incident where a manager directed a worker whose gate the operator had already answered. ⚠️ **The fifth review's `Should Fix` is named rather than fixed, deliberately:** adoption is a boot-time snapshot, so two classes fall outside it — a live **cluster** worker (no registry entry, and its only liveness channel is this server's own `pollCluster()` stamp, which first fires ~30s after boot) and a **headless** worker whose stamp aged out past the 60s TTL. Neither is a regression (before this change nothing was adopted), and the module header now names both rather than letting a reader take the roster for complete. Its two nits are taken: `liveSessionIds`' `isAlive` is now **required** rather than defaulting to presence — which is the opposite default from `registeredAsLive` nine lines below, on the same parameter name — and its `heartbeatDir` uses `??` so the `null` idiom `checkLiveness` still uses cannot reach `listLive({ dir: null })`. Change set: `server/liveness.mjs`, `server/liveness.test.mjs`, `server/worker-sessions.mjs`, `server/registry-rehydrate.mjs`, `server/registry-rehydrate.test.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`.
## v0.111.3
- fix: **a topic page's `## Goals` member list is no longer truncated at a `###` sub-heading, so entries the gate never tracked now render.** `declared_members()`'s section regex terminated on `(?=\n#{2,3} |…)`, which stops the section at the first `###` — and a topic page legitimately carries `###` prose notes and phase sub-headings *inside* `## Goals`. Measured 2026-10-06: `23 Topics/Manager Layer.md` holds four such notes (the first at line 400), so the gate admitted **52** of the section's **59** entries and **7 real member entries — every one shaped `**a task entry, admitted directly**`, identical to the 52 above them — were invisible to the tracked set**; `23 Topics/Unattended Execution.md` splits its membership into `### Phase 1` / `### Phase 2` and so rendered **zero** members where its own prose declares *"Membership is whole-goal"*. The lookahead now terminates on `##` only. ⚠️ **Paired change, and it is not optional:** the same lookahead is duplicated in `scripts/manager-predispatch.py` and `scripts/reset.py` — **both in this repo and both changed here** — and in a **third** copy that is **not in this repository**: the vault's own gate at `<vault>/.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py`, tracked in **`bborbe/obsidian-personal`**. A grep from this repo therefore finds only two, which is expected rather than a missing file. All three must carry the same boundary, or the vault gate tracks 59 members on Manager Layer while the predispatch and `reset` still track 52 — a divergence silent in exactly the direction that matters. ⚠️ **The boundary is not novel:** `scripts/fleet-board.py` has always stopped its `## Goals` read at `## ` (`_goals_section_links`, *"Stops at the next `## ` heading"*) and so never truncated — this change brings the other copies into line with it. ⚠️ **Verified on disk:** Manager Layer `declared_members()` **52 → 59**; Unattended Execution **0 → 4**; the deliberately-excluded goal `Automate General Alerts Monitoring` **stays excluded** (its bullet is `- **The lane to automate — [[…]]**`, which `_LIST_ITEM` never matched); the five sampled previously-omitted entries render with `Met` cells equal to the gate's own `_CHECKBOX` regex (`0/22`, `0/12`, `0/9`); and the gate's marker count agrees with its own rendered task-row count (`186` = `186`). ⚠️ **Regression control added** (`scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py` § `DeclaredMembersBoundary`), on the reviewer's recommendation: the fixture's `## Goals` carried no `###`, so the boundary was pinned by no assertion and could have regressed 59 → 52 with a fully green suite. The class asserts **both halves** — a member below a `###` IS admitted, **and** a member under a later `##` is still excluded (without the second, "read to the end of the document" would pass the first and make every later section a membership list). It also covers `reset.py:members_of`, which had **no coverage at all**. Verified discriminating: the old lookahead returns `['BeforeTheNote']` on the fixture where the new one returns `['BeforeTheNote', 'AfterTheNote']`. ⚠️ **Residual, deliberately not fixed here:** the widened capture now also sees bullets under a `###` note, so a removal-shaped `- [[X]] (removed 2026-10-01)` would be admitted as a member. `fleet-board.py`'s docstring records that as a **measured** false positive (*"a paragraph recording that a goal was removed from the list names it … claimed that goal as a member"*), so it is a real class rather than a hypothetical; an explicit removal-marker guard is left as follow-up rather than folded into this boundary fix.

## v0.111.2
- fix: **the manager reconciles its subject page's own `status` at resolution, so a goal or topic is never swept while its page still reads `todo`.** `docs/subject-resolution.md` gains § *Reconcile the subject's status*: read the subject page's frontmatter `status` and, when it reads `todo`/`next` or is absent, set it to `in_progress` via `vault-cli goal set` (goal branch) or `vault-cli topic set` (topic branch). Measured 2026-10-06: a tick resolved the goal `Attention Controller Ultra-Fast Reads`, swept its declared set and printed a table every interval while the goal page itself read `status: todo` and a task under it ran at `phase: execution` with a live worker — every reader (the operator, the sweep table, the model-free gate) saw queued work where there was running work, and the operator flipped the page by hand. ⚠️ **It rides the "resolve once at manager start" rule, not the sweep**, so a page the operator deliberately set back to `next` is not re-flipped on the next firing with nothing saying so; and it writes `in_progress` over `todo`/`next`/absent **only**, leaving `hold`, `backlog` and the terminal statuses alone (`hold` marks a block with no scheduled resume date, so overwriting it would erase the only record that the subject is parked). ⚠️ **Write-scoped, never resolution-scoped:** `/manager-loop` and `/manager-drive` run it; `/manager-status` is read-only by its own contract (*"No vault writes"*) and `/manager-verify` holds no `Bash(vault-cli:*)` — both resolve the subject and neither writes its status, so their absence of the reconcile is a carve-out rather than a gap.
- docs: `commands/manager-loop.md` § Resolution step G and the `commands/manager-status.md` copy re-scope *"never tick SC or flip the goal's status yourself"* to **closure**, which is what it always governed. Read as a blanket ban on `status` writes it forbade the resolution-time reconcile above and left the measured defect standing. ⚠️ **The same scoping is applied out-of-band to the operator's vault runbook `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md`** — § Step 0 gains the rule and the runbook's own closure clause is scoped to match — but that file lives in the vault, not in this repository, so it is not part of this diff and cannot be reviewed here. `commands/manager-verify.md` records the reconcile as its third unavailable block, alongside the vault lookup and the recording write, so its silence there reads as a refusal rather than an omission. ⚠️ **The invocation sits *above* the recording step, outside the block `check-recording-step.py` compares** — that guard requires the three commands' recording blocks to be identical bar three enumerated slots, and `/manager-status` must not carry an imperative it is not permitted to run; the two requirements only coexist when the reconcile is its own paragraph rather than a fourth sentence inside the recording step.
- test: **the reconcile's write-scope table is now enforced by `scripts/check-subject-write-scope.py`.** The table is a contract with four call sites — two writers carrying the invocation, two carve-outs — and nothing tied it to the command bodies, so a writer losing its invocation, or a non-writer regaining `Bash(vault-cli:*)`, would have made it silently false. The guard asserts the table names all four resolving commands, each row's verdict matches what that command's body actually does, every non-writer still points at the section (so its silence reads as a stated carve-out rather than a missing step), the two writers' paragraphs match outside the step slot, and the stated *"holds no `Bash(vault-cli:*)`"* reason still holds against the frontmatter. Wired into `precommit` beside its siblings; `scripts/tests/test_check_subject_write_scope.py` pins every assertion with a mutation that must fail — including the fail-closed paths, since an assertion that cannot run reports green against text it does not cover. ⚠️ **It also asserts the two writers hold the binaries the rule needs** (`Bash(awk:*)` and `Bash(vault-cli:*)`) — the guarantee that shipped false for `commands/manager-drive.md`, which carried the invocation without the `awk` grant the status read requires; `awk` is added to its `allowed-tools` here, alongside the two sibling resolvers that already held it. ⚠️ **The section carries no runnable snippet, deliberately.** It carried one through six review rounds and it was wrong a different way each time: inputs never assigned anywhere in the tree, an `awk` that never matched a CRLF delimiter, an external `tr`/`sed` neither writer is granted, a branch test whose `else` silently made the goal write the default, and a read keyed on the resolved page while the write was keyed on the raw name. **A markdown block is not executed by `make test`**, so every one of those was a claim nothing could check — while reading as authoritative the whole time, which is the failure mode rather than a footnote to it. The guarantees are stated as things to satisfy instead: read the page the page test found, refuse on an unreadable page *before* reading (every path that leaves the status empty for a reason other than a genuinely absent field takes the same write arm), normalise CR / trailing whitespace / case, branch on the detected branch and write through that branch's own command, derive the name from the page actually read, and use only binaries both writers are granted.

## v0.111.1
- fix: **a manager tick now names the command text it is executing, beside the version installed — so a manager can tell the plugin has moved on under it.** A plugin command body is read from disk **when the session starts** and never changes under it, so a long-lived manager keeps executing the text it loaded while the fleet installs newer versions beneath it — and nothing in the session, in the tick, or in the leg's report said so. The probe already shipped (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § *A plugin install does not reach a running session — the probe and the lever*); what was missing is that **no tick ran it**. `commands/manager-loop.md` step 4 now opens with a version line: run the probe, print the **executing** version — read from the `.in_use/"$CLAUDE_PID"` path, never echoed from the installed value — beside the **installed** one, name the divergence explicitly where they differ, and report agreement without a warning where they match. ⚠️ **The failure this removes is not cosmetic:** measured **2026-10-06** while fixing this very row, a manager session executed **0.110.1** text against **0.110.3** installed and had spent the session auditing a defect that had already shipped. ⚠️ **The probe is cited, never restated** — that section declares itself *"the single statement of the rule"*. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- fix: **the drive-leg dispatch hands the reap leg a full session UUID, not the roster's 8-character prefix — a prefix made `reap-closer.py` report a closed session as unreadable.** `scripts/reap-closer.py` globs `{id}.jsonl` **exactly**, so a prefix returns `UNREADABLE (transcripts found: 0)` at **exit 2** where the full UUID returns `CLOSED …` at **exit 0** — and `UNREADABLE` is the branch that means *"reap as before"*, so the prefix turned a session that had **already closed clean** into a reap candidate, the one outcome that read exists to prevent. `commands/manager-loop.md`'s drive-leg dispatch said only `roster verbatim`, and `ListAgents` rows carry abbreviated references; the dispatch now names the form and points at `agents/manager-drive.md`'s fourth read, which owns the rule — the id is read from the row's id set **by `scripts/session-ids.py`**, never by a prose hand-read and never from the roster row, with the script's refusal branch and the genuinely-empty-set case both named. Measured **2026-10-06**, reproduced on demand: `reap-closer.py 5a63e3e8` → `UNREADABLE (transcripts found: 0)` exit 2; `reap-closer.py 5a63e3e8-1539-455a-8c1e-ec96d01e51a8` → `CLOSED 2026-10-01T14:49:32.454Z 👤 You: nothing — session closed` exit 0. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `agents/manager-drive.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.111.0
- fix: **the gate relay no longer reads a composer holding unsubmitted text as a cleared gate — on either leg.** Both `agents/gate-relay-send.md` and `agents/gate-relay-read.md` filed *"the composer already holding an answer"* as a no-gate condition, so a pane whose input box held a typed-but-unsent answer was reported as resolved: the send leg returned `not sent (gate cleared)` with zero mutations, and the read leg returned `no-gate`, which tells the manager there is nothing to ask. Measured 2026-10-05 on the BRO-21389 MDM-via-REST manager session: a worker parked **3h22m** on `review: … reply \`draft ok\`` while `❯ draft ok` sat unsubmitted in pane 116 — the relay reported the gate cleared, and the worker stayed stopped until the operator pressed Enter by hand. ⚠️ **A composer holding text is a gate that has NOT been released** — the answer is present, the keystroke that delivers it is not — and it is the *silent* direction of the failure, so the manager stops tracking a worker whose entire remaining work is one human keystroke. ⚠️ **The send leg now requires a provably EMPTY composer before it may call a gate cleared**, and a non-empty one is its own outcome: `not sent (gate not cleared)`, the composer line quoted, zero mutations, jump link — never a fall-through to the send step. ⚠️ **That fall-through would have been a NEW defect, not a neutral omission:** `send-text` appends, so a send onto a dirty box submits a concatenated message that is neither the operator's answer nor the agent's — the mechanism the sibling topic-manager relay already records (2026-09-17, pane 113). ⚠️ **Zero mutation is load-bearing here, not merely cautious** — the box may hold the operator's OWN typed answer, so clearing it would destroy the very input that was waiting for a keystroke. ⚠️ **The empty-composer test keeps its one home** in the Manager Session runbook § Auto-compaction (the composer prompt is `❯` + **U+00A0**, never a plain space, and a pane can hold more than one `❯`) rather than being restated here. ⚠️ **The read leg is the UPSTREAM half, fixed in lockstep** — the manager's verdict comes from its `kind`, so leaving `no-gate` intact would have left the same misreading reachable before the send leg was ever dispatched; its asking-closer rule no longer requires an empty composer, and a non-empty composer is now never `no-gate`. ⚠️ **`commands/manager-loop.md` § Path-B relay rules is tightened in the same change** because its gloss — *"reports `not sent` with no pane mutation when the gate cleared"* — is correct today and becomes wrong as a direct consequence of this fix, re-teaching the misreading through the manager's own instruction. ⚠️ **The handover half is untouched by design** — the modal-marker / wizard-strip scan is the reference the composer half is measured against. Change set: `agents/gate-relay-send.md`, `agents/gate-relay-read.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`.

## v0.110.6
- fix: **the manager's liveness probe can no longer read a `task_identifier` as a session id — the extraction is now a script that refuses, not a prose rule the model is asked to remember.** `commands/manager-loop.md` forbade the bare-uuid *shape* match in prose from 2026-10-05, and prose is executed by the same model the rule constrains, so it held only while the model remembered to apply it. `scripts/session-ids.py` is the check: the probe calls it for the id set instead of extracting in prose, it reads the set by field (`claude_session_id` plus every `metrics_sessions` id — imported from `manager-predispatch.py`'s `session_id_set()`, never re-implemented, so the repo keeps one home for which fields are a session id), and it **refuses** — `REJECTED:` on stderr, exit 1 — when a `task_identifier` reaches the set. ⚠️ **Refusing is the point, and filtering would have been the wrong fix:** a filtered set is one a caller believes was read correctly, which is the silent direction this whole change exists to remove. ⚠️ **The shape mode is unreachable, not banned** — the script has no shape mode, and `--shape` is itself refused with the reason, so a caller who reaches for the sweep is told why rather than served. ⚠️ **The extraction leaving the model's hands is what makes the phantom shape impossible by construction rather than merely discouraged.** A check living only in `scripts/` would have gated nothing: the probe ran only `session-liveness.py --check <id>` per id and extracted the set itself, so the script would never have been on the path that runs — that was the reachability gap, and pointing the command at the script is the half that closes it. ⚠️ **An unreadable file is `UNREADABLE:` (exit 2), never an empty set** — an empty set reads as *"this task has no session"*, which silently drops a live row out of `ready-to-start`; and a refusal is a verdict about the extraction, so neither the manager nor the reader may fall back to a hand-read set, a rule the command files and the script's own docstring both state. ⚠️ **Every call site invokes it as `python3 <path>`** — a bare-path exec matches no granted tool prefix, so in `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` (grant `Bash(python3:*)`) it could not have fired at all. Measured 2026-09-30 and reproduced 2026-10-06 on the BRO-21389 tracked set (35 tasks, from the recorded snapshot): a bare-uuid shape match over the frontmatter yielded a **54-id population, 34 of them `task_identifier` phantoms**, against **11 tasks carrying a session id in the snapshot** (14 on today's frontmatter — session ids are appended over a task's life, so that count is the snapshot's and drifts) — the shape match sweeps in exactly one `task_identifier` per tracked task, which is why the phantom count tracks the tracked-set size rather than any property of the tasks. ⚠️ **The sweep reader's own extraction is aligned in the same change** — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 3 is the single home of the prose rule and the reader extracts the same set, so leaving it prose-only would have kept the hole open on the path the manager delegates its task-file read to; all eleven prose sites across the two commands and the agent now name the script. Change set: `scripts/session-ids.py`, `scripts/tests/test_session_ids.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`.
## v0.110.5
- docs: **`progress_hash`'s derivation now has a home in its own docstring, so a reader outside `manager-predispatch.py` can reproduce a stored value instead of guessing at one — and both of its boundaries are pinned by tests.** The field is persisted in every snapshot (`docs/fleet-surface.md` § the model-free gate loop lists it in the schema) and a stored value is unreadable to anyone who cannot recompute it; the docstring said only *"a hash of the task's `# Progress` section"* and named neither the algorithm nor the boundary. It now states the derivation — `sha256` over the **raw bytes of `_PROGRESS` group 1**, truncated to the **first 16 hex**, nothing normalised — and spells out the two boundaries, **each of which sits one byte off the obvious reading**. ⚠️ **The end boundary excludes the delimiter newline:** `foo\n\n# Other` hashes `foo\n` (the blank line is dropped) and `foo\n# Other` hashes `foo` (the content line's own newline goes with it). ⚠️ **The start boundary is not "after the heading" when the section is empty and followed by another heading:** `\s*` is greedy and backtracks to leave one newline for the literal `\n`, so group 1 starts at that content and hashes *that* section's body — only an absent section, or one running to EOF, hashes the empty string. The driver is `\s*` greed rather than heading-ness, so it holds before plain prose too, and a `#Other` with no space does not end a section at all. ⚠️ **That is a false "progress" signal, not a curiosity** — `progress_hash` gates the `busy_since` reset and feeds the digest, so editing the section *after* an empty `# Progress` reads as Progress activity, resetting the stuck clock on a worker that did nothing. Surfaced in the second review round; pre-existing, and deliberately not fixed here because it is a behaviour change rather than a documentation one. ⚠️ **Both halves were caught in review, not written correctly the first time.** The first cut said the newline before the next heading was kept and that an empty section always hashes the empty string; `ben-s-pull-request-reviewer` traced the regex against concrete inputs and falsified both. That is the argument for pinning them: `TestProgressHashContract` in `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py` now asserts each property against a **fixed byte string** (a hand-written literal, not a re-derivation — what is pinned is the bytes, not the hex, since `_sha()` still calls `hashlib.sha256` at run time), so it fails if `_PROGRESS` or the truncation moves — the pre-existing coverage asserted only that a Progress write moves the digest, which is true under every one of these readings. ⚠️ **The 2026-10-01 measurement is why the boundary earns this much prose:** a reader that hashed the section *including* its `# Progress` heading reported a mismatch against the stored value for **37 of 38** tracked rows — a near-total false-positive rate that reads as movement on every row rather than as one wrong boundary. ⚠️ **No behaviour changes**, and **nothing outside this module reads the field**, which is why the fix is documentation plus tests rather than a code change: `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` carries no `progress_hash` reference at all, and `agents/manager-drive.md:458` explicitly refuses to substitute it for a freshness reading (*"a second, silently different definition of 'stale'"*). Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`.

## v0.110.4
- fix: **a manager no longer reads an empty `pending_permissions` as "no gate is outstanding" before it directs an interactive tab worker.** `mcp__supervisor__pending_permissions` carries **this server's headless parks only**, so an **interactive tab worker** parked on an `AskUserQuestion` in its own pane leaves that list empty while the operator's answer is already given — and a manager that read `[]`, concluded nothing was outstanding and sent a direction into the row had its instrument read the wrong channel, not a missing one. `commands/manager-loop.md` § Manager contract now carries the manager-side rule the two asymmetry carriers (`commands/fleet-workers.md`, `llms.txt` § *How a worker's permission prompt is answered*) leave out: before a `SendMessage` that **directs** a worker, take that worker's **own** gate reading — for a worker **this session spawned**, `mcp__supervisor__agent_status <agent_id>` → **`open_cards`** (⚠️ `null` = store unreachable, `[]` = read-and-empty; collapsing the two reports a blocked worker as unblocked); for a worker **another session spawned**, where that tool answers `unknown agent <id>` — "not addressable from here", never "no gate" — the feed `scripts/who-needs-me.py --section needs-you`, which lists each open gate with its pane id, age, session label and question text. ⚠️ **A board-wide count is not a per-worker reading:** `scripts/attention-board.py` prints one line, `Board: N open`, with no `item_id` and no attribution, and the clause names it only to forbid that misreading. Where no reading can be taken, state the direction as **conditional on no operator answer already having been given**, so the worker declines cleanly rather than silently overriding an answer the operator already gave. ⚠️ **The carriers are cited, never restated** — the plugin's single-home rule — so the new clause adds only the manager-side act. Measured 2026-09-30, tick 15:37 on `BRO-21389 MDM via REST`: the manager read `[]` and directed a tab worker whose three gaps the operator had already answered in its pane; the worker refused, correctly, and the manager recorded the miss as its own — *"Two channels, one gate — and the manager's instrument only reads one of them."* `commands/fleet-loop.md` § Manager contract carries a **pointer** to that single home, never a copy — the fleet tier sends to workers too, and a nudge to a tab worker parked on an operator's question is the same collision as a scope direction. Change set: `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/fleet-loop.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

## v0.110.3
- fix: **`gate-owner-filter.py` recognises a manager opened through the worker path, so peer managers stop re-escalating its workers' gates.** `is_manager()` read ANY spawn-ledger record as proof of "worker" — but `/supervisor:open` starts a topic manager in a plain wezterm tab, which writes no record, while `spawn_agent(role="manager")` is a documented call that writes one, so a manager opened that way was indistinguishable from the workers it spawns. Measured 2026-10-06: the CDB in Weldall Manager (`1d379d23`, spawned by the Fleet Manager) had its workers' gates escalated by UI Manager and Fleet Manager at once, both re-raising pane 157 after the owning manager had already handled it. The record now carries the role the spawn declared — `buildRecord` accepts `role`, `writeLedger` passes `agent.role` (already resolved in-process by `spawn_agent`), and `is_manager()` reads a record as a manager only when it declares `role: "manager"`. ⚠️ **Only an explicit `manager` drops.** A record with no `role` reads as a worker: the field is absent on pre-field workers and managers alike, so defaulting the other way would flip every worker spawned before this change into a manager and drop the fleet's gates. A manager spawned by `spawn_agent(role="manager")` before this change therefore still reads as a worker until it is re-spawned or its record gains the field. Change set: `server/ledger.mjs`, `server/supervisor.mjs`, `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `server/ledger.test.mjs`, `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`.

## v0.110.2
- fix: **the manager's attention watcher now raises a gate for a worker parked on an `AskUserQuestion` modal that the union could not see.** `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py` gains a third limb — `status == "busy" and pending == "AskUserQuestion" and frozen` reports `busy+parked-modal`.
