Both existing halves can miss the same park: an `AskUserQuestion` whose assistant message ALSO carries prose reads `busy` in the registry **and** has its closer displaced by that prose, so it matches neither the `waiting` nor the `idle`+closer half. ⚠️ **The `held:` answer does not cover this case** — HELD keeps *membership* unchanged, so a worker that was never in the gated set stays ungated for as long as it is parked. Measured 2026-10-05: such a worker sat unnoticed for 30+ minutes and the manager noticed only because the operator asked. ⚠️ **The call NAME is load-bearing, not the freeze**: a pending `Bash` call is frozen too and is not a park, so only a modal — which cannot proceed without the operator — raises the gate; every non-modal or non-frozen `busy` row still reads `held:` exactly as before, so the limb narrows the accepted over-report without weakening it. ⚠️ This is **not** the registry-only predicate the mid-turn section says cannot exist: the registry genuinely cannot separate the two `busy` shapes, and this limb reads a different input — the pending call — per `CLAUDE.md` § Reading a worker, *"the transcript answers which call, the registry answers is it moving."* The limb sits deliberately **ahead of** the `_CLOSER_UNKNOWN` hold, because it does not read the closer at all and an unreadable tail must not demote a park it can see directly. Change set: `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_attention_watch.py`, `commands/manager-loop.md`.

- fix: **a tab worker's session is now bound to the task it was opened for, at spawn — and the bind is the ONLY writer.** `server/supervisor.mjs` called `bindSessionToTask` only on the **cluster** path, so a tab worker's `claude_session_id` was never written by the server, and the vault could not resolve such a worker at all. That is what left a parked worker invisible: `manager-attention-watch.py`'s `parked-on-unregistered-gate` cell fires only when a **live** session owns the row, so an unstamped row fell to the orphan path instead (measured 2026-10-05: a worker parked on a question sat unnoticed 30+ minutes). ⚠️ **The bind fires on `task`, and the documented tab shape did not pass it** — the schema called `task` *"ignored otherwise"* and `/supervisor:open` passed only `label`, so the first cut of this change was unreachable on the exact path it was written for. The schema now honours `task` on the **tab and cluster** paths, `docs/fleet-surface.md` § Spawn a worker's path A carries it, and `commands/open.md` passes it. ⚠️ **The local HEADLESS path still does not bind and reports no `bind` key** — a known gap, stated in the schema text and in `open.md` rather than papered over, because `/supervisor:open` sends `interactive=false` for every `mode: headless` row. ⚠️ **`label` is deliberately NOT the fallback** — it is free text a caller may set to anything, and a bind guessed from it would stamp the wrong task. ⚠️ **Reuses `bindSessionToTask` rather than writing the field** — it already implements the ownership rule (stamp only when the field is EMPTY, else append to `metrics_sessions`). ⚠️ **`commands/open.md` Step 4.1 no longer writes `claude_session_id` by hand.** It did so unconditionally after every spawn, which made it a SECOND writer with no ownership rule — the exact displacement `task-binding.mjs` exists to prevent (measured 2026-10-05: a spawn displaced a live owner's id, and the row survived only because `metrics_sessions` still carried it). It now reads the spawn response's `bind` field and reconciles only when `bind.error` is set. ⚠️ **A failed bind is NOT fatal here, unlike the cluster path's** — a cluster worker has no pane, so a failed bind there leaves a session nothing points at; this worker is reachable by pane and ledger, so failing the spawn would destroy a live worker to report a bookkeeping failure. It is reported on the spawn response as `bind` and logged instead, and the bind is wrapped so a throw cannot discard the response for a live worker. `bind` is `null` when the caller named no task. Change set: `server/supervisor.mjs`, `commands/open.md`, `docs/fleet-surface.md`.

## v0.110.1
- fix: **the pre-dispatch gate refuses the exit-0 replay when a row's owner moved `live` <-> `parked`, instead of printing a Status cell that is no longer true.** `digest_of` hashes `liveness_change_term`, deliberately DEATH-only since PR #154, so a worker opening or closing a gate moves no digest input — and the exit-0 branch (`scripts/manager-predispatch.py` `--print`) then reproduced the stored table verbatim, one cell of which had gone stale: the Status cell reads `⌛ waiting-on-human` for a parked row and its ordinary bucket for a live one (`agents/manager-sweep-reader.md:136`). The fix is a SEPARATE `parked_set_moved` clause in `evaluate`, riding beside the digest the way `actionable_names` does, so `digest_of` stays a pure function of the tracked set and session churn is **not** put back into it — hashing the raw liveness word again would also have refused the replay, at the ~150k-token act-leg cost the collapse exists to remove. `save_stored` now persists the parked set (`"parked"`), and a record written before the field existed reads as "cannot say" — a CHANGE, self-healed by the next `--save`, on the same fail-open rule `load_stored` applies. ⚠️ **The stale cell is the STATUS cell, never the Session cell, and the two were conflated in the caveats this change deletes.** `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 7 makes a parked session take the LIVE-shaped `[<sid8>]` delimiter, and `session-liveness.py` never reads the registry's `status`, so a `live` <-> `parked` flip renders the SAME Session cell on both sides — the old `commands/manager-status.md` step 1 and `commands/manager-drive.md` step 2 caveats described a cell that never moved, and both are removed. The Session cell's own stale route (a row whose ids live only in `metrics_sessions`, gaining its first id without moving a digest input) is **not** covered and remains open. Change set: `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_manager_predispatch.py`, `commands/manager-status.md`, `commands/manager-drive.md`.

## v0.110.0
- feat: **the manager's probe chain gains a pane-title liveness clause, so a live session no longer reads `ORPHANED` when the registry and the transcript both miss it.** `agents/manager-drive.md` (the auto-resume gate) and `commands/manager-loop.md` (§ Sweep 1's orphan bullet) now name the `wezterm cli list` tab-title match as an **additional independent signal** — matched on the **task name**, never the session id, since the pane listing carries no id and the tab name is the only join back to a session — matching the vault runbook `65 Runbooks/Manager Session.md` § Step 4, which is the authoritative twin. ⚠️ **It withholds, it never replaces** — the registry probe and the transcript-staleness cut still run on every row; a title match may **withhold** an `ORPHANED` row by supplying a **live** reading, it is **never evidence of death**, and it never turns a registry-LIVE reading dead. ⚠️ **A missing title is indeterminate, never death** — a headless worker has **no pane at all**, so absence from `wezterm cli list` proves nothing, exactly like an empty argv read. ⚠️ **The clause is independent of both existing signals by construction**: it reads neither argv nor the transcript, which is why it covers the live sessions an argv probe cannot see (measured 2026-09-15: of 37 sessions with a transcript newer than 4h, only 18 carried the id in argv) and the case a metadata flush defeats. Measured 2026-09-18 on session `32d5e57c`: at 19:12 every named clause read dead and the row was correct, and three minutes later a `wezterm cli list` match on the tab title read it live inside a single sweep cadence. ⚠️ **The flushed-transcript caveat is recorded at the clause** — the harness appends `last-prompt` and `cost-state` records **with no turn running**, so mtime tracks file writes rather than conversation activity and the 10-minute staleness test is satisfiable by a metadata flush alone. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`, `commands/manager-loop.md`.

## v0.109.3
- fix: **`reconcile()`'s history read in `scripts/answered-watch.py` is guarded, so a slow or remote store can no longer kill the watcher.** The read runs on every stream (re)connect and, since #164 widened it to `?limit=50000`, fetches and parses ~22 MB under an unchanged 10 s `STORE_TIMEOUT` — so a `URLError` or `OSError` propagated through `watch()` into `main()`, which catches only `KeyboardInterrupt`, and the process died with a traceback. A dead watcher is itself a missed wake, and it is indistinguishable from a quiet one until the wake it was armed for never fires. The call is now wrapped in the same narrow `except (urllib.error.URLError, OSError)` tuple `handle_item` already uses — the module's own principle, *"A single unreadable item must not kill the watch"*, applied to the other read path — and on that exception it warns to stderr and returns `seen` unchanged, so the next reconnect retries. `err` is threaded into `reconcile` (mirroring `handle_item`'s signature) rather than printing directly, which is what the `fetch_history` truncation warning above it still does. ⚠️ **The obvious probe does not discriminate, and the regression test records why.** Pointing the watcher at an unreachable store proves nothing: `watch()` reaches `reconcile()` only after `stream_item_ids` yields `CONNECTED`, which it does only once `urlopen(stream_url)` has succeeded, and `stream_item_ids` already swallows `URLError`/`OSError` and reconnects — so a dead store never arrives at `fetch_history` at all, and both the pre-fix and post-fix revisions survive it. The new `ReconcileTest.test_a_read_failure_warns_and_does_not_raise` therefore patches `watch.fetch_history` to raise (both `URLError` and `OSError`) and asserts the warn plus an identity-unchanged `seen`; on the pre-fix revision `4636e1dc` the same patch kills the process. ⚠️ **A review round on PR #176 found the comment's stated scope broader than the tuple delivers, and the comment now says exactly what it covers.** `socket.timeout` has been an alias of `TimeoutError` — itself an `OSError` — since Python 3.10, so the 10 s timeout on the widened read *is* caught. `http.client.IncompleteRead` (an `HTTPException`, hence a bare `Exception`) and `json.JSONDecodeError` (a `ValueError`), raised by a truncated or malformed 22 MB body, are **not**, and still kill the watch. Widening the tuple is deliberately left out of this change: the point is to mirror `handle_item`, which carries the identical gap, and widening one call site but not the other is the worse inconsistency. The same round restored `fetch_history`'s comment to its true reason — `fetch_history` takes no stream parameter, which is why it writes to stderr directly, not "the read's success path" — and the test now uses `subTest` per exception class and additionally asserts the recovery the guard exists for: a second `reconcile` after a failed one still emits. Change set: `scripts/answered-watch.py`, `scripts/tests/test_answered_watch.py`.

## v0.109.2
- fix: **`scripts/manager-attention-watch.py` holds a gate when the worker starts a NEW TURN, instead of emitting an unearned `CLEARED`.** A gated worker that began new work without answering read `busy`, which matched neither half of the predicate, so the watcher emitted a bare `CLEARED` at the mid-turn point — nothing answered, the worker still mid-turn. `is_gated()` now returns HELD (`held:<status>`) for any status that is neither `waiting` nor `idle`, and `transitions` keeps a held-mid-turn session's **membership** — no `CLEARED` (nothing was answered) and no `NEW GATE` (it was already in the set) — so the eventual settle at `idle` still emits the one `CLEARED` a genuine answer earns. ⚠️ Accepted cost, and the decision the limb exists to make: a worker that stays busy indefinitely stays listed as gated, because the registry cannot separate "resumed BECAUSE the gate was answered" from "moved on WITHOUT answering" — both read `busy` with the closer displaced. A false `CLEARED` suppresses the manager's escalation on an unanswered gate; a false `NEW GATE` costs one pane read.

## v0.109.1
- fix: **the drive leg's verdicts cache no longer invalidates the entry it just hit, and the key's derivation now has a home.** `agents/manager-drive.md` clause (1) told the leg to key each row's `task-auditor` verdict to *"the row file's content"* and named no algorithm, so each leg finished the sentence itself; clause (6) then writes `mode:` to every row it prepares — *"that write IS the decision the ledger reads"* — which changes that row's bytes. A key taken over the raw file is therefore orphaned by the leg's own next clause: the entry is written and immediately dead, and the row pays a cold `task-auditor` dispatch on the next tick. Reproduced exactly, 2026-10-06 — `sha256[:16]` of `25 Tasks/Remove the Headless Park Cap From the Personal Vault's Manager Session Runbook.md` is `f66c7a1136d52022` with its `mode: interactive` line and `2763ce6e2710efe6` without it, the same row one field apart, and the stored key is the post-write value. Clause (1) now states the derivation: `sha256` over the row file's bytes with the four tick-written fields the audit does not judge removed, truncated to the first 16 hex. ⚠️ **The cut is *the fields whose value the audit does not read*, not *everything a tick writes*, and the difference is load-bearing.** A tick writes six frontmatter fields — `mode`, `last_auto_resume`, `claude_session_id`, `metrics_sessions`, `status`, `phase` (plus `approved_at` / `approved_by` through `vault-cli task approve`) — and excluding all six would freeze a row's verdict label across an approval, because `phase` moving `todo → planning` is exactly what turns `✅ Ready for approval` into `🚀 Ready`. The excluded four are the metadata the audit ignores. ⚠️ **The missing algorithm had already drifted, which is why the truncation is now spelled out.** Measured 2026-10-06T11:35 across the twelve caches in `~/.claude/state/manager-predispatch/` (92 entries), the key carried **three** lengths — 2 caches at 12 hex (33 entries), 7 at 16 (55), 3 at 64 (4) — and each cache is internally uniform, the signature of a derivation invented per leg rather than read. A cache written at one length can never match another, so a drifted subject re-audits every one of its rows. ⚠️ **One measured non-defect is recorded so it is not re-litigated as this change's:** 21 of those 92 entries no longer match their row file, and **none** of them from a `mode:` write — stripping the `mode:` line reproduces the stored key for zero of them, and the duplicate-vault copies (`Personal/` vs `private-personal/`) are byte-identical, so it is not a wrong-copy read. Those rows' audited content genuinely moved, and a cold re-audit of a changed row is the cache working as designed. ⚠️ **The behavioural half is a live tick, not this diff** — the derivation is executed by an agent, so no unit test can assert the leg computes it; `scripts/check-content-key-formula.py` is the drift guard that the clause cannot silently lose the formula (it fails seven ways against the pre-change clause and passes after), and the end-to-end claim is a row prepared on tick N reporting a cache hit on tick N+1. ⚠️ **The removal mechanism is pinned too, because a mechanism left open is a derivation left open** — the four lines are dropped from the **raw bytes**, with no YAML parse, no re-serialisation and no newline normalisation, since those two routes disagree on CRLF, on the blank line a deleted frontmatter entry leaves behind, on quoted and multi-line values, and on a trailing newline. Without that sentence two legs could follow the clause verbatim and still disagree — the same per-leg drift this change exists to close, surviving in the one step it left unstated. ⚠️ **A review round on PR #172 found two false-greens in the guard itself, both fixed and both pinned by a test.** `EXCLUDED` was asserted with a bare `field not in clause` substring test, and the clause's own measured note quotes `` `mode: interactive` `` — so deleting `mode:` from the exclusion *sentence* left the guard green for the one field the whole fix depends on; the assertion is now scoped to the exclusion sentence. And the algorithm assertion was `re.compile(r"sha256.*?16 hex", re.S)`, an unbounded lazy span under DOTALL satisfied by `sha256` anywhere and `16 hex` anywhere later; it now requires both tokens in **one sentence**. `scripts/tests/test_check_content_key_formula.py` pins both mutations, the staleness escape hatch, fail-closed on a missing file, and — against the repository's real clause rather than the fixture — that the shipped text passes. ⚠️ **A second review round found the exclusion set ineffective for one of its four fields, and that finding was right.** `metrics_sessions` is written as a **multi-line YAML block** in this vault — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` documents that shape for it and for `blocked_by` — so *"drop each line whose key is one of those four"* removed only the `metrics_sessions:` key line and left its indented `- session_id: <uuid>` entries in the key. Those uuids change on every worker run, so the key kept churning per tick: the exclusion named the field and did not remove it. The clause now takes each key line **together with that key's continuation block**, and the guard asserts the block-extent sentence is present. ⚠️ **Two more false-greens in the guard were found and closed in the same round:** the clause window fell back to the *rest of the file* when no following `**(2)` heading was found, so a reformatted clause numbering would have let every assertion be satisfied by a later clause — it now reports itself stale instead of widening; and `sentence_containing` returned the *first* sentence carrying `sha256`, so an earlier mention without the truncation turned the guard red with a message naming the opposite problem — it now requires that **some** sentence carries both tokens. ⚠️ **A third round closed the last two gaps, both in the guard rather than the clause.** The removal *mechanism* — *"no YAML parse, no re-serialisation, no newline normalisation"* — was called pinned in this entry and in the clause while nothing asserted it, so a later swap to parse-and-re-serialise would have left every assertion green; it is now assertion (f). And `exclusion_sentence` returned only the **first** sentence carrying the anchor — the same first-match policy `sentences_containing` was changed away from earlier in this same PR, so the two helpers disagreed for no stated reason; it now returns every match and the assertion is an `any`. Change set: `agents/manager-drive.md`, `scripts/check-content-key-formula.py`, `scripts/tests/test_check_content_key_formula.py`, `Makefile`.

## v0.109.0
- feat: **`scripts/stuck-heal.py` decides the heal for a `stuck:` gate — resume headless once, then reopen as an interactive tab — and never performs it.** `spawn_agent` is an MCP tool only a Claude session holds and `scripts/restart-worker.py` refuses a headless target, so the script prints the action and `commands/manager-loop.md` step 7 has the manager execute it. Prior resumes are read from the spawn ledger (`SUPERVISOR_LEDGER_DIR` / `XDG_STATE_HOME`, as the server resolves it); the ledger is keyed by session id and a resume overwrites its record, so it records "resumed at least once" — the bound holds because the reopened worker is a tab (`stuck-tab:`, never fed back) and the dead headless session raises no further gate. Causes other than `stream-closed` are report-only; a parked judgement gate is never resumed or answered.
- docs: `commands/manager-loop.md` step 7 runs the heal on a `stuck:` gate (never `stuck-tab:`): the full task name is resolved from the session's task file (the gate label is truncated), `--kind` comes from the gate's first `::` field and is now required (a `stream-closed` default would turn a forgotten argument into a resume), and a failed interactive reopen sets the mode back to headless and posts a card, without retrying. `--session` must be at least 8 characters.

## v0.108.0
- feat: **a headless worker whose permission channel dies is now detected, instead of read as UNREGISTERED forever.** `scripts/manager-attention-watch.py` unions the supervisor's heartbeat store (`~/.local/state/claude-supervisor/live/<sid>.json`, mtime as the age authority, 60 s TTL) into its liveness read. A headless worker is an in-process SDK `query()` with no pid, so the session registry can never list it, and every such worker was HELD and never announced. `is_gated()` now tells UNREGISTERED from HEADLESS, and a new `stuck:<cause>` reason fires on a `Stream closed` permission failure, a `🔴 BLOCKED` state line (matched as a line prefix, never a substring), or 3 consecutive permission failures. Both failure triggers read **tool results only** — a worker quoting the error in its own prose is healthy — and count the TRAILING run among permission outcomes: assistant text between failures does not break it (a record-consecutive reading scores the observed 2026-10-05 death as 1), while any tool that actually ran resets it, so a recovered channel does not read STUCK.
- docs: `commands/manager-loop.md` step 7 — the manager posts a board card on a `stuck:` gate (default `session:<sid>` liveness ref: the poster is an interactive session the store resolves live); the reason list is corrected, since `headless+closer` and `stuck:<cause>` can now reach a `NEW GATE`.
- feat: a STUCK verdict on a **registered tab worker** reads `stuck-tab:<cause>` instead of `stuck:<cause>`, so the follow-up heal ladder (a headless resume) can never target a tab. A test pins the watcher's heartbeat TTL and skip-file against `server/heartbeat.mjs`.

## v0.107.2
- fix: **a card posted by `attention-ask.py` is no longer deleted the moment the session that posted it exits.** `--liveness-ref` defaulted to `session:<producer-id>`, and the store prunes an open item that *asked* (any mechanism but `ack`) whose liveness subject is not live — `attention-controller` `pkg/attention-store-impl.go` `classifyForRead` → `pruneDead` → `removeItem`, which clears the item from the items bucket **and every index**, so the card leaves no history row either. A manager's session ends on every turn boundary, every fleet restart and every crash, so every card this script posted was created with a `201` and deleted on the first read after the poster exited. Measured 2026-10-06: a fleet restart killed the posting session and the operator's approval question vanished silently — the producer holding a success, the store holding nothing, and 23 sibling cards surviving because their producers were still live. The default is now `owner:<producer-id>`, the model built for exactly this, whose `isProducerLiveWith` returns true unconditionally and deliberately: a human owner's absence cannot be read from the session registry, since a never-registered owner and an exited one produce the same signal, so reading either as gone prunes a gate the operator can still answer. Every card this script posts is an ask the OPERATOR answers on the board and the asking session merely polls back, so the operator's liveness is the honest subject and the poster's is not; an explicit `--liveness-ref` still wins. ⚠️ **The bound had to move with it, and that is the second half of the fix rather than a follow-up.** An `owner:` item is never pruned by liveness, so the producer's life stopped being the bound at exactly the moment the card stopped dying with it — and `--expires-at` defaulted to `""`, so every card went out unbounded and the board could only grow. It now defaults to `now + $ATTENTION_ASK_TTL_HOURS` (24 h) and stays overridable per call. ⚠️ **The store's half already exists, and saying otherwise was the trap.** `attention-controller` PR #95 (`feat/expires-at-enforcement`) merged the same morning, and it removes an item whose deadline has passed on the read path — so the declared bound is enforced rather than inert. The two halves are deliberately coupled: an `owner:` card is never pruned by liveness, so `expires_at` is the *only* thing bounding it, and this default is what gives that enforcement something to act on. ⚠️ **The trap was already documented here and the default was never changed** — this file records the mechanism verbatim (*"a card posted by a producer the store cannot resolve is created with a `201` and removed on the very first board read — it never reaches the operator"*) in the entry for the change that made `--liveness-ref` passable, so the field was known to decide survival and was still left pointing at the poster. Change set: `scripts/attention-ask.py`, `scripts/tests/test_attention_ask.py`.

## v0.107.1
- fix: **`defaultRun`'s SIGTERM→SIGKILL escalation becomes injectable and covered, and the latency harness's cluster timer reports a broken instrument instead of dying on one.** `killGraceMs` joins `timeoutMs` and `maxOutputBytes` as the third injectable knob. Until now the escalation was unreachable from any test: the existing timeout test lets SIGTERM kill the child, so the 2 s grace was never reached, and the one path that reaps a SIGTERM-trapping reader carried no coverage at all. ⚠️ **The escalation is invisible in the resolved value, so the test asserts on the child's disappearance rather than on a signal.** `finish`'s `settled` guard drops the later `close` — the only place the real signal is known — so a SIGTERM-ignoring child resolves at ~55 ms with `signal: 'SIGTERM'`. Measured before the change: alive at resolve, alive at +500 ms, gone at ~2300 ms. Asserting the exit signal would therefore pass on the pre-change code and prove nothing; the new test polls the child's pid and requires it gone inside a bound well under the default grace, which fails before the change and passes after. ⚠️ **The child reports its pid to a file, not to stdout** — `defaultRun` resolves at the timeout with whatever stdout it has collected by then, so reading the pid from the result races the child's own start-up; measured under the full suite, where a 50 ms timeout won that race and returned an empty stdout while the same test passed when its file was run alone. ⚠️ **`scripts/heartbeat-stamp-latency.mjs`'s `setInterval(async …)` cluster timer gains an explicit `catch`** printing `failed-instrument: cluster poll threw: <stack>` to stderr. Left uncaught, a throw from `pollCluster` — an ENOSPC inside `stampRecord`, say — became an unhandled rejection that took the run down before it printed anything, losing every other instrument's numbers with it. Verified by forcing the throw: the line appears on stderr and the run still exits 0 with its full table. ⚠️ **The summary row is now honest about its sample set** — `[].every()` is true, so a run in which every poll threw would otherwise render `cluster polls: 0 (all ok=true)`, reading healthy off zero evidence; the throw count is appended in **either** branch, so a mixed run reads `N (all ok=true), M threw` rather than hiding its throws. It is deliberately **not** a `process.on('unhandledRejection')` handler, which `node/lifecycle/crash-on-unhandled-rejection` requires to exit non-zero — that rule is about a service left in an unknown state, and its enforcement matches that registration, not a call-site `try/catch`. ⚠️ **The `scripts/` → `server/` import edge is now documented rather than incidental** — the harness must measure the real `pollCluster` and `stampRecord`/`listLive` rather than copies of them, and the edge only ever runs one way (`server/` reaches `scripts/` by spawning the Python readers as subprocesses, never by import). Change set: `server/cluster-heartbeat.mjs`, `server/cluster-heartbeat.test.mjs`, `scripts/heartbeat-stamp-latency.mjs`.

## v0.107.0
- feat: **`spawn_agent` gains `shipping: true`: the headless worker runs under `acceptEdits` with `git add` / `git commit` / `git push` pre-allowed,** so its edits and commits never depend on the supervisor permission channel — a worker whose channel dies (`Tool permission request failed: AbortError: Stream closed`, observed 2026-10-05) can otherwise neither edit nor commit. Delivered through the SDK `settings` option, which loads into the trusted `flag` tier, so no settings file is written. `acceptEdits` is deliberately not one of `POLICY_UNREACHABLE_MODES`, so every other tool still prompts and the approval policy stays in force; a node test fails if that ever changes. Refused on a tab or cluster spawn, mirroring `policySupportError`.

## v0.106.1
- fix: **the manager's documented attention-feed arm no longer reports a healthy empty queue when the feed was never read — the refusal now reaches the pipeline, and a live-but-wrong mux socket is caught instead of trusted.** The arm is `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you | gate-owner-filter.py --feed --self <sid>`, and a pipe carries stdout only: `who-needs-me.py` refused correctly and exited **1**, but wrote the refusal to **stderr**, so the filter read an empty feed and printed `gates: 0  emit: 0  dropped(peer-manager): 0  dropped(peer-manager-own): 0  dropped(claimed): 0`, exit **0**. Measured 2026-10-06 against v0.106.0 with the mux socket unreachable — a healthy empty queue certified by the success code, for a transport that never answered, and a `Monitor` armed on it never fires while still looking alive. `who-needs-me.py` now writes `WHO-NEEDS-ME-REFUSED` to **stdout** as well, and `gate-owner-filter.py` keys on that marker: on a refusal it names the upstream message and returns **1** *without printing a `gates:` line at all* — a marker printed beside the false claim is not a fix, because `gates: 0` still reads as a measurement. ⚠️ **An unmarked empty feed still exits 0 with `gates: 0`, and that half is the point:** the two states leave an identical row set, and collapsing them is the false refusal `docs/pane-reads.md` names as this defect class's mirror — it has already been shipped here once, when a `Monitor` died with `script failed (exit 2)` on every quiet tick (measured 2026-10-05, manager loop tick 154). ⚠️ **A second, independent half: readability is not plausibility.** The existing guard tests whether the socket *answers*; a stray `wezterm-mux-server` answers and its pane table holds ONE bare default pane, so the feed returned a confident `Needs you (0)` against 26 live claude processes on ttys000-ttys037 (measured 2026-10-06 ~08:10). `pane_table_implausible()` now resolves each live registered session's **tty** through the existing `fleet-colours.py:pid_ttys()` — the repo's one pid→tty reader, which already drops `??` and returns `None` on a failed `ps` — and refuses when at least one tty appears in no pane and fewer than half are accounted for. ⚠️ **The denominator is the sessions whose tty is resolvable, not every live registered session:** a headless session reports `??` and can never appear in any pane table, so counting it as unaccounted-for would fire the check on fleet composition rather than on transport health — the same false-refusal mirror, one layer over. Measured on this machine: 28 live registered sessions, 26 matched, 2 unmatched — well clear of the threshold. The check fails **open** on an unreadable registry, a failed `ps`, or a pane table carrying no `tty_name`, so a failed probe is never indistinguishable from the defect it looks for. All four arm recipes now state the refusal path — `commands/manager-loop.md` step 7, `commands/fleet-loop.md` § Attention watcher, and the vault runbooks `Manager Session.md` and `Fleet Manager Session.md` § Cadence mechanics — each on one line carrying the literal tokens `pipeline refuses`, `exited non-zero`, `does NOT arm` and `unread`, so the criterion is decided by a grep rather than by a reader's judgement. Change set: `scripts/who-needs-me.py`, `scripts/gate-owner-filter.py`, `scripts/tests/test_who_needs_me.py` (11 new cases: the stdout marker, its uniqueness on stdout, the healthy control, the one-pane fixture through `main()`, its all-panes control, and eight unit cases covering the threshold, the headless denominator and every fail-open input), `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py` (10 new cases: refusal exits non-zero, prints no `gates:` line and surfaces the text; the unmarked-empty and healthy controls; and the extractor's matching rules in isolation), `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/fleet-loop.md`.

## v0.106.0
- feat: **the open-items ledger gains a `withdraw` verb and a `set` verb, refuses a manager tick summary as a `pushed` entry, and marks an open entry that names no task — the three ways an entry could sit open forever, none of which the store could express.** ⚠️ **`withdraw` is the third close path, not a synonym for `close --evidence`:** `answer` closes an `asked-of-you` because the operator replied, `close --evidence` closes any entry whose own resolution condition was met and verified on disk, and `withdraw` closes one the operator withdrew, because it should not be outstanding — filed in error, superseded, or never real. `skills/open-items/SKILL.md` already promised that path for `asked-of-me` (*"or the operator withdraws it"*) with no verb behind it, so the kind table and the script disagreed. It is **REFUSED on `asked-of-you`**, whose only close path is `answer` — a withdrawal there would stamp an operator act onto a question they may never have seen, the forgery `answer`'s own docstring warns about reached through a second verb — and the refusal writes nothing at all. `withdrawn_at` records the distinction, so a later reader tells a withdrawal from an evidence-close rather than testing for absence. ⚠️ **The `add` guard is scoped to `pushed` on purpose.** A tick summary filed as `pushed` names no task, so that kind's close condition — its task file reading `status: completed` — can never fire; measured 2026-10-05, six 2026-09-29 manager-loop tick summaries sat open in one ledger for a week, indistinguishable in the render from an ask genuinely still outstanding. The refusal is `TICK_SUMMARY_RE` (`^Manager-loop tick \d+ \(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}`), measured against that live 72-entry ledger: it matches **seven** entries, all `pushed` — the six open ones plus one already closed that had named a task — and no entry of any other kind. It is deliberately **not** applied to `asked-of-me`, which carries the operator's words verbatim and where a refusal could block a genuine instruction that happens to quote a tick; the paired negative control in the suite pins exactly that. ⚠️ **`set --task` closes the act step's missing half.** `add` was the only writer of `task`, so the ledger's own act rule — *"no task → file one and name it on the entry"* — named an action no verb could perform, and a sweep that filed a covering task could not record it on the entry. It cannot fake a resolution: `list` re-resolves the target on every read, so a task backing no file still renders `⚠️ UNRESOLVABLE`. ⚠️ **`⚠️ NO TASK` is the render half of the same defect.** `target_state` returned `none` for an entry naming no task and `MARKERS` had no key for `none`, so such an entry printed exactly like a healthy one. The marker is scoped by **kind** as well as by state, in a new `marker_for` helper: an `asked-of-you` legitimately names no task because it resolves on the operator's answer, so a bare `none` key would have fired on every question the ledger holds. ⚠️ **That carve-out covers every state, not only `none`** — added after review. Covering `none` alone left a question added with `--task` naming no file rendering `⚠️ UNRESOLVABLE`, which is the same false positive by a different route and had no repair verb: `set` refuses that kind, and it is the verb the UNRESOLVABLE rule names. The kind makes no file claim in any state, so no task-state marker is a true statement about it. Expect a visible jump on the first sweep — 10 of the 16 open entries in the live ledger are `asked-of-me` naming no task. ⚠️ **Both new verbs refuse a terminal entry, and `set` also refuses an `asked-of-you`** — added after review found the state guard missing. Without it `withdraw`'s unconditional write replaced an entry's existing `closed_evidence` with a withdrawal claim, recording a resolved ask as one that was never real: the exact indistinguishability the verb's own docstring says it exists to prevent, reachable because `find()` matches on an id PREFIX, so a stale or partial `--id` lands on the wrong entry. And naming a task on an `asked-of-you` moved its `target_state` off the `none` that `marker_for`'s kind carve-out keys on, so an unresolvable name rendered `UNRESOLVABLE` on a question whose real resolution is the operator's answer. Both refusals write nothing. The guard's input is normalised (`tick_summary_text`) rather than the measured `^`-anchored pattern growing a prefix group, so a leading space **and** a list or quote marker are not silent bypasses while the measurement still describes the shipped shape — and the marker case is the one the text actually arrives in, since a tick summary is copied out of a sweep's own output as `- Manager-loop tick 9 (…)` or `> Manager-loop tick 9 (…)`. ⚠️ **The subcommand enumeration is removed from `commands/manager-loop.md` and `commands/fleet-loop.md` rather than extended.** Both carried a copy of the skill's list, both said *"never restate it here"* on the same line, and the copy had **already** drifted before this change — it omitted `classify`, which the skill's `argument-hint` has carried all along — so a manager reading only the command file was already being told a shorter list than the skill offers. Adding `set` and `withdraw` would have repaired the instance and left the next verb to repeat it; the pointer to the skill is what stays true. ⚠️ **The same class was then ENUMERATED and closed rather than patched one surface at a time**, after review named a third instance. `commands/fleet-loop.md` still told a fleet manager to *"close on evidence only"* in two places — its ledger bullet and its guardrail — so the layer whose ledger accumulates the same uncloseable entries was the one layer never told `withdraw` existed; and `llms.txt` summarized the skill without it. Both now name the three close paths and point at the skill's kind table for which applies to which kind. ⚠️ **The vault's `Manager Session` runbook carries the same gap and is deliberately NOT fixed here** — naming a verb before the installed script has it is the same drift in the other direction, so it is updated once this ships. ⚠️ **A further pass closed five gaps, three of them one class — a new verb mutating without a guard or a record of the prior value.** `withdraw` now refuses an empty `--reason` (a blank records `operator withdrew it: ` — a withdrawal carrying zero operator words, the attribution forgery this verb exists to prevent, reached by omission; `close` guards the same field the same way). `set` refuses an empty `--resolves-on` (an empty close condition is one nothing can check — the defect the `NO TASK` marker exists to make visible, reintroduced through a flag) and **warns on stderr naming the title it replaced**, since re-pointing is legitimate but a silent re-point rewrites a decision leaving no trace. ⚠️ **And the narrowing clause "an entry that was never an ask" is corrected wherever it appeared** — it excluded `asked-of-me`, which the kind table names outright as withdrawable (*"or the operator withdraws it"*), so the half of the kinds this verb most often serves was described as out of scope while the pointer in the same bullet included it. ⚠️ **The first version of this bullet claimed it had been corrected "in all five places", and that claim was FALSE** — a sixth survived inside `cmd_withdraw`'s own docstring, and review found it. The count is not repeated here for exactly that reason; the claim is that the phrase is gone from the tree, which is checkable with one grep. ⚠️ **And `find()` now refuses an EMPTY `--id`** — `item["id"].startswith("")` is true for every entry, so `--id ""`, which is what an unset shell variable expands to, silently resolved to the ledger's first one: on `withdraw` that stamped an operator attribution onto an entry nobody named. Guarded in `find()` rather than per verb, so all six share the refusal. **Two findings from the same pass are declined, with the reason recorded rather than the finding dropped:** `cmd_close` carries the same unconditional `closed_evidence` write this PR guards against on the new verbs, and `marker_for`'s kind carve-out now covers `unresolvable` as well as `none` — the first is pre-existing on a verb outside this change's scope, where the same guard belongs in its own PR so the behaviour change is reviewable on its own; the second is a deliberate disagreement with a later review, which argued the carve-out removed a signal: `UNRESOLVABLE` asserts *"its close condition can never fire"*, which is untrue of an `asked-of-you` whose close condition is the operator's answer, so on that kind the flag makes a false claim rather than surfacing a true one. A superseded operator instruction is the common case, not an exception. ⚠️ **Two further surfaces closed classes this change had already claimed to close, and one is DECLINED.** `set --task` was the third and last flag on the new verbs with no empty-value guard — `resolve_task("")` returns None, so it wrote `task: ""` with no path and stripped a previously valid resolution path; and the leading-marker strip now covers the checkbox (`- [ ] …`) and ordered (`1. …`) forms a rendered task list emits, not only bullets and quotes. **Declined, with the reason recorded rather than the finding dropped:** `find()`'s id-PREFIX matching, which lets a stale or truncated `--id` land on a different entry. It is pre-existing, shared by all six verbs, and the fix belongs at `find()` — an ambiguous-prefix refusal when more than one entry matches — rather than bolted onto either new verb, where it would leave the other four exposed. Change set: `scripts/open-items.py`, `skills/open-items/SKILL.md`, `scripts/tests/test_open_items.py` (35 new cases: the refusal and its three paired negative controls, the whitespace and list/quote bypasses, every real tick shape, the three close paths' non-interference, the two terminal/kind refusals on `withdraw` and `set`, the marker's kind scoping in both states, and `set`'s inability to fake a resolution), `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/fleet-loop.md`, `llms.txt`.

## v0.105.9
- fix: **`answered-watch.py`'s history re-baseline now passes an explicit limit, so it stops reading a silently truncated page.** `fetch_history()` called `GET /api/1.0/attention/history` **bare**, and that endpoint is paged with a default page of **1000** items — measured 2026-10-05, the unpaged read returns **1,000** of the store's **25,683**, and a key that reads 2 cards unpaged reads **9** with the limit. `reconcile()` re-baselines from this read on every (re)connect, so the truncation drops any answer that arrived while the stream was down and has since fallen outside the window: it is neither adopted into `seen` nor emitted — a **missed wake**, the exact failure this arm exists to prevent. The read now carries `?limit=50000` (`$ATTENTION_HISTORY_LIMIT`), the same constant, env var and default as `attention-card-lookup.py`, so the two readers cannot drift apart. ⚠️ **The adopt-silently branch is unaffected** — an item predating `started_at` would not emit anyway — so the fix changes only the catch-up direction; reachability is volume-dependent (1,000 items was ≈20 h of store traffic at the measured rate), so it bites on a long outage or a long-lived session rather than routinely. Pinned by a test asserting the URL the reader actually builds, not the constant. ⚠️ **And a full page now warns** — `len(items) >= limit` prints `WARN: history read may be truncated…` to stderr, so the ceiling this limit creates is observable rather than silent: raising the limit relocates the truncation, and the warning is what stops it going unnoticed at the new height.

## v0.105.8
- fix: **the drive leg's card clause now requires a card's STATE to be read live, so a state carried in prose can no longer be reported as current.** The below-bar hold-line clause in `agents/manager-drive.md`'s `<error_handling>` block — the resolution rule's single home — already required the card **id** to be resolved through `scripts/attention-card-lookup.py` rather than recalled, but said nothing about the card's **state**, and a leg duly reported `70eb5521 … open created=2026-10-02T22:24:48Z` while a live read of the same item returned `closed`, created `2026-10-03T21:29:54Z`. ⚠️ **The state came from a goal page's carried-forward note** whose own text concedes its list is *"last-known, not current"* — so the leg inherited a claim rather than taking a reading, and it inherited it in the direction that suppresses a raise. The clause now states that a state must come from **this tick's** store read, and that the read already in hand counts: `attention-card-lookup.py recent` prints `STATE:` beside the **full** `ITEM_ID:` in the same call that resolves the card, so it is a live read and needs no second round-trip, while `attention-ask.py poll <the full 32-hex item id>` / `GET /api/1.0/attention/<item-id>` cover only a card the lookup did not answer. ⚠️ **`latest` is named as NOT a state source, and neither is `list`** — `latest` prints a full `ITEM_ID:` and **no state field at all**, its exit 0 saying only that *some* card under the key is `answered`; the `state=` rows are **`list`'s**, and every id on them is truncated to 8 characters, so a state read off `list` cannot be tied to a pollable card either. ⚠️ **Re-reading a state already held is forbidden** — a redundant poll that fails on a transient store blip while the first read succeeded would convert an available state into `unread`, manufacturing the ambiguity the clause exists to remove. It further states that a goal page's note, a previous tick's entry, a cache file and a session's own recollection are each last-known and never current; and that an 8-character id cannot be polled at all — `poll` refuses a short id **before it ever reaches the store** (exit **2**, `REFUSED: '<id>' is not a store item id — one is 32 hex characters, and this is 8.`), which is a fact about the **id** and never about the card. ⚠️ **The clause names that token's two neighbours, because all three look alike and only one is about the card:** this `REFUSED:` is a malformed **id**, the `REFUSED:` bound to the ambiguity hold line is undecidable **dedup-key ordering**, and `FAILED:` (exit 1) is an unreadable **store**. None is a statement that the card is gone, and none licenses the re-post this clause forbids. A state that cannot be read live is reported **unread**, never as the state remembered. ⚠️ **The other end is fixed in `commands/manager-loop.md` step 4, where the `# Status Summary` rule lives.** *"Reconciles it from task-file facts"* was read as a summary rather than as a limit, so a fact no task file carries — a card's `open` / `closed` — was reconciled into the page tick after tick and read as measured fact while nothing re-read it. The step now states that a store fact is read live when needed and is **never written as a standing claim**, and that a recorded one carries the read's own timestamp and the **full** item id, never an 8-character prefix. ⚠️ **The vault half ships in place and has no PR of its own:** the standing claim this leg inherited lives on `24 Goals/Managers Spawn Interactive Claude Workers in the Cluster.md`, which now carries a page-level correction with the live read — all **five** card ids that page carried (the four its recurring line names, plus `c4ae6554`) `closed`, three of them re-created under the same dedup keys.

## v0.105.7
- fix: **`gate-owner-filter.py` treats an empty feed as a clean empty result instead of a usage error, so the documented watcher arm survives a quiet fleet.** The argument guard tested the *derived row set* (`if not panes: parser.error(…)`) rather than the *source*, which collapsed two different facts into one: `--feed` with no `[<pane>]` rows is a **successful** read of an empty feed — the common case — while a call carrying neither `--pane` nor `--feed` is malformed. So the documented arm `who-needs-me.py --section needs-you | gate-owner-filter.py --feed --self <sid>` exited **2** with `pass --pane, --feed, or both` on every quiet tick, and a `Monitor` armed with it died with `script failed (exit 2)` within seconds while its sibling watchers stayed up: the manager's push channel was dead exactly when the fleet was quiet, and the failure read as a bad command rather than as a lost watch. Measured 2026-10-05 (manager loop tick 154): the feed read `Needs you (0)` / `Nothing needs you.` and the pipeline died with that usage error, reproduced in both argument orders and against a synthetic empty feed on stdin. An empty feed now exits **0**, emits nothing on stdout, and keeps the `gates: 0  emit: 0 …` line on stderr — so an empty result stays legible as *nothing to filter* rather than *the filter is broken*. ⚠️ **The negative control is deliberately kept:** a call with neither flag still exits 2 and `--pane` alone is still a source, so the fix is "test the source", never "delete the guard". Five cases pin both halves in `scripts/tests/test_gate_owner_filter.py`; the pre-fix failure reproduces against an installed copy with `printf '' | gate-owner-filter.py --feed --self x` (exit 2). ⚠️ **This is the second silent-miss at the same seam, and the opposite direction from the first** — `feed_rows`' own docstring records a correct guard misreading a *wrong* extractor's empty result; here the extractor is right and the guard misreads its empty result as a bad call.

## v0.105.6
- fix: **the in-flight check now reads the session registry first, so the `parked-on-unregistered-gate` cell stops mis-rendering in both directions.** Measured 2026-10-05 against a live registry: the check's marker and transcript limbs were wrong **both** ways while the registry's `status` field was right in both — **six** sessions read `shell` while the transcript limb called them `quiet` (a long tool call writes no transcript; one had been silent 54.8 min inside a live child shell), and **one** read `waiting` while the transcript limb called it in flight (a parked worker's re-render moves its transcript inside `LIVE_WINDOW`). The check is now three limbs, tried in order: the registry's `status` (`shell` ⇒ in flight, `waiting` ⇒ quiet, `idle`/`busy` ⇒ no signal, fall through), then the tool marker, then the transcript. ⚠️ **`busy` is deliberately not read as working** — `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md`'s input 11 rejects it as the weaker candidate, on the ground that trusting `busy` while distrusting `idle` is inconsistent; both are the same field and neither describes the tool loop. ⚠️ **The registry limb is a CALLER read** — the sweep reader's `<constraints>` forbid it the routes to it — `pgrep`/`ps`, transcript recency and the clock — and the registry is not among the reader's declared inputs, so the verdict stays the caller-supplied input 11 and the reader is unchanged.
- fix: **`sweep-gate.py`'s `session_in_flight()` now carries the ≥20-minute marker branch it was silently dropping.** ⚠️ **This half ships in the VAULT, not in this repository** — `.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py` is a vault-local script edited in place, with no PR and no release of its own; nothing in this tree contains it. It is recorded here because the plugin's check and the gate's copy are one discriminator under the runbook's *"four surfaces, and they must agree"* rule, and a reader comparing the two needs to know the second half exists and where. The copy returned `True` for **any** `state: "open"` marker, however old, while the check it cites (`agents/manager-drive.md:100-134`) prescribes three marker branches — `open` inside ~20 min ⇒ in flight, `open` past it ⇒ *"Do not drop on this check; the stuck path is what should fire"*, absent/cleared ⇒ transcript fallback. A marker left by a session that died mid-tool-call therefore read as "in flight" forever. The new `STUCK_MARKER_SECONDS` mirrors `scripts/who-needs-me.py`'s `--stuck-min` default, which the drive leg names as the owner of that reading. ⚠️ **Latent until now, and coupled:** no live session carries a marker today (nothing writes them), and the live-owner branch returns before the in-flight branch, so the defect had no live row to misfire on — it activates the moment the marker's writer is restored, which is why the age test lands here rather than with that row.
- refactor: **all seven restatements of the in-flight check move together** — `agents/manager-drive.md`, `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` (input 11 and the cell's `working (session)` branch), `commands/manager-loop.md`, `commands/manager-status.md`, plus the vault-side `.claude/scripts/sweep-gate.py`, its fixture and the Manager Session runbook's two restatements. ⚠️ **Four of the seven are in this repository; the other three are vault-local and ship in place** — this bullet records the coordinated change, not a claim that all seven travel in this PR. `in_flight` remains a render-only input — not a `digest_of` input, the branch stays in `_classify` and never reaches `apply_stuck` — so `scripts/manager-predispatch.py` needs no matching edit. The fixture's in-flight block grew from 4 cases to **11**, including the two registry directions and four limb-1 controls.

## v0.105.5
- fix: **`/supervisor:ready` step 1 resolves the task name exactly, and against the right population.** It read `vault-cli --vault "<vault>" task search "<name>"` and required *"exactly one match"* — but `task search` is a **semantic** search, so run verbatim it returns scored near-misses and never the row named (measured 2026-10-05 in the `Attention Routing` manager session: five hits, `0.609` … `0.494`, none of them the task), and a caller following the step literally readies *nothing*. It now runs `scripts/inbox.py --locate "<name>"` — a new exact resolver matching the title for equality at **any phase** — and binds the vault it prints, which steps 2–4 interpolate. ⚠️ **`--locate` exists because the sibling `--resolve` is the wrong verb here, and the first cut of this change used it.** `--resolve` is scoped to the inbox (`phase: todo`), while Gate 1 *refuses* `phase: todo` and proceeds only on approved rows — so `--resolve` resolves the **complement** of ready's input and returns `rc 3` for every row ready exists to ready (`test_settled_row_does_not_resolve` pins that, deliberately). A resolver scoped to the inbox cannot serve a caller whose population is the inbox's complement. `vault-cli task get` is not a fallback either — it substring-matches, so a middle fragment resolves. ⚠️ **A `python3` grant is added to `allowed-tools` in the same change, and without it the fix is dead on arrival** — the command's own frontmatter denies the new call at runtime, so the resolver never runs and the step silently keeps its old behaviour. It is the path-scoped form (`Bash(python3 ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT:-…}/scripts/*)`, as `commands/fleet-verify.md` carries), not the blanket `Bash(python3:*)`: the command interpolates an operator-supplied `<name>` into the shell line, so the grant is bounded to the resolver rather than to the interpreter. `--vault` is passed through to the resolver when the caller supplies one, which is what suppresses the `rc 4` ambiguity refusal (`inbox.py` scans before it filters); the "never create a task from an unmatched name" clause is unchanged. The unused `Bash(grep:*)` grant is dropped — nothing in the command calls `grep` — which holds the file at its 100-line ceiling (`agent-cmd/command-thin`). `commands/open.md`'s use of `task search` is correct and deliberately untouched: it wants *closest candidates*, which is what a fuzzy search is for.

## v0.105.4
- fix: **a cluster spawn no longer overwrites the `claude_session_id` of the task it binds to.** `bindSessionToTask` issued `vault-cli task set <task> claude_session_id <id>`, which REPLACES the field — and that field is an ownership stamp, read as "who is working this task" by the manager sweep's orphan predicate, the vault UI's session column and the auto-resume gate, so the write retracted a fact rather than adding one. Measured 2026-10-05: a cluster spawn bound to a task whose owner was live displaced that owner's id with the probe session's, and a CLUSTER worker has no `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`, so `session-liveness.py --check` can only ever return ABSENT for it — the row was left naming a session that can never read LIVE. It survived only because `metrics_sessions` still carried the real owner twice and the liveness rule is "alive if ANY id in the whole set returns LIVE"; a freshly spawned row names its owner in `claude_session_id` alone and would have been silently orphaned by the very spawn measuring it. The binding now stamps `claude_session_id` **only when the field is empty** — the rule session-connect already follows, and what keeps the goal's "the task's `claude_session_id` is bound to it" true for the ordinary unowned case — and otherwise appends the session to `metrics_sessions`, leaving the owner intact. ⚠️ **Refusing a bind onto an owned task was the alternative and is worse from this position:** `startClusterSession` has already run by the time the bind happens, so a refusal there leaves a live worker that nothing points at. ⚠️ **An unreadable owner refuses rather than reading as unowned** — defaulting on a transient `vault-cli` error would reach the same displacement by the error path, and silently — and the guard reads the **body**, not just the exit code: a task that does not exist answers `{"success": false, "error": …}` with exit **0** (measured), so an exit-code-only check calls it unowned and issues the stamping write. The function moved to `server/task-binding.mjs` with eight tests beside it; the load-bearing one asserts on the argv issued (never `task set … claude_session_id` when an owner is present), so it holds without a vault.

## v0.105.3
- fix: **the ownership-scoped attention watcher now carries the feed's `⟳replay` mark, so it stops firing on gates that were already answered.** `gate-owner-filter.py --feed` printed a bare pane id and nothing else — which is the watcher's entire input — so a row `who-needs-me.py` had already rendered `5h49m  ⟳replay  ⚙ …` reached the watcher as a plain `864` and woke the manager on a gate answered hours ago. Measured 2026-10-03 on the Manager Layer topic (ticks 89-90): **10 firings in ~70 minutes, 2 real.** ⚠️ **The mark is a safety guard being discarded, not noise** — `who-needs-me.py` stamps it on every row it returns from its event-log fallback (the branch it takes when the attention store is unreachable) precisely so a manager does not act on a stale gate, and this filter was the one consumer throwing it away. ⚠️ **`panes_from_feed` now returns `(pane, replayed)` pairs, with a wrapper projecting it back to ids alone, so the existing contract and its tests are untouched.** The mark is read off the same line as the `[<pane>]` marker rather than re-derived: it is a property of the read that produced the row, and a `--pane` caller has no line to read it from. ⚠️ **A LIVE row renders byte-for-byte as before** — the mark is appended only when present, so a responsive store's output is unchanged; a mark that was always present would fail that requirement, the same rule `who-needs-me.py`'s own render states. ⚠️ **Nothing parses this output programmatically** — the arm feeds a `Monitor` a manager reads — so the shape change is contained to the prose that describes it: `commands/manager-loop.md` step 7 and `commands/fleet-loop.md` here, plus the Manager Session runbook § Cadence mechanics in the vault, which carries the line-shape rule.

## v0.105.2
- fix: **the pre-dispatch gate no longer lets session-liveness churn authorise a dispatch.** `scripts/manager-predispatch.py`'s `digest_of` hashed the raw Session-column liveness word, so a worker starting or ending a turn — `live` <-> `parked` — moved the digest and the gate answered `CHANGE` (exit 10) on ticks where no row's *eligibility* moved. ⚠️ **Which surfaces that reaches is stated exactly, because `/manager-loop` is not one of them:** the `CHANGE` verdict is consumed by the `--print` / `--check` replay paths — `/manager-status` step 1 and `/manager-drive` step 2 — while the loop calls only `--write-tracked` / `--compare-tracked` / `--save` / `--write-verdicts` and never branches on the digest, so its act leg is not gated here. Measured 2026-09-30 on one goal's loop: four act legs in 55 minutes, three consecutive with the identical decision set (`to open 0 · to resume 0 · reaped 0 · nudged 0`) — recorded as that goal's own measurement, not as a dispatch this digest was gating. ⚠️ **The suppression is not ABSOLUTE, and the second route is named rather than left to be inferred:** `stuck` is a separate digest input and `idle_stuck` clears it for a PARKED row, so a row already flagged stuck (idle past the threshold, `phase: execution`, an open box) that then opens a gate still moves the digest on a pure `live -> parked` flip. Intended — a stuck row that parks on a human genuinely stops being stuck, so replaying a stale table over it would be the worse failure. What the collapse removes is the churn that informed nothing, not every transition whose *cause* is a gate. ⚠️ **The term is COLLAPSED, never deleted, and that distinction is the whole fix.** `liveness_change_term` is identical for every ALIVE verdict (`live` / `parked`) and moves only when an owner goes `LIVE -> ABSENT`, so the death transition the module docstring grades — *"a worker dying changes session liveness … this is the criterion the whole gate is graded on"* — is still carried. Deleting the term satisfies the churn half and regresses that one, so `TestLiveness.test_a_worker_dying_still_moves_the_digest` exists as the guard against the cheap fix, paired with `test_liveness_churn_alone_does_not_move_the_digest`. ⚠️ **The term is deliberately UNGUARDED** — an earlier cut returned `0` for a row with no `session` key and silently dropped death detection for every row whose ids live only in `metrics_sessions` (`read_task` sets `session` from the frontmatter key alone while `sessions` carries the whole id set, and `liveness_of` probes `sessions` first). No guard is needed anyway: a never-started row reads `none` on both sides of a comparison, so it cannot churn. `test_death_is_detected_for_every_ownership_shape` pins the shapes. ⚠️ **Two surfaces follow the split, and both are updated here.** the liveness bullets in `commands/manager-loop.md` and `commands/manager-status.md` now name `task_identifier` as the phantom shape a bare-uuid match sweeps in — a uuid of exactly the same shape, carried by most task files, giving a task with no session a phantom owner that silently drops it out of `ready-to-start` — matching `agents/manager-sweep-reader.md` step 3, which already carried the warning. And `docs/fleet-surface.md` now records that the gate's digest and the loop's per-subject snapshot **diverge at that one term deliberately**: the snapshot keeps the raw `liveness` word for the render, and its writer `sweep-gate-classify.py` keeps its own `liveness -> parked` trigger, which is a different gate with a different job. The field SET is unchanged; the divergence is by design, not drift. ⚠️ **One consequence of the collapse is named rather than left to be rediscovered:** `parked` is what makes a row render `⌛ waiting-on-human`, so a worker newly opening a gate no longer moves the digest and the exit-0 replay path reproduces the row's last cell. ⚠️ **That stale cell is real and uncovered on the two surfaces that replay** — `/manager-status` step 1 and `/manager-drive` step 2 both stop on exit 0 before reading anything else — and `/manager-loop` is **not** a mitigation: it never calls `--print`/`--check`, so it never consults this digest, and its attention-feed read is step 4's first bullet. The independent surfacing that does hold is step 7's `manager-attention-watch.py` / feed-Monitor arms, which fire `NEW GATE` off the registry and the feed rather than off the digest — and **both replay bullets now carry the caveat** beside the two limitations they already list, so a reader is not left trusting a cell the replay cannot refresh. ⚠️ **One upgrade-time effect is named rather than left to be discovered:** the term's shape changed (the raw word → a bare `0`/`1`) and the stored record carries no version, so every subject holding a pre-collapse digest reports one spurious `CHANGE` on its first run after the upgrade. Bounded and fail-open — a `CHANGE` authorises a sweep, it never suppresses one — and self-healing on the loop's next `--save`. Accepted, because a raised gate changes no row's *eligibility* — the gate's question is "is this run worth a dispatch at all".

## v0.105.1
- fix: **`attention-ask.py` refuses an invented `--producer-kind` before it is sent.** The store validates the field against a closed enum (`attention-controller` `pkg/producer-kind.go`: `session` / `agent` / `cron` / `dark-factory`) and answers anything else with a `400` naming the field, so a guessed kind cost a round trip and read as a store fault rather than a bad argument — and `--help` could not list the accepted set either, because both arguments declared a `default` and no `choices`. `post` and `post-batch` now constrain it, so argparse rejects the value locally and prints the valid ones. ⚠️ **The round trip was the cheap half of the cost; the expensive half was that nothing said which values exist.** Two cases pin it: the refusal must name the offending value, because a bare exit `2` is also what a missing required argument produces and would pass whether or not the constraint is there; and the accepted tuple is pinned to the four this repo records, so a local edit that widens or narrows it fails in the suite rather than at the next e2e. ⚠️ **That is not the same as guarding the store's side** — the enum's source is a sibling repo this suite cannot read, so a change there still passes silently and the e2e remains the check for it.

## v0.105.0
- feat: **`/supervisor:manager-loop` and `/supervisor:fleet-loop` no longer cap their tick at `min(<interval>, 900)` while the session owns a live headless worker.** The cap, added in `## v0.57.1`, made two things mutually exclusive that need not be: owning a headless worker and running a long tick. ⚠️ **The fact survives; only the binding goes.** A headless permission park still auto-denies after 15 minutes (`skills/supervising-workers/SKILL.md:70`), so a tick longer than that window can still resume a worker with a denial it did not earn — that is now a **choice the manager accepts**, not a constraint forced on it. The substitute is the wrangler auto-policy (`/supervisor:manager-drain` → the `supervisor:manager-wrangler` agent), which answers parks without the manager's tick. ⚠️ **And it is a ONE-SHOT loop — it stops as soon as its queue empties**, so a long tick behind a live headless worker needs it **run repeatedly for the life of the tick**, or a standing form; measured 2026-10-05, it answered 10 parks and then stopped while the worker was still mid-flow, its own report warning that any park raised after that point would auto-deny. Both commands state the same rule, and `manager-loop.md` § Interval's heading and framing were reconciled to match (the attention watcher was never an interval cap — it stays on its own 30-min-bounded arm). The released `## v0.57.1` entry is left as historical record.

## v0.104.0
- feat: **a dedup key can be resolved to its latest-answered attention card.** `scripts/attention-card-lookup.py` filters `GET /api/1.0/attention/history` by `--dedup-key` and returns the card with the maximal `answered_at` (`latest`), the newest card by `created_at` whatever its state (`recent`), or every card under the key oldest-answer-first (`list`). ⚠️ **`latest` and `recent` answer different questions and neither substitutes for the other** — `latest` filters to **answered** cards and is the "which answer governs this row" read, while `recent` takes the newest card by `created_at` whatever its state and is the "which card carries this hold" read. The hold line needs `recent`, because a hold is usually on a card the operator has **not** answered yet: an answered-only read returns `NO_ANSWERED:` for exactly the case the consuming clause most needs to serve, and that clause forbids re-posting — leaving the reader no way to obtain the id at all. (Caught in review before merge; the first cut shipped `latest` alone.) It exists because the drive leg's hold line must quote the card the operator already answered — `agents/manager-drive.md:303` says *"quote the existing `item-id` instead"* — and a dedup key can carry SEVERAL cards: the store's suppression is open-scoped, so once an item is answered the same key writes a NEW row rather than returning the old one, and quoting an arbitrary member reports a superseded answer in the direction that suppresses a raise. ⚠️ **There was no lookup-by-dedup-key anywhere, and each candidate is wrong by construction:** `GET /api/1.0/attention` returns **open** items only, so an answered card is absent from it; `attention-ask.py` exposes only `post` / `post-batch` / `poll <item-id>`, and `poll` refuses anything that is not a 32-hex item id; and `waiting-approval-state.py` / `under-target-state.py` each carry a single `card_item_id` for **their own** card, one per topic, neither being the below-bar per-task card — which has no state file at all. ⚠️ **Two traps are pinned rather than documented away.** The history read is **paged**, and the default page is 1000 items: an unpaged read showed a key carrying 2 cards where it carries 9, and would read a key whose cards predate the window as `NO_CARDS` — the false negative that invites the very re-post this lookup exists to prevent — so the limit is raised deliberately (`--limit` / `$ATTENTION_HISTORY_LIMIT`, default 50000). And a **tie is refused, never broken**: two cards sharing the maximum `answered_at` make "latest-answered" undecidable, and quoting either member would report an answer the operator may not have given. A prefix shorter than 8 characters matches nothing, and a prefix resolving to more than one distinct full key is refused rather than unioned — measured 2026-10-05, `7fbee982` resolves to two full keys (`…-860f-4eb4-…`, 6 cards; `…-860f-4ebf-…`, 1 card) whose cards cannot be ordered against each other. Exit codes follow `waiting-approval-state.py`'s shape: `0` ok · `3` no cards / none answered / no created_at · `2` refused · `1` store unreadable (`FAILED:`) — the `1` matters because a store that is down must not read as a key with nothing under it, and the caller's next act is to quote a card. Eighteen tests pin the selection-by-`answered_at` (never by position), the `recent`-vs-`latest` split including the negative probe that `latest` does *not* return the open card, both tie refusals, an unanswered `closed` card never outranking a real answer, the two-key prefix refusal, and all four exit paths (0 / 1 / 2 / 3, the last via a mocked `urlopen`), plus a naive-stamp read. ⚠️ **The drive leg's card-id resolution has ONE home** — the below-bar hold-line clause in `agents/manager-drive.md`'s `<error_handling>` block, which clause (1) already designates as the owner of the hold line and the card's fields; the escalate-once clause in `<process>` states the principle and points there rather than carrying a second copy, per the file's own single-home doctrine. Every one of those no-id outcomes also has a **defined hold line**, so no manager improvises one and no two branches render alike. The surviving copy names both reads and all four exit tokens (`REFUSED:` / `NO_CARDS:` / `NO_ANSWERED:` / `NO_CREATED_AT:` / `FAILED:`), and states that **none of them means the card is gone** — the reading that would otherwise license the re-post the same clause forbids.

## v0.103.0
- feat: **an `asked-of-you` ledger entry now records WHERE its gate was raised — the raising session and the pane (`$WEZTERM_PANE`) — captured automatically at `add` time, and a new `classify` subcommand reads it back.** The ledger stored no per-entry origin, so an entry whose raising pane was gone rendered identically to one still outstanding, and the render rule ("never omitted") printed both forever — the surface could not distinguish "cannot close" from "has not closed", which is the whole reason the ledger exists. ⚠️ **A session-liveness check cannot make that distinction:** every entry in a ledger carries the ledger's own session, which stays LIVE long after one of its panes is gone, so the **pane** is the discriminator and it was nowhere in the JSON. `classify` reports `gone` / `live` / `unknown` per entry and renders **no marker** on the sweep's `📋 Open with the operator` lines; it adds **no close path** — recording the origin is the fix, not a compensating exit from a state the write site already refuses to enter. A legacy entry written before the field existed reports `unknown`, never `gone` ("could not tell" must not read as "dead"). `skills/open-items/SKILL.md` documents the requirement; twelve tests pin the write, the read, the two-verdict case and the unreadable-probe case. ⚠️ The new pane probe is a transport read, so it is registered in `scripts/transport-read-check.py` — `SITE_LIST`, the three-state driver and a populated payload — so the mechanism and the recorded enumeration agree; `make precommit`'s `test_transport_reads` is what enforces it.

## v0.102.4
- fix: **`pollCluster` no longer blocks the event loop, so a hanging cluster reader can no longer delay a headless worker's heartbeat stamp.** `server/cluster-heartbeat.mjs` read the cluster with `spawnSync(..., { timeout: 20_000 })` on the server's own `clusterTimer`, and the headless stamp timers share that loop — so a reader that hit its timeout stopped the world for the full 20 s budget, and a worker whose stamp timer started in phase with the cluster timer took the whole block into its refresh gap. ⚠️ **Measured before the fix, and the margin was ~10 s.** With one server-wide cluster timer plus stamp timers at 0/6/12/18/24 s offsets (the fleet's workers each spawn at their own phase), the worst inter-write gap was **50 007 ms** against `HEARTBEAT_TTL_MS = 60_000`, decaying 44 006 → 38 006 → 32 007 → 30 002 as the offset grew — the gap is the 20 s block plus whatever remained of the 30 s interval. The stamp file's own write times are the instrument: the stamp is refreshed exactly at each write, so the inter-write gap **is** the peak age `readLive` computes. ⚠️ **It is one stretched interval per worker, not a per-tick stretch** — after the first gap the interval settles at 30 000 ms, the phase then landing the stamp in the loop's free window — so this is a thin margin rather than a sustained stall, and the margin is what anything else blocking the loop spends. ⚠️ **PR #148 (merged `efdda22`) is what made it matter:** `worker-sessions.mjs` now takes the union of the registry and the heartbeat store, so a stale headless stamp stopped being a liveness display and started **under-counting the fleet cap** on the spawn path, which is the one count that must never read low. ⚠️ **The fix keeps the 20 s budget and the result shape** — `spawn` + a promise resolving to the same `{status, stdout}` object, with the same timed-out result (`status: null`, `signal: 'SIGTERM'`, `ETIMEDOUT`) — so the injectable `run` seam and every caller's contract are unchanged. `pollCluster` is now `async`; an injected `run` may be sync or async, because it is awaited either way. ⚠️ **The `clusterTimer` callback is async now, and two consequences are deliberate:** an in-flight guard so a slow injected `run` cannot stack polls (the 20 s budget against a 30 s interval already prevents it for the default), and a `catch`, because an async interval callback that throws has no caller to reject to and would surface as an unhandled rejection. ⚠️ **The new test is discriminating, not decorative.** A `run` that answers asynchronously is the seam's own shape for a hanging reader, and a synchronous `pollCluster` reads the returned promise as the process result, finds no `status` on it, and reports *"cluster store unreadable"* — so the test fails on the pre-fix behaviour rather than passing on a no-op. Verified by running it against the pre-fix module: `not ok 7`, the other six green. `scripts/heartbeat-stamp-latency.mjs` carries the harness that produced the before/after pair, so the numbers above are re-runnable rather than transcribed. ⚠️ **Three review findings were folded in before the merge, all of them in the function this change rewrote.** The async `run` had silently dropped `spawnSync`'s **1 MB `maxBuffer`** bound — an oversized reader is capped again and fails the poll with `ENOBUFS`, exactly as it did under `spawnSync`, rather than accumulating unbounded on the loop this change exists to keep free. The timeout path now **escalates `SIGTERM` → `SIGKILL`** after a 2 s grace: `finish`'s `settled` guard drops the later `close`, so a reader that traps the first signal would otherwise never be reaped and would hold its pipes for the rest of the server's life. And `defaultRun` is **exported with both knobs injectable**, so the only code that can actually spawn and hang is no longer the only code with no coverage — the timeout and the output cap are reachable in milliseconds instead of 20 s and 1 MB. A fourth nit is closed the same way: `make check` now parses `scripts/*.mjs` alongside `server/*.mjs`, so the harness carrying the measurements is syntax-checked — it previously reached no gate at all while importing from `server/`, which is how that import would have rotted silently. ⚠️ **A second review round added two more, both in that same function pair.** The `clusterTimer` catch is now **transition-gated** like the `ok: false` branch beside it and records `ok: false` so the gate works — a throw source that persists would otherwise log every 30 s and bury the signal the block comment says the design exists to keep readable. And the SIGKILL escalation is cancelled on the child's **`close`**, not in `finish` — the correct half of the fix rather than the obvious one: `finish` runs at the moment `kill()` schedules the escalation, so clearing it there would cancel the SIGKILL in the one case it exists for, a reader that traps SIGTERM, while `close` is the event that means the child is actually gone and a pending escalation has nothing left to reap.
